## THE VOLUME 07 BATCH 0002 — ten days, days 311 to 320, and the sixth form is on a page

**This block is the newest in this file and it wins.** It was written by the batch that wrote Chapters 0311 to 0320, it changed no day, no weekday, no anchor, no live thread's state and no decision of record, and it wrote no plan. The record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-07-batch-0002.md` and the rules the ten chapters put on a page are at `bible/power-system.md` §42. **The next phase is `workspace/volume-07/batch-0003/PROMPT.md` and it is a batch.**

### 1. The day map, and the run was recomputed from day 1

**Days 311 to 320, one chapter to one day, the chapter number is the day number, and there is no double day in this batch and there is no double day in this volume.** 311 Thursday, 312 Friday, 313 Saturday, 314 Sunday, 315 Monday, 316 Tuesday, 317 Wednesday, 318 Thursday, 319 Friday, 320 Saturday. **The Short Month days are the fifty-sixth to the sixtieth, the ordinal being the day less 255, and the Bare Month days are the first to the fifth, the ordinal being the day less 315.** Forty-two weekday tokens stand across the ten files, forty-one were correct on the first reading, and one was a fault in Chapter 0319, where a Wednesday was written for the day after a Friday and is now a Saturday. **The next phase recomputes its own days from day 1 and does not take them from this block.**

### 2. The calendar, and what is on a page about it

1. **The sixth form is on a page and it is `bible/power-system.md` §42 item 1.** *The Nth day of the Bare Month*, the Nth being the day less 315. Five lines. A notary's name under the first line. **No figure on it.** Chapter 0316.
2. **The name is on a page and it is §42 item 1 also.** *The Bare Month.* Said once, in the local language, in daylight, in a room over a market with a number in chalk on the door, by **Sera Vail, a notary of fifty, twenty-six years a notary, who named the Short Month on day 256 on the same reasoning.** Chapter 0316. **No other chapter of this volume names it and no chapter before it names it, guesses at it or hints at it, and Batch 0001 did not name it and was not allowed to.**
3. **The month has no stated length and no chapter says what it does at its end, how many days it has, or whether it is long or short.** She was asked how long it is, in front of about nine people, on the first day of it, and said **I am not going to say**, and asked for the declining to be entered as hers. The reason is hers: she has put a number at the head of a month once, the days did not stop where the number stopped, and nobody found that out from the paper, they found it out from the days. §42 item 2.
4. **The date on the door of that room on day 316 was a count of the days of the flood in chalk, written by the woman of about twenty-nine, and she said the reason out loud before anybody asked her**: a number a person put on a door is a date until somebody puts a better one on it, and if she is wrong then every day after is wrong on the same count, and nobody in four hundred miles can check it against anything. **The notary rubbed it out with a cloth and wrote the date in the new word, and that is the only date on any door in this manuscript that is a plain count of the days of the flood.** §42 item 5.
5. **No chapter of this batch uses an ordinal for a month and none uses a bare month form**, and the only two instruments are a count of days of the flood and a day of a named month with its own form.

### 3. The instruments the batch put on a page, and the three things with no figure under them

6. **A REGISTER WITH THREE COLUMNS AND NO COLUMN FOR THE DAY A SHEET WENT OUT.** What was asked, what came back, and the day, the day being the day the room met. Nine years of it. **A question that took four days and a question that took nine days are on the page in exactly the same shape, and nobody in four hundred miles can check it, including the woman who keeps it.** Found in Chapter 0314 and the finding is the man of about forty-three's, in a mouth: *you are not missing a column for the day the sheet went out, you are missing it because of the four days.* §42 item 6.
7. **A FORM WITH A NOTARY'S NAME ON IT, FIVE LINES, AND NOWHERE ON IT TO SAY WHAT A DAY WAS FOR**, and a space under the last line about the width of two fingers with nothing in it. **A space is where a number goes when a person has decided not to write one, and the man of about forty-three has found four of those in nine boxes and two of them are a hundred and forty years old, and he said he was not going to make a law of it and not going to improve on it.** Chapters 0314, 0317, 0318. §42 item 6.
8. **A FOLDED SHEET THIRTY YEARS OLD IN A TIN, IN A WOMAN'S OWN HAND, WITH NO NAME AT THE TOP OF IT AND NO DAY ON IT**, which is what two households call their own ground and where the water went. **She has refused to let it be entered, copied or taken out of the room, on the condition that if it ever is to be entered she is asked first and told no if the answer is no, and she is not going to be told afterwards that it has already been done because she was in the room.** Chapter 0320. §42 item 6.
9. **A THIRD NAME FOR A PIECE OF GROUND, ON A SHEET WITH NO DAY AND NO NAME ON IT.** *The nine acres*, from a woman of about thirty-three behind a bread board, out of nine places walked in three days. Seven of the other eight answers are the two names the two documents use. The ninth place had eight houses in it and nobody came to a door and the ninth line was left empty. Chapter 0311. §42 item 8.

### 4. The people, in the order a later writer needs them

10. **Tamsin Quill** — on a page for the first time since the seventeenth day of the Short Month, in Chapter 0311, working, on a road. **Second handle invented there and in no other chapter: a folded sheet of road with about nine places on it, folded the same way every time — long way in half, then in half again across, and the corner that came last goes under — and the reason she gave once is that a sheet folded some other way can be taken for a notice, and a notice has a day on it, and hers has not got a day on it and is not going to get one.** Adrian does not ask her for anything. **She is never in a room with the woman of about twenty-nine in this volume and nothing settles whether they are one woman.**
11. **Sera Vail** — a notary of fifty, twenty-six years a notary, holds page forty-one, is on a page already from Chapter 0256. In Chapters 0313 and 0316. **Second handle: a date she has to write.** She named the Short Month on day 256 on the same reasoning. **She is not the person who registered the span on the Long Month and a later writer may not make her the person who did.**
12. **The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register** — her second handle is her second slate, and it is the same in every chapter she is in. **She wrote a count of the days of the flood on a door on day 316 and said the reason out loud first, which is what she does, and this is the fourth thing she has refused out loud in this flood and named the cost of before saying it, and the cost is that every day after is wrong on the same count if she is.** She did not put a fourth column in a nine-year register and did not refuse one, and said both out loud. **She did not ask a girl of seventeen's name to be taken off a slate, and has never said so out loud to anybody.**
13. **The man of about fifty-seven** — a length of new rope he has not used, and the rope is not the man of about sixty's rope and the two ropes are not joined in any chapter of this volume. **He refused the naming of the month out loud, in daylight, with a date on the door, and not one of his four sentences was about the calendar. He is not angry, nobody argued with him, nobody told him he is wrong, and he is not defeated and does not leave.** He also said the other half of the volume's live conflict out loud: *a page they can ask to have produced is the only kind there has ever been that settled anything in his life.* He said one sentence about the days of the week and nothing else: **that is a Tuesday, is it.**
14. **The man of about forty-three** — nine boxes, four columns in his own index including a day, and **he has never read the index back in eleven years and said the reason out loud in a room on a Sunday**: if he read it back he would find nine mistakes and then have to decide what to do about nine. He read the notary's five lines twice, the second time more slowly. He refused to read the eight names at the foot of the copy in Chapter 0303 and the eight names are still not read and he is in none of these ten chapters about them.
15. **Hettis Roke**, forty-four, nineteen years in — a waxed slate in a case, which he turned face up and then face down in Chapter 0314. **He refused a room his roll on day 314, said its shape out loud, and asked that the refusal be entered; he came back on day 317 and said four sentences into a row with nothing in it and asked that it be entered; he has eight people of his own on no page in four hundred miles, has wanted a page for them for nine years, did not ask and gave no reason. His roll is in no room in these ten chapters and is not laid beside the Crown's roll and the two are not compared.**
16. **The man of about forty-one in a very good coat** — a hand inside a coat with nothing in it, put in and taken out and put in; nine days of a page in the coat whose front he has not shown anybody. **He came on day 318 to say that a form two days old is going to cost somebody money that is not his, and asked for nothing, and said he is not going to be able to say in about four months that nobody warned him.** A day is not a date.
17. **The man of about thirty-six, who holds gates on a turn** — a bar stood against a post, a barrow, a spade, a bootful emptied before he answers. At a gate where four streets come together, which is not the shut gate and not the keeper of the Redroot gate. **He has begun saying the day out loud to a boy of about eleven with a yoke and two pails, and the boy says it back.** Chapter 0319. He is not in a room in any of these ten chapters.
18. **The woman of about fifty at the shut gate** — the same five handles, unchanged. **In Chapter 0312 she is asked nothing, nobody is sent to her, and she reads a sheet she had said out loud she would never read, because the wind took it off the wall, and puts it back and leaves it up.** Her name is on no page and may not be given. **The one thing only she and the man with the barrow know is not said in these ten chapters and nobody asked him about it.**
19. **The girl of seventeen** — on no roll of anything, has copied every asking anybody has written in this flood and has signed none of them. **She read the sixtieth day of the Short Month out loud off a plain slate with her own name at the top of it and was made to say the second part again and slower, and she asked the only question she asked in that room, which is what she is to copy for tomorrow, and nobody answered her.** That is the whole of her use in this volume.
20. **The woman of about sixty-nine and her tin.** She came up from the end of the lane with nobody having sent for her, opened the tin in a room, and set a condition. The folded sheet thirty years old is still on that table.
21. **Adrian Vale** — named in 0311, 0313, 0314, 0317 and 0318, in no chapter in any form in the other five, and in no chapter in any form in Chapter 0316 on the morning the month was named. **Aged nowhere in Volume 07. Stage 2 on all fifty days. No working, no threshold, no workway, and *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten files.** He was asked no question about the boundary in any chapter he is in.

### 5. What is live and what this batch did not close

**The eleven live items at `state/volume-06-close.md` §3 stand at their full length and this batch closed none of them.** In particular: the boundary is not settled and the line was not measured, dug, counted, probed or had a rod put in it, and no character in these ten chapters goes and looks at it; the nineteen stones and the eight that are different are not resolved and the eight names at the foot of the copy are not read; the keeper of the Redroot gate is not used, was not counted, and is in none of these ten chapters; the girl of seventeen is unasked, unfetched and unresolved and has had exactly one use; the two women of twenty-nine are never in one room and nothing settles whether they are one woman; the consent fracture is not mended and nobody apologised in it; a word in this world for the people from the other side does not exist and no chapter invented one; the ninth line is not moved, dated, read aloud or resolved and the man who wrote it is in none of these ten chapters; the board is not written on and the wall above it is not washed; the bag is not picked up, nobody put a boot on the nine feet, **no chapter of this batch says what a wage in salt is worth and Break is not open**; the case is not opened, the schedule is not filled in and the Regent is not defeated; the two rolls of the same gates are not put side by side and the word *holder* is not defined; a woman of forty-eight in a fourth house at Nine Elms was not approached; and no chapter used an ordinal for a month, converted a hundred and forty years into days, put a number on the Marches, or used a distance this world does not have.

**THE WORD IS AT ZERO IN ALL TEN OF THESE FILES, and that is a decision and not an oversight: the volume's climax is a subtraction of it in Chapter 0342 and a batch that used it three times in ten days would have spent it. The clause the mark is not a pattern is not repeated, and none of the three sentences this manuscript has used about that word is in any mouth in any of these ten chapters and none was improved on.**

**AND THE COUNT OF THIS CITY'S SILENCES IS 212 AT DAY 311 AND 221 AT DAY 320, derived, on no page, in no mouth, and in none of the ten chapters, and the wall said nothing on all ten days and this batch spends no panel.**

## THE VOLUME 07 BATCH 0003 — ten days, four notices, four rooms, and a name off an index

**This block is the newest in this file and it wins.** It was written by the batch that wrote Chapters 0321 to 0330, it changed no day, no weekday, no anchor, no live thread's state, and no decision of record, and it wrote no plan. The record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-07-batch-0003.md` and the rules the ten chapters put on a page are at `bible/power-system.md` §43. **The next phase is `workspace/volume-07/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` and it is a batch.**

### 1. The day map, and the run was recomputed from day 1

**Days 321 to 330, one chapter to one day, the chapter number is the day number, and there is no double day in this batch and there is no double day in this volume.** 321 Sunday, 322 Monday, 323 Tuesday, 324 Wednesday, 325 Thursday, 326 Friday, 327 Saturday, 328 Sunday, 329 Monday, 330 Tuesday. **The Bare Month days are the sixth to the fifteenth, the ordinal being the day less 315, and no chapter of this batch uses an ordinal for a month and no chapter of this batch uses a bare month form.** Twenty-one weekday tokens stand across the ten files, all twenty-one were read in their sentences, all twenty-one are correct, and one fault was found and repaired in the writing pass — a room on a Wednesday in Chapter 0325 where the room is on a Tuesday, now a day and not a figure. **The next phase recomputes its own days from day 1 and does not take them from this block.**

### 2. The anchors, and what this batch printed and what it did not

**The full table is at `bible/power-system.md` §33A item 5 and is unchanged and was not amended by this batch. At day 321 and at day 330: the posting 120 and 129; the board's figures 97 and 106; the board unwritten 96 and 105; the wind at that wall 92 and 101; the trestle count 281 and 290; the thirty-nine men under the near gate unpaid 236 and 245; the share with the post taken out 153 and 162; the four who have not left 176 and 185; the bag on the boards 81 and 90; the case on the table 70 and 79; the road shut 27 and 36; the line in the ground first on a page 22 and 31; the chalk on the table 23 and 32; and the barrow of boards 25 or 26 at day 321.** **NOT ONE OF THOSE FIGURES IS PRINTED IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS AND NOT ONE IS IN A MOUTH, and no lead paragraph of any of the ten carries an interval figure, which is the third batch in a row to write ten days with no interval chain in it.**

**THE KEEPER OF THE REDROOT GATE IS 142 AND 151 AT THE TWO ENDS OF THIS BATCH AND BOTH ARE DERIVED AND ON NO PAGE AND IN NO MOUTH AND WERE NOT COUNTED, NAMED, PRINTED OR USED IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS, and she is in none of them.** The count of this city's silences is 222 at day 321 and 231 at day 330, derived, on no page, in no mouth, **and in none of the ten chapters**, and the wall said nothing on all ten days and this batch spends no panel.

### 3. The four days of notice, four of the volume's six, and all four are four

**A notice on any day other than the fourth day before the room is a three-day instrument, and a three-day instrument does not fail, it arrives warm.** Four went out in this batch, on days 326, 327, 328 and 329, for rooms on days 330, 331, 332 and 333, **each naming its own morning inside its own chapter, all four unsigned, all four with nothing at the top of them, and all four going to a place and not to a house.** **The prompt this batch was handed said four and corrected the three that the prompt the previous batch was handed said, and the correction is right and the fourth notice, for the woman of about sixty-one and the room on the eighteenth, is on a page in Chapter 0329 and would not have existed at all in a batch that believed it had three.** The remaining notice is on day 335 for a room on day 339, and it is Batch 0004's, and it is for a man of about fifty-two who digs.

**AND THE INSTRUMENT IS NOT THE SAME ONE AS THE FIRST TWO OF THE VOLUME, AND A LATER WRITER MAY NOT TREAT THEM AS THE SAME THING.** The first two were a question written by a person with a pen and carried by a man who was not one of the people it was about. **These four are a question in the words of the person it is about, written by that person, in a hand that is not the hand of anybody in the room it was read in, and the person whose words they are is in the room on the day the room is and not on the day the sheet goes out.** The reason is given out loud in Chapter 0326 and it is the carrier's and nine years old: a sheet with a person's own hand on it cannot be argued with at all, and that is not a good thing, and it is the only reason the instrument works. **AND THE SECOND OF THE FOUR IS COPIED NINE TIMES, WHICH THE REVIEW FOUND VOIDS THAT PROPERTY, AND THE REPAIR IS NOT TO NARROW THE CLAIM AND IS IN CHAPTER 0327, WHICH IS THE CHAPTER THAT MULTIPLIES IT.** Nine copies are one hand nine times over and not nine hands, **and the reason a copy is still unarguable is standing and not a hand: there is nobody in four hundred miles with standing to argue with a notice, because the only person who could say what one means is the person whose hand it is and that person is not going to be asked and has not been asked, and a person who wants to be arguing with a sheet has to put a name at the top of it, and a name is the only thing on that sheet which would change what it is. And a notice is still a notice after it has been copied, and the nine make the agreement testable, because nine houses holding the same hand is nine houses that can each see down the row that the one next door did not write a name on theirs.** The first of the four went to one place and the second went to nine places and neither of them is a three-day instrument.

### 4. The cast, and the four people who are on a page now

**Volume 07 adds nine people who were on no page and this batch puts four of them on one, and the movement is the oldest instrument in this book: a question going out in a person's own words, in writing, four days before a room, unsigned, with no name at the top of it, going to a place and not to a house.** A **woman of about twenty-one** is in Chapter 0330 with **a pen with the nib ground narrow, cut down with a knife**; a **man of about forty-eight** is in Chapter 0328 with **a folding knife with a hinge that has gone stiff**; a **woman of about sixty-one** is in Chapter 0329 with **a stick of hazel she carries and does not lean on**. **A man of about twenty-five is a RETURN and not an introduction, because he is on a page in Chapter 0309, and his handle is his left boot mended at the front with a length of cord that is not new, invented in Chapter 0327, and a state file that publishes a zero for him is wrong twice.**

**A man of about fifty-two who digs is on a page, and his name is Nev Cutler and it is on a page in Chapter 0321 in daylight with a date on the door, said once, out loud, by a reader off an index in his own hand, and nobody put it on anything, and his second handle is a rod he does not carry, invented in Chapter 0321 and in no other chapter as an introduction.** The descriptors this batch took that were measured at zero across all fifty files of Volume 06: a woman of about twenty-one, a man of about forty-eight, a woman of about sixty-one. **All four crowded descriptors of Volume 06 are at zero on these ten files, and the man of about thirty-six who holds gates on a turn is in none of them and is carried forward to Chapters 0338 and 0345 on the two instructions against one.** The co-presence prohibition holds at zero and the unbounded search for *man of about fifty* returns eighteen paragraphs every one of which is the string inside *woman of about fifty*.

### 5. Adrian, and the count is two and they are 0321 and 0325 and not 0324, and the not-asking is three and is a different count

**He is named in Chapter 0321 and Chapter 0325 and in the other eight he is in no chapter in any form, and a state file that says 0324 is wrong on the page.** In 0321 he is in a room reading a clerk's hand and is not asked one question about the boundary. In 0325 he is on eleven miles of flats about nine feet off a man with a spade saying nothing, and his name is on the page twice there, and a man out of doors in the wind is not the case the naming rule was written for. **In 0324 he is in the room for the whole morning and the name is not on that page: Tamsin Quill says his name once and the chapter does not print it, so he is *he* and the man who brought the plan from end to end, and nobody asks him a question about the boundary or about anything else. SAYING A NAME AND PRINTING ONE ARE TWO DIFFERENT ACTS AND MERGING THEM COUNTS THREE CHAPTERS INSTEAD OF TWO.**

**The eight are not one reason: five chapters he is not in the room and nobody came to fetch him, one chapter he is in the room and is not named in it, and two chapters he is in no chapter in any form and the chapter does not say so, and 0325 is not among the eight because he is named in it. And the chapters in which the not-asking is shown rather than asserted are 0321, 0324 and 0325, which is three, and merging that with the two is the error a later writer will make if they read this block quickly.** He is aged nowhere in Volume 07, is Stage 2 on all fifty days, performed no working, opened no threshold, was offered no workway and asked for none, and *passage* and *privilege* are at zero.

### 6. What is on a page now that was not

**A man's name read off an index in four seconds. A rod against a wall. A day on which nobody was sent for, and the reason, in a mouth. A form filled in for the first time in four hundred miles, in the keeper's own three columns, and what came back was nothing. A number under the first line that this city cannot support and that one man four hundred miles off will have to write in himself, in his own handwriting, and cannot explain. Four ways of writing a day in one nine-year book and no column saying which one any row is in. Nine rows at the back of that book, nine years old, in an instrument this city stopped writing in years ago, and the only rows in it that have a day in them a person could stand behind, because nobody can date them. Five instruments in one room and none of them has ever had to be read by anybody who did not already know which one they were holding. Six sentences, and a decision that nobody goes, and a season and a certainty named out loud by the man who paid for both. Four sheets in four hands with nothing at the top of any of them, to four places, for four rooms, four days each, **and the second of the four to nine places out of one hand copied nine times over, and the reason a copy is still unarguable, which is that eight other people are holding the same hand with nothing at the top of it.** A window in a room over a market that had not shut for nine years. A ladder and a bit of shuttering and about nine days of work. A stick of hazel. Four words nobody asked for, and no word in this city for what the four of them are, and the whole of what they were told. A pen with the nib ground narrow. One date in chalk on one door on the low ground with nothing above it and nothing rubbed out, and a door with one date on it being a notice and not a record.**

### 7. THE VOLUME'S WORD, AND WHAT THE NEXT TWENTY CHAPTERS MAY NOT DO WITH IT

**THE WORD IS AT ZERO IN ALL TEN OF THESE FILES, and that is a decision and not an oversight.** It is on a page once, in chalk, on the register-keeper's second slate in Chapter 0310, and **it comes off that slate in chalk, with a cloth, in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, in Chapter 0342, and after that it is on no page in the world and never will be.** Chapter 0321 refers to the slate twice and both times it says there is a word on it and does not say the word. Chapter 0323 has the slate face down on a table all morning and turns it over to write the day on the face of it. **Nobody may measure, dig, count, probe, resolve or connect the line, and nobody may put a rod in it or in anything, and no character in these ten chapters goes and looks at it. None of the three sentences this manuscript has used about that word goes into a mouth, including the panel at `chapter-0041.md:45`, and the clause at `chapter-0037.md:231` is in the same prohibition, and the one clause that may be put on a page once is not repeated in any of these ten chapters.**

### 8. THE HINGE IS PAID, THE NAMING IS ON A PAGE, AND THIS BATCH REPEATED NEITHER

**The Bare Month is named in Chapter 0316 and is named in no chapter of this batch, and no chapter of this batch guesses at it, hints at it or says what it does at its end, and the count of days it has, whether it is long or short, and the words *length*, *long* and *short* applied to it, are at zero on all ten files.** The volume's one panel is fixed to Chapter 0340 and no chapter of this batch spends it, and no chapter of this batch contains a panel or a rule in a block or anything that answers a question.

**THE DAY-154 SENTENCE IS NOT SAID, RESTATED, PARAPHRASED, IMPROVED ON OR PUT INTO A SECOND MOUTH IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS, and this batch spent three days writing about a name on a page and a number under a first line and did not once go near it.** The cheapest near-miss available to a batch on this subject is the claim that a sheet with nothing at the top of it cannot be argued with, and that is a claim about a notice and not about a person, and it is in Chapter 0326 in the carrier's mouth.

### 9. What is still live and what this batch did not close

**The eleven live items at `state/volume-06-close.md` §3 stand at their full length and this batch closed none of them.** In particular: the boundary is not settled and the line was not measured, dug, counted, probed or resolved and nobody went to look at it; the nineteen stones and the eight that are different are not resolved and the eight names at the foot of the copy were not read; **a register with three columns and no column for the day a sheet went out is not resolved and this batch says so out loud in Chapter 0323 and does not get to it**; the keeper of the Redroot gate is not used and was not counted; the woman of about fifty at the shut gate was not asked anything and nobody was sent to her and the one thing only she and the man with the barrow know is not said in any of these ten chapters; the girl of seventeen is unasked, unfetched and unresolved; the two women of twenty-nine are never in one room and nothing settles whether they are one woman; the consent fracture is not mended and nobody apologised in it; a word in this world for the people from the other side does not exist and no chapter invented one; the ninth line is not moved, dated, read aloud or resolved and the man who wrote it is in none of these ten chapters; the board is not written on and the wall above it is not washed; the bag is not picked up, nobody put a boot on the nine feet, **no chapter of this batch says what a wage in salt is worth and Break is not open**; the case is not opened and the schedule is not filled in and is not the subject of a chapter and the Regent is not defeated; **the two rolls of the same gates are not put side by side and the word *holder* is not defined and the request to strike it out of any list was not printed**; a woman of forty-eight in a fourth house at Nine Elms was not approached; and no chapter used an ordinal for a month, converted a hundred and forty years into days, put a number on the Marches, or used a distance this world does not have.

**AND THE FOUR THINGS THIS BATCH ADDED TO THE LIVE LIST, all of them open, all of them cheaper than they look.** The man of about fifty-two who digs is on a page with a name and a rod and the room did not send him. The man of about fifty-seven has now lost a season out loud and had it entered and been told he is wrong by nobody. The form has been filled in once and the space under the first line is still the width of two fingers. **And the four people who have not left are four people on a page with no word for what they are, three of them have said yes in their own words, one of them has said no in a sentence that is hers, and nobody in this volume has ever asked any of them whether it will go.**

The exchange: forty-eight plain speeches and twenty-four answered, fifty per cent, and the denominator is the caveat. The chorus frame: 144 in 288 paragraphs, one in two, against one in about three in Batch 0002 and one in about four in Volume 06, and THIS IS THE ONE MEASUREMENT IN THIS BATCH THAT IS WORSE THAN THE TWO BEFORE IT and it is published because a count is not a target and because the cause is named in `bible/power-system.md` §43 item 23: two of the ten chapters are rooms in which nobody is being asked anything and the narrator's one counting habit has nothing else to attach itself to. A repair pass has the instrument and the method.** A stray closing quotation mark is at zero on these ten files and the nineteen on a page in Chapters 0281 to 0290 are still unrepaired and belong to a Volume 07 review-repair pass.

**AND THE REPAIRS THE WRITING PASS MADE WERE THIRTY-TWO OF ONE FAMILY, AND IT IS THE FAMILY THIS REPOSITORY KEEPS PAYING FOR, AND IT IS PUBLISHED BECAUSE A FIGURE A LATER WRITER QUOTES IS NOT THE SAME THING AS A FAULT A LATER WRITER MAKES.** A script was used to thin the narrator's chorus frame in a chapter whose subject is a person being left alone; it was run against a copy of the ten files that was not the copy the writing pass believed it was, and a second pass ran on top of the first. **At thirty-two sites a clause that had been introduced by the frame was left abutting the clause in front of it with the comma, the conjunction and the frame gone. All thirty-two have been repaired by putting the frame back exactly where it stood, word for word, and the ten files now carry zero fused words, measured on a vocabulary built from the twenty untouched files of Chapters 0301 to 0320 and checked with a list of thirty-one fusions.** No notice is missing, no room is missing, no beat is missing and no speech was cut. **The general finding: a writing pass that edits prose with a script must make its own copy first and must verify the copy it is editing against the file it means to be editing, and a check that counts words cannot see a fused word.**

Nobody is asked whether it will go.**

## THE VOLUME 07 BATCH 0004 — ten days, the fifth notice, a digger's condition, and the one panel

**This block is the newest in this file and it supersedes the block above it for every figure in it. Nothing above it was restructured.** The record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-07-batch-0004.md` and the ten days are on a page at `bible/power-system.md` §44. **It closed no thread, resolved nothing, opened no new antagonist, and made no calendar decision: §32, §33 and §33A are unchanged and §33A item 7, which is the register of the volume's six notices, is the authority for the fifth of them.**

**1. THE DAY MAP, RECOMPUTED FROM DAY 1 BEING A TUESDAY AND FROM NOTHING ELSE BEFORE ANY PROSE WAS WRITTEN.** 331 a Wednesday, the sixteenth; 332 a Thursday, the seventeenth; 333 a Friday, the eighteenth; 334 a Saturday, the nineteenth; 335 a Sunday, the twentieth; 336 a Monday, the twenty-first; 337 a Tuesday, the twenty-second; 338 a Wednesday, the twenty-third; 339 a Thursday, the twenty-fourth; 340 a Friday, the twenty-fifth. The prompt's table reproduces and was thrown away. **Twenty-eight weekday tokens stand across the ten files and three faults were found and repaired in the writing pass, all of one family: a Monday in 0331 where a Saturday is (Chapter 0320, day 320), a Saturday in 0337 where a Sunday is (Chapter 0335, day 335), and a Tuesday in 0336 attached to a slate that is attached to no day at all.**

**2. THE FIFTH NOTICE, AND IT IS FOUR DAYS.** Out on the twentieth of the Bare Month, in the morning, for a room on the twenty-fourth, unsigned, with nothing at the top of it, written by the person it is about in his own words on the rough side of a board off the wall of his own hut. **The reason it is on a board is that there is no form in four hundred miles to write it on and none was sent, and the reason none was sent is on the page in the carrier's mouth: a page with a person's name at the top of it is a page and this is not one.** It went out at about the fifth hour of the afternoon and the carrier walked eleven miles in the last of the light and did not open it. **No chapter may reduce this notice to three days, may not put a name at the top of it, and no sixth notice is written in this volume.**

**3. THREE ROOMS AND A DOORWAY.** The register-keeper is in none of the ten files, and the only mention of anything of hers in the ten is Chapter 0336, where a register that was not in the room is listed among the things under about four pairs of hands; the correct statement is that she is in none of them and that one of them names the object and not the woman. Tamsin Quill is in 0331 and 0336 and nowhere else; the two women of twenty-nine are never in one room and nothing settles whether they are one woman; the consent fracture is visible in 0336, is not mended, and nobody apologised in it, and the fifth condition is not given. **The fourth of the four said no on the fourteenth of the Bare Month in four words before anybody asked, and in Chapter 0333 she came to the door of the room on the eighteenth and did not come in and said nothing, and the room happened without her, and neither of the two ways the room could have gone is a failure and the no is not said again, not softened, not argued with, not apologised for, not overturned and not made a defeat.**

**4. WHAT IS ON A PAGE IN THESE TEN DAYS AND IS NOT SETTLED.** A man who was one of the nine places a notice went to and had his own sheet in his coat for four days. A yes said without a question. Nine words nobody has said, in two rooms, by a man who looked at a doorway and kept them. Eight of nineteen stones that are not the same stone and nobody able to say when, and the order not settled. A skylight mended and a window that had not shut for nine years and no one who will ever be able to say. A man who refused the only page in four hundred miles that would have got his winter back, in a room where he is right, and he does not leave and the money is in his hand. Four doors in a row and not one person who can say where their own ground stops. A digger who says yes and will not put his hands on it and will look at it from the end of it. A second bar at a gate nobody sent for. A date said out loud to a boy with two pails. A rule about words on a wall. A name taken off a board in about the ninth year of this flood in a man's own hand with his own cloth.

**5. WHAT WAS NOT TOUCHED.** The word on the second slate is at zero in all ten files and comes off in Chapter 0342. The day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on in any of the ten, and Chapter 0340 comes nearest to it from the side of the panel's rule and in a mouth that is not the protagonist's. The ninth line was not moved, dated, read aloud, taken down, thanked for or resolved. **The line in the ground was not measured, dug, counted, probed or resolved, no character in any of the ten goes and looks at it, no rod was put in it or in anything, no third hole was made, and the two holes nine feet apart were not cleared, measured or referred to.** The woman of about fifty at the shut gate is in none of the ten and nobody was sent to her. The keeper of the Redroot gate is in none of them and her days are not counted. The girl of seventeen is not fetched. The four are on a page in three of the ten, nobody was asked whether it would go, and *delegation* is at zero. The store's board is not written on and the wall above it is not washed. The barrow of boards at the shut gate and the barrow on the flats were not joined and the man with the barrow is in none of these ten chapters. The case is shut, the schedule is not filled in and is not the subject of a chapter, the Regent is nine hundred miles away, the two rolls of the same gates are not put side by side, *holder* is at zero on all ten files and is not defined, and a keeper is not a holder. The island is not owned and Break is not open and no chapter of these ten says what a wage in salt is worth. The box is shut, the six rooms are six, the chapel stone is silent, the eight names are not read, and the page on the back of a stone stays wrong on purpose.

**6. THE MEASUREMENTS, WITH THE DEFINITIONS.** The share of words inside bold marks is **8.9 to 19.8 per cent, a mean of 13.9, in fifty-eight spans**, and the longest bolded turn is **ninety-four** in Chapter 0332 and no turn is over the ninety-five hard ceiling. **Sentences of forty words or more: 228, all 228 unique, none duplicated inside a file and none across the ten.** These are the post-repair run; the batch as written measured 8.8 to 19.9 with a mean of 13.5 and 226 sentences, and the bold measure and the long-sentence count both moved because a review-repair pass took four half-bold turns to whole and two bolded narrative paragraphs to plain and split one ninety-eight-word turn. **Weekday tokens: twenty-nine, all correct when each is read in its own sentence, and Chapter 0335 carries none; the batch record published twenty-eight and twenty-nine is the figure.** **Plain speeches answered by a bolded speech in the next paragraph: sixteen of twenty-eight, and the denominator is twenty-eight and that is the whole of the caveat.** **The chorus frame on the bare head *about four people in* is seventy-eight in two hundred and eighty-nine paragraphs, one in three point seven, against one in two in Batch 0003, and the writing pass stripped sixty frames out of named paragraphs and kept every observation.** **The word-run across all forty files of Volume 07 is a top of fifty-five between Chapters 0321 and 0335 and it is the rod in the hut and it is a motif and not a duplication, because §17.8 requires a second handle to be the same in every chapter its person is in; the next is forty-six between 0319 and 0338 and is the gate-keeper frame, and it was cut from fifty-six by rephrasing a beat so that a motif that diverges is cheaper than a motif that does not, and the third is forty-one between 0333 and 0339 and is the chorus frame carrying a room's self-arrived rule, which is the same class of thing and was not in the batch record's list.** **The count of this city's silences is 232 at day 331 and 240 at day 340, derived, on no page, in no mouth, and printed in none of the ten chapters**, and the trestle count is exactly three hundred at day 340 and the bag on the boards is exactly a hundred days old at day 340 and both figures are at zero in these ten chapters.

**7. TWO OPEN FIGURES RECORDED AS QUESTIONS AND NOT SETTLED.** The age of the man who trades on the Open Hand board, which the outline gives as about thirty-seven and the prose of Volumes 02 to 06 gives as about thirty-nine and Chapter 0309 gives as about thirty-seven; **he is given the trade and the board and no age in Chapters 0331 to 0340, and a later chapter may not settle it either.** And the fact that the fourth of the four said no on the fourteenth of the Bare Month in a room with a date on the door, and that the room on the eighteenth has now been written and is Chapter 0333 and is not a defeat and is not to be softened.

THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-07/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`, AND IT IS A BATCH AND NOT A VOLUME CLOSE.** It writes Chapters 0341 to 0350, days 341 to 350, one chapter to one day, ending on a Monday, and it is the last ten days of Volume 07 and it contains the climax, the resolution and the final image. ## THE VOLUME 07 BATCH 0005 — the climax, the cost, the resolution, and the last image

**The authoritative block for the current phase is the one headed *THE VOLUME 07 BATCH 0005*, above, and the next phase is `workspace/volume-07/close/PROMPT.md`, which is a VOLUME CLOSE and not a batch. Everything above this block in this file is history and is superseded by it. A section above this one that disagrees with it on a day, a figure or a name is wrong and this one wins.**

1. **THE WORD WENT OFF THE SECOND SLATE AND THE WORD IS NOW OFF EVERY PAGE IN THE WORLD.** Chapter 0342, day 342, a Sunday, the twenty-seventh of the Bare Month, in the room over a market, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, and about nine people in it. **The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall did it, with a cloth, and the piece of chalk he had carried in the inside breast pocket since the fifty-fifth day of the Short Month was not used, and he did not use it because a slate is the one surface in four hundred miles a word can be taken off, and the ninth line on a wall nine miles up the road was written in a harder chalk on purpose so that it could not be rubbed out.** He has no standing: he is barred from every roster in this city at his own request. **The register-keeper has the slate and said out loud that she can do this in a room in daylight with a date on the door and that is the whole of what she can do, and that the third thing — a person — cannot be done at all and everybody in the room has been working for a fortnight on the assumption that it can.** His reason is four sentences and it is his: a name is only a removal when the thing named can be found; a line in an orchard cannot be found because nobody is going to it; so a line can be named safely; the moment somebody starts going to it the name becomes a description, and the name has to go. **THE WORD IS IN NARRATION TWICE IN CHAPTER 0342 AND IN NO MOUTH IN ANY OF THE FIFTY FILES, and it is at zero in the forty-seven files that are not Chapters 0310 and 0342, and the day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on in any of the ten.** The protagonist is in that room and is not asked one question about it, three of the four people who came to this city behind a question are standing in it, the fourth is not coming, the man of about twenty-five's nine words are in his coat and were not said, and nobody was asked whether anything would go.

2. **AND EVERYTHING AFTER THE CLIMAX IS WORSE, AND THE CHAPTER SAYS IT IS WORSE.** Chapter 0343, day 343, a Monday, the twenty-eighth. **The naming bought that field one afternoon and it cost that field thirty-two days, and the man of about fifty-seven said the arithmetic out loud in a yard and nobody told him it was a sad one, and a man who carries things for a living said the one sentence that made the afternoon worse and said it to the room and not to him: a man told this room yesterday that there is a man with a barrow who has been up that lane; a man who digs is going to stand at the end of a cut in that field on a day not one of us knows; the word came off a slate in this room on Sunday because of the first of those; there is no form in four hundred miles that holds a thing and what it costs.** The digger gave no day in Chapter 0339 and gave none here, and the room did not know and was not allowed to ask, and found out on the morning of it.

3. **THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO WHO DIGS WENT, AND LEARNED NOTHING, AND SAID ONE SENTENCE.** Chapter 0344, day 344, a Tuesday, the twenty-ninth, eleven miles the other way. **He put a barrow against the end of a cut at a stone about nine hundred paces off a shut gate and looked along a mark in the ground the whole way from the end of it and did not touch it, and he did not put a foot in it and did not stand over it and did not go along it, and he came back four paces at a time.** His sentence is that he cannot tell you one thing about it that he did not know at the seventh hour, and that he would like it said out loud before anybody asks him what he saw, and that that is all he has and he has walked eleven miles. **The sentence is not an answer, it is on no page, it has not been repeated and it is not going to be.** The man of about sixty with a handcart and a length of rope was on that ground the same morning and nobody sent him and he built nothing; a woman came out of the second house with a bucket and was not asked anything; the man of about thirty-one came up the road with nothing in his hands. **Nothing was measured, nothing was dug, nothing was counted, no rod went into anything, and no character in this chapter went and looked at the line except him.**

4. **A SECOND BAR ON NO ROLL, A ROLL THAT IS A GATE AND A KEEPER AND A SET OF DAYS, AND A SIXTH REFUSAL NOBODY ASKED FOR.** Chapter 0345, day 345, a Wednesday, the thirtieth, at a gate where four streets come together. **A keeper empties a bootful before he answers a hard question and not after.** The second bar is in a bracket of his own making, put in on a Wednesday night because a gate that does not shut is not a gate, and nobody sent for it; **a gate with two bars is one gate and goes on the roll as one gate, and the second one is on no roll and the first one is not either, and no clerk four hundred miles off is going to come up that road and count them, and that is the arrangement and it is the cheapest thing in four hundred miles.** Nobody said what it was worth; the man of about thirty-six said he knows and is not going to say it; the man of about thirty-four said the same. **The ninth line is not read aloud, not moved, not dated, not taken down, not thanked for and not resolved, and its author asked in advance not to be thanked and was not thanked, and whether it works stays unanswered. A keeper is not a holder, the word is at zero, and the two rolls of the same gates are not put side by side.**

5. **THE RESOLUTION IS ON A PAGE AND IT IS A REFUSAL.** Chapter 0346, day 346, a Thursday, the thirty-first. **The thirty-first day of that month at the top of an entry, and underneath it that a straight mark in a field may not be dug, and no name and no word for the mark, and the third column of a form that has had three columns for nine years left empty on purpose, and a woman who keeps the register saying out loud that she will leave the room rather than fill it.** **A refusal is not a return, and that is why the entry is the only instrument in four hundred miles that an office nine hundred miles away cannot ask to have produced.** The man of about fifty-seven is told, in the room, what he has, and what he has is a line he cannot have shown anybody and a winter he cannot get back, and he says the cost out loud and **nobody in the room tells him he was wrong, because nobody in the room is wrong, and he is not a villain in this and is not converted and does not leave and is not happy.** The woman of about sixty-nine came a mile with an empty tin and **her sheet of paper thirty years old is not entered, is not copied and does not go out of the room, and the condition she put on it on the fifth of that month is hers and nobody may un-condition it.** Adrian is in the room and reads the entry and the empty column and is not asked one question about it, and is the only one of the ten chapters in which he is named. **And the woman of about sixty-nine asks the question this volume hands on, once, in a mouth, in daylight, with a date on the door, and nobody answers it: what is a person for, on a page, when the only thing anybody needs them for is a journey.**

6. **NINE BOARDS OFF A WALL AND A BARE WALL, AND NOBODY MADE A DECISION.** Chapter 0347, day 347, a Friday, the thirty-second. **The man of about thirty-eight came up that lane for the first time since the thirty-ninth day of the Short Month; the arrangement for taking them up was for the forty-ninth, which was a Thursday, and it held for about four weeks after the Thursday and then stopped holding for anybody and neither of the two ever found out.** He did not ask the woman of about fifty anything and she told him nothing and neither of them offered anything and neither of them was asked. He took them off one at a time in the wrong order and lost a quarter of an hour and put the two soft ones on the bottom of the barrow. **A barrow of boards against the wall of that gate is a different barrow of boards on a different wall nine miles the other way and no chapter may join the two.**

7. **A FENCE FOUR FEET OFF A LINE NOBODY HAS MEASURED, AND IT IS THE LAST POLITICAL ACT OF THE FLOOD.** Chapter 0348, day 348, a Saturday, the thirty-third, and Chapter 0350, day 350, a Monday, the thirty-fifth, which is the last image. **A man of about sixty was told no about a fence across the top of that road in the first week of that month, said he would come back, has come back four times, cut sixteen posts in a copse about four miles the other way and stacked them in a cart, set out from a row of trees because a row of trees is a thing you can see from the road, and did not measure anything: four feet is a number he has in his mouth and he said so out loud, and a fence is not an account of anything and does not know what it is four feet off.** Nobody sent him and nobody stops him. **The count is a count of willow and not a measurement, and it closes: sixteen cut, nine in the ground on the Saturday with seven left in the cart, thirteen in the ground on the Monday with three left for the Thursday, and the last one of the Monday went where the twelfth one was not because a man who walks a rope out along a road gets to the end with about a foot and nothing to do with it.** **About four people on that road worked out that a fence is the most ordinary thing in the world and the most permanent, and that it is going to be there when neither of the two men who never agreed where the line was is, and that the fence does not care.** The man of about fifty-seven, who could stop it, said he could stop it and was told that that is not the same as being able to say where it is, and then put his hand flat on the second post the way a man does to a gate post and did not press on it. **The nineteen stones are not this fence and may not be joined to it, and the woman of about thirty-three carried the order of them in on the seventeenth of that month and has been out and looked at them more than once, and a man who goes up ladders says he can tell that eight of the nineteen are a different stone off a floor without seeing them and is not going to guess at it in a room, and nobody can say when, and that is a different thing four hundred miles inland.**

8. **A RECOVERY CHAPTER IN WHICH A ROOM HOLDS A SUNDAY WITH NOTHING ON THE TABLE, AND THE CONSENT FRACTURE IS VISIBLE AND IS NOT MENDED.** Chapter 0349, day 349, a Sunday, the thirty-fourth, and the only one of the ten in which Tamsin Quill is on a page. **She has her folded sheet of road with about nine places on it, folded the same way every time, and a ninth line with nothing on it, and she has had it in the inside of her coat since the fifteenth of the Bare Month, which is nineteen days, and he asked her for it once on the second day and has not asked since and says so, and she says she has noticed every day and is not asking him why, and he says he is not going to put it in a room because a room has a date on the door and everything that goes in it is on a page by the fourth hour, and nobody apologises and nobody mends it and the fifth condition is not given.** A woman of about twenty-one came in and said out loud that the not-waiting is not a mood and that nobody in the room is going to change it by saying something, and she and the man of about twenty-five had the ninth line of the sheet between them and neither of them put it there. **The road north of the Marches is shut and does not open and nobody shut it. The nine words are not said. The word for what the four of them are is at zero.**

9. **WHAT IS STILL OPEN AT THE END OF THE VOLUME, IN ONE LIST.** **The line in the orchard has no name on any page in the world and the reader has been told what it was called for one day of this flood; it was not measured, dug, counted, probed or resolved, and nobody will measure it; the man of about fifty-two has looked at it once from the end of it and has said one sentence that is not an answer and that is on no page.** The ninth line and whether it works, unanswered, and its author is on no roster. The man of about fifty-seven has a line he cannot show anybody, a winter he cannot get back, a length of new rope he has not used, and a fence four feet off something, and nobody told him he was wrong. The man of about twenty-seven washes a board every morning and has decided not to wash the wall above it. The Crown's schedule stands and is correct and is not opened, not filled in and not withdrawn, and the Regent is nine hundred miles away and is not defeated. **The register in this city has an entry with a day on it and nothing under the day, and a shelf has a slate on it with a date on it and nothing under it, and the woman of about sixty-nine's sheet is in a room and not in a book and does not go out of the building.** Four people have not left and were never asked, and three of them are in rooms and the fourth is not coming, and the word for what they are is not in this language. A woman of about twenty-one is in a room and does not know what she is for and nobody in this volume is going to tell her. The man of about twenty-five's nine words are in a coat. The man who trades on a board has a name off it that he took off in his own hand in about the ninth year of this flood and about four hundred people can see the place and not one of them will ever be told. The road north of the Marches is shut on day 350 for fifty-six days and nobody shut it and it does not open in this flood. The board has not been written on since day 225. The island is not owned and nobody picked up the bag.

10. **THE MEASUREMENTS, WITH THE DEFINITIONS, AND THE TWO THINGS A LATER WRITER MUST NOT COMPARE, AND THESE ARE THE FIGURES AFTER THE REVIEW REPAIR AND THE REPAIR IS AT `state/batch-summaries/volume-07-batch-0005.md` §11.** The share of words inside bold marks is **7.0 to 11.6 per cent, a mean of 9.4, in forty-nine spans**, and the longest bolded turn is **eighty-nine** and no turn is over the ninety-five hard ceiling. **THE MEAN IS FOUR AND A HALF POINTS BELOW THE FOUR BATCHES BEFORE IT AND THE CAUSE IS NAMED IN THE BATCH RECORD AND IN `bible/power-system.md` §45 ITEM 9: six of the ten chapters have an object or a piece of ground or a road for a subject.** The chorus frame is **seventy-nine in four hundred and forty-five paragraphs, one in five point six**, and it is at zero in **two** of the ten, 0345 and 0347, and **not** in 0350, which carries two, and the earlier edition of this item said three and was wrong on its own files. The exchange is **134 plain speeches and 27 answered at twenty point one per cent**, and the instrument reproduces Batch 0004's published 16 of 28 exactly, **and the repair did not move it, because a paragraph with no quotation mark and no bold mark is skipped by it.** Weekday tokens are **eighty-one**, **twelve** were repaired, and one token went out and one came in. Long sentences of forty words or more are **273**, the two being the two narration paragraphs the repair inserted. **The word-run figure of twelve is on a sequential token match and is NOT the instrument the earlier batches published and must not be set beside them, and on the longest-common-contiguous-token instrument the five pairs the repair touched all stand at their pre-repair figures.** A fused word is at zero on all ten files. **AND THE COUNT OF THE FENCE IS NOW ON THIS PAGE BECAUSE NONE OF THE ABOVE CHECKS COUNTS AN OBJECT: sixteen cut, nine in the ground on the Saturday, thirteen in the ground on the Monday, three for the Thursday.**

11. **AND ONE INTERVAL FAULT IN THE PLAN LAYER, WHICH A BATCH ROUTES AND DOES NOT SETTLE.** `outline/volume-07.md` §13 says the word was on a slate *for sixteen days*. **The figure is thirty-two, and it is thirty-two on the pages of Chapters 0342 and 0343 with both anchors named in the same sentences, and the outline is not amended by a batch and the amendment belongs to the Volume 07 close.**

12. **THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-07/close/PROMPT.md`, AND IT IS A VOLUME CLOSE AND NOT A BATCH.** It writes no chapter. It reads the finished volume, rewrites and re-verifies `state/volume-07-close.md`, which does not exist yet and which this batch did not create, and then it creates whatever the next volume needs, **and it is the only phase in this repository after an outline phase that is allowed to decide anything, and the four decisions it takes have to be written in `outline/series.md` under *DECISIONS OF RECORD* in its own words, the way the Volume 07 outline phase wrote its four, and one of the four is the interval figure at 11 above.**

## THE VOLUME 07 CLOSE, AND ITS REVIEW REPAIR PASS — the authoritative block for the current phase, and it wins over everything above it

**The next phase is `workspace/volume-08/outline/PROMPT.md`, which is a VOLUME OUTLINE PHASE and not a batch.** `state/volume-07-close.md` is the volume's inheritance and it is the one page a writer reads before planning anything; it was written by the close phase, re-measured by a review, and repaired by a review repair pass whose findings are at its §9. The block headed *THE VOLUME 07 BATCH 0005 — the climax, the cost, the resolution, and the last image*, which this header named until now, is history, together with the blocks headed *THE VOLUME 07 BATCH 0004* and *THE VOLUME 06 CLOSE*. **Everything above the newest block is history and is superseded by it, and a section above the newest one that disagrees with it on a day, a figure or a name is wrong and the newest one wins.** Where this file and a chapter disagree about a day, a name or a figure, the chapter is right.

**WHAT DID NOT MOVE, and it is the short list and it is the important one. No day, no weekday, no anchor, no card, no beat, no thread state and no outline figure moved. `outline/ending.md` was not opened. No new final enemy was introduced. The antagonist ladder, the relationship milestones, Ivenn Marrow, the First Grammar, the Single Witness, the Quiet Choir, the return crisis, the prompt being edited, the settlement labelled unwritten, the sister, Northstar Civic, Celia Rusk, the twelve civic seals and the Charter of Two Worlds are all untouched. The plot of Volume 08 as decision two struck and replaced it is unchanged.**

**WHAT MOVED, and every item is a repair of a published figure and not a change to a story.**

1. **The day map for days 351 to 400 was wrong on twenty-four of its fifty rows and is repaired, and this is the largest single correction in the pass because every Volume 08 chapter dates itself from it.** `bible/power-system.md` §33B's right-hand block began `0376 | 376 | Tuesday`; a weekday is a figure and day 1 is a Tuesday, so **day 376 is a Saturday**, and every row 376 to 399 was one step out until row 0400 came back to a correct Tuesday, which left the block discontinuous with itself and invisible to any count down the column. **All fifty rows recompute from day 1 at zero mismatches. BM numbers were right throughout. 351 to 375 was right throughout. §33A's 301 to 350 re-checks at zero.** The run is now: 351 Tuesday / 352 Wednesday / 353 Thursday / 354 Friday / 355 Saturday / 356 Sunday / 357 Monday, repeating, so **375 is a Friday and 400 is a Tuesday, and 351 is BM 36 and 400 is BM 85, and both ends of the volume are Tuesdays because 350 is a Monday and 351 and 400 are both one more than a multiple of seven.**
2. **§33B item 3's prose contradicted its own table and is corrected: the forty-ninth day of the Bare Month is a Monday, not a Sunday.** Day 364 is a Monday. The thirty-fifth is a Monday and the eighty-fifth is a Tuesday were both right. **A section that states a derived figure in prose beside the same figure in a table has two chances to be wrong and no instrument that compares them.**
3. **Five figures in `outline/series.md`'s Volume 08 decisions block are corrected and the definition is now published beside them, which it was not.** 329 chapter files becomes **350**, in three places. *Commons* is **41 in 19 files either case — 22 in 12 capitalised, 19 in 8 lower-case — and not *41 in 8*, which was two instruments in one figure.** *Player* is **6 in 6 files** and *players* **8 in 6**, and the published 16 was the two added together. ***labor* is a false zero: the manuscript spells it *labour* and stands 58 in 37 files, so the argument that the struck crop-and-water mechanism turned on a noun this book has never used is withdrawn — and the mechanism is still struck, on the untouched ground that this book has no crop-and-water crisis at all.** *Earthside* is **1 in 1 file** word-bounded and case-sensitive, **2 in 2** either case. *Redroot* 65 in 30, *Ivenn* 46 in 26, *power* 3 and *common* 1 in Volume 07 and all nineteen Volume 07 zeros reproduce exactly.
4. **The cast decision merged two men and does not any more.** The refusal of a page with a name at the top of it in **Chapter 0334 is the man of about fifty-seven's**, at `chapters/volume-07/chapter-0334.md:19` and `:27`; the board-trader's refusal is **Chapter 0340**. The words *is a return* are dropped from the board-trader and **the man of about fifty-seven keeps the rope and the half holding, which are his.**
5. **`chapters/volume-07/chapter-0309.md:41` now reads *a man of about thirty-nine*, and it is the one chapter word the whole repair pass made.** The settled age of the man who trades on the Open Hand board is thirty-nine: 133 in 69 files word-bounded, against 6 in 1 for *man of about thirty-seven*, and all six of those are the sack-carrier in `chapters/volume-06/chapter-0293.md`, who is a real person on a page and is not this man. **The record's own figure of *150 in 75* was wrong and is corrected to 132 in 68 before the repair and 133 in 69 after, with the definition beside it; the 150 was an unbounded search that returns the 23 instances of *woman of about thirty-nine*, and its Volume 05 file count was 26 where there are 19.**

**THE STANDING PROHIBITIONS CARRY FORWARD UNCHANGED, at `state/volume-07-close.md` §4 at full length, and the ones a next writer is most likely to break are these. The sixth form, *the Nth day of the Bare Month*, the Nth being day less 315, established on a page in Chapter 0316 by Sera Vail after a refusal whose reason was the refuser's, WITH NO LENGTH STATED. NO CHAPTER MAY USE AN ORDINAL FOR A MONTH, forward-looking from day 301, and the one load-bearing existing use at `chapters/volume-04/chapter-0152.md:79` is left standing. NO CHAPTER OF VOLUME 08 MAY WRITE A DAY AFTER THE EIGHTY-FIFTH OF THE BARE MONTH, MAY NOT SAY THE MONTH IS NEARLY OVER, MAY NOT SAY WHAT IT IS A MONTH OF, AND MAY NOT CONVERT EIGHTY-FIVE INTO A SPAN. A WEEKDAY IS A FIGURE AND IS NEVER TAKEN FROM A NEIGHBOURING CHAPTER, A CARD, A DAY MAP OR A STATE FILE. THE DAY-154 SENTENCE IS NOT SAID, RESTATED, PARAPHRASED, IMPROVED ON OR PUT INTO A SECOND MOUTH. THE WORD ON THE SECOND SLATE IS ON A PAGE ONCE IN CHAPTER 0310, IN NARRATION TWICE IN CHAPTER 0342, IN NO MOUTH, AND AT ZERO ELSEWHERE. THE WALL SAID ONE THING IN FIFTY DAYS AND IT IS SPENT. NOBODY IN THIS BOOK GETS STRONGER. ADRIAN IS NAMED IN THE CHAPTERS WHERE HE READS A PAGE AND IN NO CHAPTER WHERE PEOPLE DECLINE TO ASK HIM, AND HE WAS NAMED IN ONE OF VOLUME 07'S LAST TEN.**

**AND THE FOUR NEW RULES ABOUT INSTRUMENTS, which are not prose and bind every batch from this line. A batch's record publishes the bolded share for each of its ten chapters with the chapter's subject beside it, and NO VOLUME MAY READ ITS OWN MEAN AS A QUALITY FIGURE AND NO REVIEW MAY CERTIFY OR FAIL A VOLUME ON A MEAN. A batch's record publishes the count of paragraphs carrying neither speaker marker, WHICH IS A FAULT AND NOT A STATISTIC AND MUST BE ZERO, and the count of every set of objects its ten chapters name more than once between them, read in each chapter that names it. A descriptor map counts occurrences and does not decide which occurrence is the same person, and that decision belongs to an outline or a close phase. And four days of notice is an instrument: a notice on any other day is a three-day instrument, and a three-day instrument does not fail, it arrives warm.**

**THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-08/outline/PROMPT.md`, AND IT IS AN OUTLINE PHASE AND NOT A BATCH.** It writes `outline/volume-08.md` and the first Volume 08 batch's cards and no prose, it inherits four decisions of record in `outline/series.md` which are the gate, it amends `bible/power-system.md` §33A item 7's register of notices, and it creates exactly one next phase, which is the batch that writes Chapter 0351.

**THE FIVE DATES A LATER WRITER NEEDS AND CANNOT DERIVE FROM ANYTHING.** **Day 351, a Tuesday:** the return comes up the road, and the first notice goes out for a room on day 355. **Day 353, a Thursday:** the last three fence posts go in. **Day 357, a Monday:** the man of about thirty-eight is asked what the column is for. **Day 375, a Friday:** somebody asks one of the four whether it will go. **Day 391, a Sunday:** three people go down the road, and the register line carries that day and nothing under it. **Day 393, a Tuesday:** the return goes out on its date with a name at the top of it, and the volume's one panel is spent. **Day 396, a Friday:** the new question is asked and is not answered.**

**THE STANDING PROHIBITIONS CARRY FORWARD UNCHANGED, at `state/volume-07-close.md` §4 at full length, and the ones a Volume 08 batch is most likely to break are these.** The sixth form, *the Nth day of the Bare Month*, the Nth being day less 315, established on a page in Chapter 0316 by Sera Vail, a notary of fifty, after a refusal whose reason was the refuser's, WITH NO LENGTH STATED. **NO CHAPTER OF VOLUME 08 MAY WRITE A DAY AFTER THE EIGHTY-FIFTH OF THAT MONTH, MAY NOT SAY THE MONTH IS NEARLY OVER, MAY NOT SAY WHAT IT IS A MONTH OF, AND MAY NOT CONVERT EIGHTY-FIVE INTO A SPAN.** NO CHAPTER MAY USE AN ORDINAL FOR A MONTH, forward-looking from day 301, and the one load-bearing existing use at `chapters/volume-04/chapter-0152.md:79` is left standing. A WEEKDAY IS A FIGURE AND IS NEVER TAKEN FROM A NEIGHBOURING CHAPTER, A CARD, A DAY MAP OR A STATE FILE. **THE WORD ON THE SECOND SLATE, MEASURED BY THIS PHASE ON VOLUME 07'S FIFTY FILES: the lower-case form stands at 2, both in `chapters/volume-07/chapter-0342.md` at lines 9 and 121, both in narration; the capitalised form stands at 1, at `chapters/volume-07/chapter-0310.md:31`. The ninth line is not moved, dated, read aloud, taken down, thanked for or resolved. The consent fracture is not mended, nobody apologises for it, and the fifth condition is not given. The two women of twenty-nine are never in one room. The two rolls of the same gates are not put side by side and *holder* is not defined. The Crown's schedule is not opened, filled in or withdrawn. The Regent is nine hundred miles away and is not defeated. Nobody in this book gets stronger. **ADRIAN IS NAMED IN THE CHAPTERS WHERE HE READS A PAGE AND IN NO CHAPTER WHERE PEOPLE DECLINE TO ASK HIM, and he was named in one of Volume 07's last ten and this volume's cards name which is which in every card he appears in.**

**AND TWO NEW PROHIBITIONS THIS PHASE ADDS, both of which are about counting and both of which are the cheapest way to break a refusal.** **NO CHAPTER OF VOLUME 08 MAY COUNT THE DAYS SINCE THE WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-ONE SAID NO, AT ANY VALUE, IN NARRATION OR IN A MOUTH. Counting them turns a refusal into a thing that is running out.** And **the figure that the man of about thirty-eight has in his head, which is not the figure he sends, is on no page and is printed in no chapter of this volume and in no file this phase produces.** He has been adding to it about four times a year for nine years without being asked to.

**AND THE FOUR RULES ABOUT INSTRUMENTS, which are not prose and bind every batch from this line.** A batch's record publishes the bolded share for each of its ten chapters with the chapter's subject beside it, and **NO VOLUME MAY READ ITS OWN MEAN AS A QUALITY FIGURE AND NO REVIEW MAY CERTIFY OR FAIL A VOLUME ON A MEAN.** A batch's record publishes the count of paragraphs carrying neither speaker marker, **WHICH IS A FAULT AND NOT A STATISTIC AND MUST BE ZERO**, and the count of every set of objects its ten chapters name more than once between them, read in each chapter that names it. **A descriptor map counts occurrences and does not decide which occurrence is the same person, and that decision belongs to an outline or a close phase** — and the one this phase made is at `outline/volume-08.md` §20.9, where **the two men of about thirty-eight are declared to be two people**, the wharf man who speaks the volume's name and the barrow man who took nine boards off a wall. And four days of notice is an instrument: a notice on any other day is a three-day instrument, and a three-day instrument does not fail, it arrives warm.

**AND THE SEVEN ROUND FIGURES THAT FALL INSIDE DAYS 351 TO 400, ALL OF THEM TRAPS, ALL OF THEM NAMED SO THAT NO BATCH WALKS INTO ONE, because a figure that comes out round has been checked twice and not once.** Day 351: the case has stood on a table exactly a hundred days, from day 251. Day 368: the share with the post taken out is exactly two hundred days, from day 168. Day 385: the thirty-nine men under the near gate are exactly three hundred days unpaid, from day 85. Day 400: the bag on the boards is a hundred and sixty days old, from day 240; the board has stood unwritten a hundred and seventy-five days, from day 225; the trestle count is three hundred and sixty, from day 40; and the four have not left in two hundred and fifty-five days, from day 145, **and 255 is the last day of the Long Month, so a reader who sees two hundred and fifty-five will see a month and not a count.** A chapter that prints any of these names the day it is measured from in the same sentence.

**AND THE ANCHOR THAT HAS ENDED, which a later writer will otherwise compute.** The barrow of boards against the wall of that gate was 54 or 55 days old at day 350 counting from day 296 inclusive or from day 295, **and the boards came off that wall on day 347, so the anchor has NO VALUE AT ALL after day 347 and there is no value at day 400 because the barrow is not there.** No chapter of Volume 08 may print one.

A SHARE WITH THE POST TAKEN OUT WAS IN THE WRONG COAT AND IS NOW IN THE RIGHT ONE, AND IT IS A CANON ERROR AND NOT A FAULT OF STYLE.** It is Adrian Vale's — offered to him on day 168, in the inside of his coat ever since, taken out once, neither said yes nor said no. **Chapter 0368 as first written had the man of about thirty-nine carrying it and saying its age out loud on a wharf, and two people on the same boards are two people.** **The figure is now said by the man of about thirty-eight, about a man in this city who is not on those boards, and how long that man has known is on no page and is not going to be, and the coat is not opened, and Adrian Vale is in no chapter of days 361 to 370 in any form.** The chapter now names the thirteenth day of the **Long** Month, because the thirteenth of an unnamed month is day 328 and only the Long Month makes two hundred come out.

**AND THE OTHER SEVENTEEN EDITS ARE PROSE, EVERY ONE OF THEM A CONCRETE FINDING, AND NONE OF THEM CUT A SPEECH.** A day anchor in Chapter 0362 that contradicted Chapter 0357 and Chapter 0363's own count of mornings. Two places in Chapter 0368 that put a wharf in a market. **1. THE BLOCK ABOVE SAID FOUR ROOM FIGURES WERE NINE AND THEN TEN AND THREE OF THEM WERE, AND THAT IS THE FINDING THIS PASS EXISTS TO REPAIR.** `chapter-0364.md:7` had ten and `:39` had seven, and the record's §10 item 4 and the block above both said all four were right. **Line 39 reads ten now.** A continuity file that certifies a figure is the one place in this repository where a wrong number does the most damage, because it is the file a writer trusts instead of measuring.

**2. A PRIVATE FIGURE WAS ON THREE MEN IN THE TEN CHAPTERS OF THIS BATCH AND IS NOW ON TWO, AND THE REASON IS WORTH CARRYING FORWARD.** **About two hundred rooms is not one man's number.** `state/character-state.md` and `state/open-threads.md` give the **man of about thirty-one** about two hundred rooms and about eleven kitchens a month and the distinction that he is the only person in nine kitchens nobody asked anything in, on a page at `chapters/volume-07/chapter-0343.md:29` in a different form. Three men, one figure, and the reason two of them were fixed and the third was not is scope and not judgement.**

**3. TWO MEN STILL SHARE THE FIGURE AND THE NINE, AND NOBODY FIXED IT AND NOBODY MAY.** The man of about thirty-one is the only person in nine kitchens nobody asked anything in; the man of about forty-eight has not been asked anything in about nine of his two hundred rooms. **Both are in their own mouths or in a state file quoting a mouth, neither was put there by this batch, and a batch may not decide that two men are not the same figure.** Carried as a debt at `state/open-threads.md` for the Volume 08 close.

**4. ONE OBJECT WAS GIVEN TO THE WRONG MAN SEVEN CHAPTERS APART, AND CHAPTER 0369 WAS THE ONE THAT MOVED.** `chapter-0362.md:9` reads *a place with about nine households behind one wall*, which is the lane past two gates and is the form used in Chapters 0351, 0354, 0356, 0359 and 0362. `chapter-0369.md:51` had the man of about thirty-nine saying *a household of about nine behind a wall*, which is the woman of about fifty-two's household, about a lane he has never been to. **He says nine households now, and `:53` no longer calls them about nine people and no longer calls the two things in his one sentence two households. The record's object inventory has no row for either figure, and an inventory with no row for a number is an inventory that cannot catch the number being given to the wrong person; no row was added, because adding one is a claim that the two figures are the same and they are not.**

**5. THE SALT WAS BEING CUT OFF A BARROW FOR TWO HUNDRED DAYS IN ONE SENTENCE AND THIRTY-FIVE IN ANOTHER, IN CHAPTER 0368 AND AGAIN IN CHAPTER 0369.** `chapter-0368.md:7` and `chapter-0369.md:19` say every morning of this flood; `chapter-0368.md:49` and `chapter-0369.md:27` said for about five weeks. **About five weeks is a real figure on a real page and it belongs to a different observation — the man of about thirty-eight walking past two barrows that belong to nobody, at `chapter-0368.md:23` and in the narration at `:13`. Both of those stand, and the figure was copied from them.** Both salt clauses now read every morning of this flood.

**6. ONE CHAPTER CREDITED THE WRONG MAN WITH A SCENE HE IS NOT IN.** `chapter-0370.md:19` gave the man of about thirty-nine a true thing about a wall on a shut road: the wall with a shape going off it is Chapter 0365, on a lane, and it is the man of about thirty-four with a barrow, and the shut road is Chapter 0364 and it is the man of about fifty-seven with a new rope he has never used. **He has said nothing out loud on a road in these ten chapters and the chapter now says what he did do, in order: a board of nineteen rates put down flat on a wet board on a Friday, which Chapter 0368 says nobody in this city has ever seen him do, and a number said out loud in a room on Saturday and thanked by nobody, which is Chapter 0369.**

**7. ONE SENTENCE HAD A STRANDED PREPOSITION AND IT WAS THE LAST SENTENCE OF ITS CHAPTER.** `chapter-0365.md:65` read *a lane nine households took their days out of in the Short Month* and reads *gave their days back*, which is the established idiom at `chapters/volume-07/chapter-0344.md:89` and `:0347:89` and is what the nine households of that lane did.

**AND THE FIGURES ARE CORRECTED IN THE RECORD AT ITS §3 AND IN THE BLOCKS ABOVE, AND THEY ARE NOT REPEATED HERE.** Two were wrong and both are named beside the figures that replace them: the bolded share of words, and the count of chapters that moved when the earlier repairs were made, which is seven and not five. **And the chorus frame is now measured on two instruments, because the declared string *about four people in* returned forty-seven in a record and returns sixty-seven on the files, and the four chapters it called at zero carry forty-nine instances of the same frame under other prepositions. A metric whose unit is a four-word phrase rewards a chapter for changing *in* to *on*, and the claim that this batch is less dense than its predecessor is true on the narrow instrument and false on the wide one. No frame was cut. The two instruments and both ratios are at the record's §3 item 4 and the next prompt carries both.**

**AND ONE CRAFT FINDING MEASURED AND PUBLISHED AND NOT ACTED ON: the name of the month is printed once in ten chapters and the form *that month* twenty times.** It is the house pattern of the volume before it and no batch printed it by accident. **The next prompt is told to name the month in the date in each of its chapters, in the plan's own form, and that form touches no prohibition in this volume.**

**AND THE FOUR DEBTS CARRY FORWARD WHOLE AND UNPAID, AND THIS PASS REPAIRED NONE OF THEM AND TOOK NONE OF THEM.** The same words the last pass used, because the second time would have been no better than the first. **AND THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-08/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`, AND IT IS A BATCH.**
