# VOLUME 08, BATCH 0003 — the authoritative block for the current phase

**This block wins over everything above it. A batch closes no thread and opens none, and everything the Volume 08 Batch 0002 block above carries is still live at the length it is at. Its record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0003.md` and `outline/volume-08.md` is still the plan, and where a chapter and the plan disagree the chapter is right.**

**1. THE SIXTH NOTICE IS ON A PAGE AND IT IS FOUR DAYS AND THE SEVENTH IS DAY 389.** Chapter 0371, day 371, a Monday, the fifty-sixth day of the Bare Month. The sheet was folded twice with a thumb, ruled into two by the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register because there is no form in four hundred miles with a room on it, and it carried a day and a place and nothing else, and there was no name at the top of it and none may be added. **Its own morning and the room's day are in one sentence with the count: this morning is the fifty-sixth day of the Bare Month, a Monday, and the room it is for is on the sixtieth day of that month, a Friday, and that is four days counted from this morning including this morning.** The price of the missing name was said out loud by the woman who writes them — that if you come to that room on that day, then afterward there is nothing whatever in four hundred miles that can say you were there. The man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living took it down nine steps and did not open it. **THE PROMPT THIS BATCH INHERITED CALLED THIS NOTICE THE SEVENTH OF THE SEVEN AND IT IS THE SIXTH, and the register of the seven is at `bible/power-system.md` §33A item 7, §47 item 5, `outline/volume-08.md` §14.4 and §20.10 and `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0002.md` §11, and all five give 351, 354, 359, 362, 366, 371 and 389 for rooms on 355, 358, 363, 366, 370, 375 and 393.** *Open.*

**2. THE FOURTH PLACE HAS A NEW REASON FOR BEING EMPTY AND THE REASON IS A MAN AND NOT A HOUSEHOLD.** Chapter 0372, day 372, a Tuesday. The man of about thirty-eight came up nine steps at about the eighth hour, sat down at the end of the bench, and was not asked anything. He said out loud, and nobody asked him why, **that he has filled in the first, second and third of the four places four times a year since the winter before the water out of what he is given, and that he has never once filled in the fourth one, and that nobody has ever sent him anywhere to find out how you fill in a fourth one, and that he has assumed since the winter before the water that it is a share of something, and that a share of something has to come out of a household, and that he has never gone to a household.** The second speech: he is not going to, and he is not going to be asked, and if he is asked he will say the same three sentences in the same order. **Nobody sent him. The woman of about fifty-two was not in that room and was not asked for her days and has not been asked for her days a second time anywhere in these ten chapters, and her sentence is not improved on and is not anybody else's sentence.** *Open, and the household is still not asked.*

**3. THE MIDPOINT IS ON A PAGE AND THE ANSWER IS NOT.** Chapter 0375, day 375, a Friday, the sixtieth day of the Bare Month, the room over a market, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, about nine people. **Tamsin Quill, at a bench with a folded sheet of road in her apron, asked the woman of about twenty-one, four feet off her with a pen in her apron, whether it will go. Two words and a question, said once, in the local language, in daylight, in front of about nine people, and she did not ask it twice.** Nobody in that room asked her anything else. **What she said is not on any page this batch produced and is not in any file this batch produced.** The form on the table was not touched and no column was filled and no document was produced and no place was named in that room. The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall was at the back with his hand flat on his own inside breast pocket and did not offer the chalk. The man of about thirty-nine was at the back where the light does not reach, with his own slate flat on his knee, and said nothing at the point where a room runs out of forms. **The second slate was on the shelf behind the table with a date on it and nothing under it and nobody went near the shelf.** **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER WAS NOT IN THAT ROOM, and the question was asked at about the eighth hour and she came up to the step at the back with no name on it at about the tenth hour with the slate under her arm and did not come in and was there until about the fourth hour of the afternoon, and the two of them were about four paces apart and said nothing to each other, and the two women of twenty-nine are not in one room on any page and nothing in this volume settles whether they are one woman and nothing may.** The fourth of the four is not in this city and is not going to be asked about anything by anybody, and **no chapter of these ten counted the days since she said no at any value in narration or in a mouth.** *Open, and nobody is going to be the second one to ask.*

**4. A YARD, AND A MAN WHO WORKED SOMETHING OUT AND WAS NOT TOLD HE WAS RIGHT.** Chapter 0376, day 376, a Saturday, the sixty-first day of the Bare Month, behind a row of kitchens at the end of a lane. **Adrian Vale is in this chapter and in no other of these ten.** The person in the yard is a woman of about sixty-nine with an empty tin in both hands which she did not open, and **the man of about fifty-two who digs is not in that yard and nobody in it is going to ask him anything.** He watched about five hours, worked out at about the third hour that the order nine things are done in is the order he gave, and said it out loud in order. She told him he had it the other way round at the second kettle, said she was not going to tell him which one he had wrong, said neither was anybody else in that yard, and gave her reason: a man who is told has it right tomorrow and a man who is not told will have it wrong tomorrow and will think about it. **The chapter says he worked it out and does not say he was right, and about four people in that yard noticed a thing about one of the nine and are not going to say it out loud, and he went away along a lane without being told. There is no form in four hundred miles with a place for the order things are done in, he is the only person in this city who has it, and nobody asked him to write it down.** *Open.*

**5. THE TWO FOUR-PLACE FORMS WERE KEPT APART OUT LOUD, IN A ROOM, BY A PERSON, AND THIS IS THE ONLY CHAPTER OF VOLUME 08 THAT MAY DO IT.** Chapter 0378, day 378, a Monday, the sixty-third day of the Bare Month. The return was on a bench and the case was on a table four feet away, both in that room, both unopened. The man of about thirty-four put a hand out and stopped about nine inches short of the form and said out loud that he was not going to put the two together, that he had been trying since a Tuesday on a lane, and that he was wrong to try. **He then counted eight places aloud: a page on the bench with four places under a heading, a docket in the case with four places in it, and not one of the eight the same as any of the other seven; the date on one is not the date on the other; the gate on one is not the gate on the other; and the third place in that case is a word nobody in this city has said out loud in a room in about nine years, which he has wondered about for a fortnight and has not said and is not going to say, and that is not modesty, that is a woman at a gate telling him the whole of a roll in four sentences to nine feet of stone and refusing to be told where two bars are.** The roll at the gate: a gate, a keeper, a set of days, three places, four streets off, not in that room and not going to be brought into it. **The two rolls of the same gates are not put side by side, the case is not opened, the schedule is not filled in and is not withdrawn and is not the subject of the chapter, *holder* is at zero in all ten of these files and is not defined in a mouth by anybody, and the two rolls are not laid beside each other in any chapter of this volume.** *Open.*

**6. NINE ROWS NOBODY CAN DATE, AND A ROOM THAT DID NOT COPY THEM.** Chapter 0379, day 379, a Tuesday. The register has three columns, the first for what went out, the second for what came back, and the third empty on purpose since a Thursday in that month. The nine rows at the back are in an instrument this city stopped writing in years ago; she can read the hand and every word of what was asked and cannot tell what day any of it happened on. **They have not been looked at since the eighth day of that month, and that is fifty-six days back from the sixty-fourth, and the figure is in the sentence that names the day it is measured from.** The room decided not to copy them, and the reason is in her own mouth: a copy has to be dated to be of any use and not one of those nine can be dated, and a page that cannot be dated is the only kind of page in this city that no office nine hundred miles off can ask to have produced. **Nothing was entered this morning and no name went under any day and the third column is still empty and the second slate still has a date on it and nothing under it.** *Open, and the register line with nothing under a day is day 391 and is not this batch's.*

**7. THE FOLDED SHEET OF ROAD IS ON A PAGE TWICE IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS AND IT IS NOT GOING ANYWHERE.** Chapter 0373, day 373, a Wednesday: she took it out of her apron on the bottom step of nine steps in the open, unfolded it on her knees, and folded it again in four with the same four creases, and the man of about thirty-four counted the places on it from about nine feet off and told her he had counted it and had not asked her one thing about any of it, and she did not hand it to him. **The fourth crease has gone soft and that is the whole of what has changed on it, and she said the reason out loud, and the reason is that a crease is a thing about her hands and not about the paper, and she is the only person in four hundred miles who can tell you which of those two it is.** Chapter 0380, day 380, a Wednesday, the sixty-fifth day of the Bare Month: she put it on the bench in the middle of the room in front of about nine people and did not hand it round, unfolded it, said out loud that there are about nine places on it and about nine of them in the room and that the two nines are not the same nine and that she was telling them so before any one of them worked it out, and then said that it is not going to anybody, not to be written out on anything, not into a form, and not to a man who fills in four places four times a year. **It is one sheet and it is Tamsin Quill's and it is not the sheet thirty years old in a tin, and the two are never in one room, and it did not leave that room and it is not a page and is not going to be made one.** *Open.*

**8. AND THE THINGS THESE TEN CHAPTERS DID NOT DO.** The word on the second slate is at zero in all ten files and no chapter gives the mark in a field a word by any route. The day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on, **and the register-keeper's line about the day after a name goes at the top of a page was drafted into Chapter 0375 and taken out again before this record was made, because the man who is right about it in this volume is the man of about thirty-nine in Chapter 0393 and that chapter is not this batch's.** The question of day 346 is not answered. Nobody was asked whether any of the four will go except the one person, once, on day 375. The fence is not in these ten chapters and no chapter of the ten prints a post, a withy or a total. The town four hundred miles inland is not entered and the page is not fetched. The Bare Month did not end and no chapter printed a day after the sixty-fifth or said the month was nearly over or said what it is a month of, and the words *length*, *long* and *short* applied to the month are at zero, and **the month's name is printed once or twice in every one of these ten chapters and *that month* eight times, which is the legibility instruction this batch was given and the one thing its two predecessors did not do.** No barrow was spent on anything; the one occurrence of the word is a denial. The keeper at the shut gate was not asked anything in any of these ten and her brass key is not in any of them. *Open.*

**AND THE FOUR DEBTS CARRY FORWARD WHOLE AND UNPAID AND UNROUTED, AND THIS BATCH REPAIRED NONE OF THEM AND TOOK NONE OF THEM.** The nineteen stray closing quotation marks at `0281:37,65,81`, `0282:31,39,57`, `0283:47,53`, `0284:13,39`, `0285:39`, `0286:63`, `0287:25,41`, `0288:43`, `0289:31,49`, `0290:9,25`; `chapters/volume-07/chapter-0341.md:149`; the forty-word near-duplicate across the 0254 and 0262 boundary; and the unmarked first-person paragraph at `chapters/volume-04/chapter-0175.md:47`. **And the fifth, *about two hundred rooms* on three men of whom two share a nine in a second place, which is a debt for the Volume 08 close and which no batch may settle.**

**AND THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-08/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`, AND IT IS A BATCH.**

## VOLUME 08, BATCH 0003 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS — this block wins over every block above it

**The prose of Chapters 0371 to 0380 was reworked sentence by sentence and NOTHING THAT IS CONTINUITY MOVED.** The disposition is `reviews/volume-08/batch-0003.md`; the batch's own half is `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0003.md` §11. Verified on the files: **the set of bolded speech paragraphs in all ten files is byte-identical to the first edition, and the multiset of every digit string across the ten files is unchanged at zero lost and zero added.** So every one of the eight numbered items in the block above stands exactly as written, and the reader should not expect a corrected figure in this one.

**WHAT MOVED WAS THE SENTENCE AROUND THEM, and the finding it establishes is a class and not an incident.** The narration in all ten chapters had collapsed into one repeated frame that restated the sentence immediately before it — one instance in every 1.9 paragraphs, no chapter at zero, sixteen consecutive times in Chapter 0371 and twenty-three in Chapter 0375. **The observations the frame was carrying were real and are all still on the page, and what was cut was the sentence saying somebody in the room had worked the observation out.** **The chorus frame therefore remains a declared device of this volume at thirty-two instances in one hundred and ninety-eight paragraphs, one in 6.2, and fifteen on the declared head *about four people in*, one in 13.2, and the rule that governs it is now a sentence and not a threshold: a chorus frame may carry an observation into a paragraph, and it may not restate one the paragraph has already made.** That rule is at `outline/volume-08.md` §17 item 11a and it binds the next batch.

**AND THREE CONTINUITIES THAT WERE ALWAYS THE PLAN'S ARE NOW NAMED AS DEBTS, because a review found them and a batch may not fix them.** **The room over the market is the lead in seven of this batch's ten chapters — 0371, 0372, 0374, 0375, 0378, 0379 and 0380 — and in nine of the volume's first thirty, and 0376 and 0377 are the two that leave it; this is a fault of the allocation and the plan is not built this way, since `outline/volume-08.md` §5 names six other locations. Adrian Vale is in two of the ten and five of the thirty, and §4's list of 0352, 0355, 0360, 0371, 0376, 0381 and 0392 is the binding one and was honoured exactly. And the cast of these ten is separated for a reader only by the object in each person's hand — the chalk, the slate, the folding knife, the satchel — where `outline/volume-08.md` §17 item 9 requires a second handle beside the age and the trade.** All three are carried at `state/open-threads.md` as Debts 6, 8 and 7, for the Volume 08 close, and all three are in the next prompt by name.

**AND ONE THING WAS CORRECTED IN THE PLAN ITSELF RATHER THAN ONLY IN A STATE FILE.** `outline/volume-08.md` §8 placed Adrian in the midpoint chapter and §4's list did not. **§8 is now corrected in `outline/volume-08.md`: §4 governs, Chapter 0375 has no Adrian in it in any form, and the claim is withdrawn in the plan and not only in a batch record.**

**THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-08/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`, AND IT IS A BATCH, AND ITS RANGE IS UNCHANGED: Chapters 0381 to 0390, days 381 to 390, from a Thursday to a Saturday.**

## VOLUME 08, BATCH 0003 — THE STATE-LAYER COMPACTION, and it is the first time in this repository that anything in the state layer has been MOVED rather than appended to

**A review of this batch raised the state files as a MEDIUM finding and it was right: `state/continuity.md` stood at 1.1 MB, `state/character-state.md` at 712 KB, `state/chapter-summaries.md` at 644 KB and `state/open-threads.md` at 496 KB, every one of them an append-only archive in which each batch restated in full what it had done, and the next writer was instructed to read roughly two megabytes of prior context before writing a word. AGENTS.md asks for rolling summaries. These were not rolling summaries.**

**WHAT WAS DONE.** **The body of each of the five large state files that belongs to Volumes 01 to 06 was moved to `state/archive/`, verbatim, and each archive carries in its own header why it moved, what is true of it, how many lines went, and the SHA256 of the moved text so that a later pass can prove it did not alter what it moved.** Nothing was edited, abbreviated, corrected, summarised away or deleted. **What is live in each file is the whole of Volume 07 and the whole of Volume 08 to date, which is one complete volume and one in progress, and that is a rolling window rather than an archive.**

| File | Before | After | Moved to |
|---|---|---|---|
| `state/current.md` | 323 KB | **95 KB** | `state/archive/current.md.volumes-01-to-06.md` |
| `state/continuity.md` | 1,072 KB | **204 KB** | `state/archive/continuity.md.volumes-01-to-06.md` |
| `state/character-state.md` | 711 KB | **260 KB** | `state/archive/character-state.md.volumes-01-to-06.md` |
| `state/chapter-summaries.md` | 642 KB | **148 KB** | `state/archive/chapter-summaries.md.volumes-01-to-06.md` |
| `state/open-threads.md` | 498 KB | **152 KB** | `state/archive/open-threads.md.volumes-01-to-06.md` |
| **live total** | **3,246 KB** | **859 KB** | archive 2,387 KB, retained whole |

**THE CHECK, RUN AFTER THE LAST EDIT AND NOT BEFORE, AND IT IS REPRODUCIBLE FROM GIT.** Every non-blank, non-heading line of all five files at `HEAD` was compared against the union of the live file and its archive: **zero lines lost in all five, and zero headings lost in all five.** The character roster at the head of `state/character-state.md` and the file headers of the other four were kept in place, and `state/current.md`, which had no file-level header left after the cut, was given one, because **its first line had been naming `volume-08-batch-0001` as the current phase and that phase ran three batches ago** — a stale navigation line of exactly the class every prior review in this repository has raised.

**AND WHY IT IS RECORDED AT LENGTH INSTEAD OF BEING DONE QUIETLY.** **A record that quietly rewrites itself is the failure this repository names, and this is the largest rewrite of the state layer since the manuscript began.** So: the cut is stated, the reason is stated, the table is published, the archives are retained whole, and the checks that prove nothing was lost are named so a later phase can rerun them. **A writer who needs Volumes 01 to 06 is not deprived of them; they are in one directory and it is called `archive`.**

**AND WHAT WAS DELIBERATELY NOT COMPACTED.** `state/volume-01-close.md` through `state/volume-07-close.md` and all of `state/batch-summaries/` are untouched, because a close record is a decision of record and a batch record is the witness of a phase, and neither is a rolling file. **`state/phase-ledger.json` was not touched and never may be; it still reads `phase-000-bootstrap / planned / attempts: 0` and every review in this repository has recorded that and none of them has edited it.**

## THE FIGURES THAT HOLD ACROSS THE TEN AND MAY NOT BE PRINTED DIFFERENTLY BY ANY LATER CHAPTER

- **The notices of this volume: seven, and the seventh went out on day 389 for the room on day 393.** The seven went out on 351, 354, 359, 362, 366, 371 and 389, for rooms on 355, 358, 363, 366, 370, 375 and 393. All four days. All unsigned. Nothing at the top of any of them. To a place and not to a house. **Chapter 0389 names both of its own figures in one sentence with the count and the sentence is on the page; Chapter 0388 rules the seventh and does not send it, and says the room it is for is the seventy-eighth day of that month, a Tuesday, which is day 393.**
- **The return: one form, a heading and four places under it — a district, a population, a rota and a share — and no place for the person.** The fourth is a household's. **No chapter of these ten prints a fifth place. The Crown's schedule is a different four-place form, is never laid beside it, and the word *holder* is at zero in all ten files.**
- **The register and the slates: one register with three columns, the third empty on purpose since a Thursday in that month; one second slate on the shelf behind the table with a date on it and nothing under it.** **The third column stayed empty, the second slate stayed on the shelf in five of the ten files, nobody went near the shelf in any of the ten, nothing was entered in the register in any of the ten, and no name went under any day.**
- **The folded sheet of road: one, with about nine places on it, folded in four, the same four creases in the same four places, and the fourth crease has gone soft.** It is Tamsin Quill's and not the sheet thirty years old in a tin, and the two sheets are never in one room. **It is named in eight of these ten chapters and it is the object of Chapter 0386, where it is put on a step in the open in front of one woman and refused.**
- **The barrows: three, one each with the three who go, and a handcart with a hazel mallet whose head is bound in cord and a coil of rope, which is the man of about sixty's and is not one of the three.** **Chapter 0382 is the chapter that brings them to the top of some boards, and by the fifth hour of day 382 there are three standing under a sacking with their handles up, one of them his and two of them nobody's. Day 391 is not this batch's and no barrow went down a road in any of these ten.**
- **The fence: sixteen willow posts, nine on day 348, four more on day 350 making thirteen, the last three on day 353, and eleven withies bound with the same cord.** **Not in this batch's range, and 0397 and 0400 are the chapters that carry it. Chapter 0382 says in one clause that the cord on the mallet is the same cord that is on the fence, which is on a page at `outline/volume-08.md` §20.10, and adds that this is a fact about a piece of cord and is not joined to anything. No chapter of these ten prints a post, a withy or a total.**
- **About four hundred and forty, on eleven miles of flats, and the figure the man of about thirty-eight sends is not the figure he has.** **Chapter 0383 prints the first of the two figures twice, both times in that man's own mouth, says out loud that there is a second one, says that the second one has never been on a page in this city, and prints the second one nowhere. No chapter of these ten puts a column against either figure and none of them puts the two of them in one sentence as two numbers.**
- **The boards and the bare wall: nine boards came off the wall of a shut gate on day 347 and about four inches of dry stone is at the foot of it.** **Chapter 0387 is on a lane past two gates with a wall on it and a shape going off it, and prints no board count, no figure of dry stone and no total. The barrow of boards does not exist after day 347 and appears in none of these ten.**
- **The gate with two bars: one gate, two bars, the second on no roll at all.** **Chapter 0387's shut gate has a bar of ash in two brackets and a brass key on a tarred cord and prints no bar count, and Chapter 0385 has a different gate at the end of a road out of the city. The two gates are not put side by side in any of the ten and no chapter says what the difference is worth.**
- **The man of about thirty-four's piece of chalk, in his own inside breast pocket, not taken out since the forty-third day of the Short Month, which is day 298.** **The full form is printed in six of the ten and in no chapter of the ten is it shortened to a relative phrase, and there is no *the last week of that month* in any of these ten files.**

## WHAT IS NOW TRUE OF EVERY PERSON THESE TEN DAYS PUT ON A PAGE

- **The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall** worked under a gate from the second hour to the seventh on day 385 and lost a day of selling, and has never once said so to the person he sells to. **He took the weight of a barrow up a wet board on his right hand on day 382 and could not close that hand again until he put it in cold water about a quarter of an hour before the light went, and he told nobody, and the man of about thirty-eight knew and did not say that he knew.** He said out loud, before anybody asked him for it, and on five separate days, the reason for each thing: why a notice is not going to be changed, why he is not going to write on a wall, what a room is going to do to a man who said a number on a road, who is going to go up the nine steps first, and why nobody is going to that household. **His chalk has not been out.**
- **The man of about thirty-eight at the wharf** said about four hundred and forty out loud on his own boards on day 383, said there is a second number, and has not said which of the two he believes and is not going to. **He worked the second barrow up the wet boards himself on day 382 and would not let anybody help him, which is not modesty and has not been mistaken for it by anybody in this city.** He has been putting a figure in a column four times a year since the winter before the water and has never once gone to find out what a share is. **A sheet of paper went against the wall at the top end of his boards on day 389 with a stone off a doorstep on it and he looked at it and did not ask what was on it.**
- **The man of about thirty-nine** was asked nothing in nine rooms in a fortnight and then asked one question, in the open, on a wharf. **He said out loud on a road on day 385 that the thirty-nine men under the near gate are three hundred days unpaid, with the eighty-fifth and the three hundred and eighty-fifth named in the sentence, and it is the first figure anybody in four hundred miles has ever attached to those men, and it is on nobody's page.** He said out loud in Chapter 0384, the first whole sentence in nine rooms that was not a question, that there are two empty thirds in that room and they are not the same empty. **He said out loud on a wharf on day 389 that he is not going to be sent for the room, because being sent for is being sent for, and after that there is no way for anybody at the far end of anything to find out whether he went or not.** He was told in advance, in Chapter 0385, what a room is going to do with what he said on a road, and he did not have to work it out in front of nine people.
- **The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register** said out loud, in front of about nine people on day 381, that she was not going to ask a man in her room what was in the inside of his coat, and gave the price of the not-asking, which is that a page in a coat is the only page in four hundred miles that cannot be asked for. **She read all four places under a heading out loud in the local language on day 384 and did not improve on any of them.** She ruled the seventh notice by hand on day 388 and sent it on day 389 and said out loud, while there was still somebody in the room, that there is not going to be an eighth in this flood. **She has not been up the nine steps since.**
- **Tamsin Quill** put the only page in this city that has a road on it on a step in the open on day 386 in front of one woman, was refused, and said out loud that the refusal was of a thing she had already decided and not of a thing she had put in front of the other one. **She did not take the sheet out of her apron at all on day 390 and said out loud that it is not going in anything they take, not against a wall, not into a book and not on the back of a form, and that the crease that has gone soft is the only part of that page anybody in four hundred miles is losing.**
- **The woman of about twenty-one** was asked one question in this flood in a room, on day 375, about whether it will go, and nobody has asked her a second one and nobody ever is going to. **On day 386 she refused a page standing up, before the fourth crease was open, and she was not waiting for it. On day 390 she was asked a different question by a different person — *What will you carry* — and answered *What I am standing in*, and she has not started waiting to be told what she is for.** The nib the man of about forty-eight ground on a step outside a room is named by him once in Chapter 0367 and is named again in Chapter 0390 as a pen in her apron and is not improved on.
- **The man of about forty-eight** sat in a room for five hours on day 390, shut and opened a folding knife with a hinge that does not spring about nine times, and said one word. **Nobody in that room found it remarkable and about four of them found the opposite.** He is described by the second handle, *a man who has been in a great many rooms in this city and has not been asked anything in about nine of them*, and **about two hundred rooms is at zero in all ten of these chapters and may not be used about him in any of them.**
- **The man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living** has carried seven of these sheets to four walls and has not opened one of them, and said out loud in a room on day 389 that he is not going to say the reason a third time in front of nine people. **He is the only person in this city who can be shown to have been in seven places and cannot be shown to have been anywhere.**
- **The man of about twenty-five** has nine words he wrote himself in the inside of his coat, has not said them in four rooms, and on day 387 stood nine feet off a wall, put his flat hand on the softest part of the shape on it, said nothing, and took it off again.
- **The man of about fifty at the shut gate** wrote a date on the post on day 387 and did not rub anything out, said four words out loud to the post, and the four of them were a date and were not for either of the two men. **Her brass key was in her apron pocket and nobody on that lane asked her about it and nobody has.**
- **The man of about sixty with the handcart** brought a hazel mallet and a coil of rope up wet boards on day 382, took the mallet out, and did not hit anything. **The carrying was the whole of what he came for and nobody said so to him.**
- **The thirty-nine men under the near gate** turn up. The bar goes across at the seventh hour every working day. The ruled line at the bottom of the column is still ruled and still empty and one of the thirty-nine could have filled it and did not.
- **The fourth of the four** is not in this city and is not going to be asked about anything by anybody, and is named as such and in no other words in Chapters 0384, 0386 and 0390. **A party of four is four. No chapter of these ten counted the days since she said no, at any value, in narration or in a mouth.**

## WHAT DID NOT MOVE

**No day, no weekday, no month day, no anchor, no form, no notice, no object count and no plan beat moved. `outline/ending.md` was not opened. No new final enemy was introduced and no antagonist ladder was extended. The seven notices still stand at 351→355, 354→358, 359→363, 362→366, 366→370, 371→375 and 389→393, and the one this batch put on a page is the seventh. The volume's own name is still a man's answer spoken once in Chapter 0357 and written down in no file, and this block does not record it. Adrian Vale is still in no chapter of these ten in any form but Chapter 0381, and the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten. The man of about thirty-four's chalk is still in his own breast pocket, unout since the forty-third day of the Short Month. The man of about fifty-nine is still nobody's man and is still the last image's man. The woman of about fifty-two is still not going to be asked. And the next phase is `workspace/volume-08/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`.**

## VOLUME 08, BATCH 0004 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS — this block wins over every block above it

**Six prose faults, one wrong figure in this batch's own record and the same figure in the bible, and three faults in the next phase's prompt. All ten are repaired. No chapter was restarted and no day, no weekday, no month day, no notice, no form, no object count that holds, no anchor, no cast member and no decision of record moved.** The disposition is `reviews/volume-08/batch-0004.md` and the pages are at `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0004.md` §11.

**THE ROOM GEOMETRY OF CHAPTER 0381 IS NOW SETTLED, and it is the only thing in this block that changes a fact a later chapter could have contradicted.** The light comes in over the step at the back with no name on it and stops about nine inches past the near end of the bench and does not come any further. The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall is at the near end of the bench. **The man of about thirty-nine is against the wall at the end of that bench nearest the step**, with his own slate flat on his knee and his right hand over the face of it, and he is in the lit strip, and neither man can be seen from the step, and Adrian Vale is at the far end of the bench with the form turned round to face him and reads a page that is not his. **A later chapter of this volume may not put the man of about thirty-nine behind the step or out of the light in this room, and the phrase *against the wall by the step at the back where the light does not reach* belongs to the other rooms he stands at in this volume and not to this one.**

**AND THE ROOM COUNT HAS ONE OWNER AND IT IS NOT THE MAN AT THE NEAR END OF THE BENCH.** The man of about thirty-nine's is: at the back of three rooms in a fortnight on day 363, four on 372 and four on 375, a sixth time in a fortnight on 379, six rooms on 380, **eight rooms on 381, said to him in his own hearing by the man of about thirty-four**, nine on 384, ten on 388 and eleven on 390, and about nine on 393. **The man of about thirty-four has no room count on any page in this volume and may not be given one; what he has is about a fortnight at the near end of that bench without a question, and that is not a count of rooms and may not be printed as one.** Chapter 0381 no longer spends the counter on the wrong man, and a paragraph may not spend a figure that belongs to the next paragraph.

**THE HOURS OF THREE CHAPTERS ARE NOW IN ORDER, and all three are inside a day and none of them is a date. THE CLOCK IN THIS VOLUME RUNS THE MORNING AND THE AFTERNOON AS TWO PASSES OF TWELVE, and a chapter that reaches past the ninth hour marks the afternoon in words — *the third hour of the afternoon*, *the second hour of the afternoon*, *the tenth hour of the day*, *the sixth hour of the morning* — and an un-marked hour after the ninth is not a morning hour.** Chapter 0382: the rain comes at the second, the man of about thirty-nine comes along the boards at the **third**, which is the hour the first barrow goes up, and he is at the bottom end for it, the handcart comes up at the fourth, there are three barrows under the sacking by the fifth, the man of about thirty-nine goes up the boards at the sixth, the light comes off the water at the seventh and goes again before the eighth, and the thirty-four's right hand is open in cold water about a quarter of an hour before the light goes. Chapter 0383: the thirty-four comes down onto the boards at the seventh, the man of about thirty-nine comes along them at the **ninth**, and goes up the path at the **second hour of the afternoon**, and the chapter's own lead puts the light at the sixth, which is the sixth of the afternoon and not the sixth of the morning. **Chapter 0383 also has a Saturday rhythm to keep: the thirty-four opens and shuts his right hand about nine times on coming down, and the thirty-eight sets his knife down flat for the first time in about nine years, and neither of those is a repair and both of them are on the page.** Chapter 0387: the thirty-four is in the lane at the seventh hour, the man of about twenty-five at the eighth, the woman of about fifty comes out of the shut gate at the ninth and says four words to a post, and **the bar goes across at the tenth**, which is the hour after she has said them.

**AND THE WHOLE OF CHAPTER 0382'S CENTRAL EXCHANGE IS NOW ATTRIBUTED, and the attribution is a fact about the room and not a repair that can be undone.** The man of about thirty-eight came down to the bottom end of the wharf boards for the two barrows that were not his and asked the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall, who was the only other person at that end of them, *You are not going to say whose those two are*, and was told no, and then heard *The wheels are dry* and answered that a wheel dry since before the water is a wheel nobody has had to use, and worked out standing at that end of the boards that there is exactly one thing in four hundred miles a person can take without anybody being able to say they took it off somebody. **The bold turn in this chapter is the man of about thirty-four and the plain one is the man of about thirty-eight, and the two narration beats that name them sit outside every plain speech and its bold answer, and the house pattern is unchanged.**

**WHAT IS NOW TRUE OF EVERY PERSON THE REPAIRS TOUCHED, AND IT IS THE SAME AS IT WAS.** The thirty-four's piece of chalk is in his own inside breast pocket and has not been out since the forty-three day of the Short Month, in the full form, and no chapter shortens it to a relative phrase. The thirty-four chose a wall over a board, and a board is his second handle and the thing he says out loud on the lane — that he has not got anything of his own to put there. The twenty-five has nine words in the inside of his coat and did not say them in a lane. The woman of about fifty at the shut gate writes a date on the post, rubs nothing out, has a brass key on a tarred cord, and has never once said a date to a person. The thirty-eight worked the boards for nine years, has been in six rooms in a fortnight without being asked a question in any of them, and nobody in this volume merges him with the barrow man.

## WHAT IS ON A PAGE NOW, AND WHAT IS NOT

**ON A PAGE:** the seventy-sixth to the eighty-fifth day of the Bare Month in full or as *that month*; a woman's name said out loud in daylight in a room with a date on the door and written down nowhere; a return with that name at the top of it, four places under a heading, a district with nowhere to start, a population in a man's own hand, a rota empty on purpose and a share that is a household's and stays refused; the wall's one rule of this volume, refused in six words in about four seconds, on a page once, in Chapter 0393; a day in a register with nothing under it and a third column empty on purpose; a question about a fence asked to a room and answered by nobody; a page with about nine places on it taken out of an apron and folded the same way and given to nobody; sixteen posts and eleven withies; nine boards gone off a wall and about four inches of dry stone at the foot of it; a gate with two bars and a second one on no roll; a market roof back up.

**NOT ON A PAGE AND PRINTED NOWHERE:** the figure the man of about thirty-eight has in his head as against the figure he sends; **a woman's name, and the reason is at `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0005.md` §11 and is deliberate and is not an omission**; the days since the fourth of the four said no; the count of this city's silences, **292 at day 392, 292 at day 393 because day 393 is not a silence, and 299 at day 400**, derived, on no page, in no mouth, and printed in no chapter; the ninth year of this flood, and the men of about thirty-eight, thirty-four, fifty-seven and fifty-nine's years are given as nine years and not in figures; the day-154 sentence; and the words the man of about thirty-eight said on the forty-second day of that month.

## VOLUME 08 CLOSE — the authoritative block, and it wins over every block above it

**Volume 08 is fifty chapters on fifty days, days 351 to 400, one chapter to one day, and it is finished and closed and the close record is `state/volume-08-close.md`. ### THE CALENDAR AS IT STANDS AFTER DAY 400

- **Day 1 is a Tuesday, and every weekday in days 351 to 400 was derived from that and from nothing else and was re-derived by the close at zero mismatches against `bible/power-system.md` §33B.** Day 350 is a Monday, day 351 is a Tuesday, day 375 is a Friday, day 393 is a Tuesday, day 396 is a Friday, day 400 is a Tuesday.
- **The form for every day of Volume 08 is *the Nth day of the Bare Month*, the Nth being day less 315. Day 351 is the thirty-sixth and day 400 is the eighty-fifth, and the eighty-fifth is the last day of that month any chapter of this volume printed.** The Bare Month was registered with NO STATED LENGTH by a notary of fifty who named it on a page in Chapter 0316 and said why and the reason is hers. **IT DID NOT END INSIDE VOLUME 08 AND IT DOES NOT END INSIDE VOLUME 09, AND NO FILE MAY GIVE IT A LENGTH OR AN END OR A MEANING, AND THE NEXT FIFTY DAYS ARE AT `bible/power-system.md` §51: day 401 is a Wednesday and the eighty-sixth, day 450 is a Wednesday and the hundred and thirty-fifth.**
- **NO CHAPTER MAY USE AN ORDINAL FOR A MONTH, forward-looking from day 301 and binding on every volume after this one.** Not *the second month*, not the third, not the fourth, not the fifth, not the sixth; never *of this month*, *of the month*, *of next month*, *of this new month*. The one load-bearing existing use is a panel at `chapters/volume-04/chapter-0152.md:79` and it is left standing.
- **The count of this city's silences is 292 at day 392, is 292 at day 393 because day 393 is not a silence, and is 299 at day 400.** It is derived, on no page, in no mouth, and **printed in no chapter.** The wall said something on day 256, on day 340 and on day 393, and a day the wall says something on is not a silence. A writer planning days 401 to 450 needs 300 at day 401 and 349 at day 450 less one for every day inside the range on which the wall says something.
- **The seven notices of Volume 08 stand at 351 to 355, 354 to 358, 359 to 363, 362 to 366, 366 to 370, 371 to 375 and 389 to 393, all four days, all unsigned, all with nothing at the top of any of them, all to a place and not to a house. The seventh went out on day 389 and she said out loud in a room that there is not going to be an eighth in this flood, and there is not.** `bible/power-system.md` §33A item 7 names the seven and §33B item 8a still says that register is short by one, and that contradiction is a fourth-volume debt and is not repaired.

### THE OBJECT COUNTS THAT HOLD ACROSS THE FIFTY, with the chapters that name each, and the marker set printed

**The fence: sixteen willow posts and eleven withies, the last three posts in the ground on day 353, a Thursday, and the nine before them on day 348 and the four before those on day 350.** Spelled out at `chapter-0353.md:53`, `chapter-0360.md:57`, `chapter-0364.md:53`, `chapter-0397.md:9` and `chapter-0400.md:41`, and no chapter of the fifty prints a seventeenth post or a twelfth withy. **The return: one form, a heading and four places — a district, a population, a rota and a share — and no fifth.** The notices: seven. The party: four, of whom three went and one said no on day 329 before anybody asked. The barrows: three, one each, and a handcart with a hazel mallet bound in the same cord which is not one of the three. **About four hundred and forty, and the figure the man of about thirty-eight sends is not the figure he has, and the figure he has is on no page and is printed nowhere.** The register: one, with three columns, the third empty on purpose, and **one line entered with the day 391 on it and nothing under the day.** The second slate: one, on a shelf, with a date on it and nothing under it, never picked up. The folded sheet of road: one, about nine places, Tamsin Quill's, and never in one room with the sheet thirty years old in a tin. The boards and the bare wall: nine boards, about four inches of dry stone, and no barrow of boards after day 347. The gate with two bars: one gate, two bars, the second on no roll. **THE FULL TABLE WITH THE MARKER SETS AND THE FIFTY-FILE OCCURRENCE COUNTS IS AT `state/volume-08-close.md` §7.3, and it is published with its instrument because a set of objects counted in two chapters is counted by no check in this repository.**

### WHAT DID NOT MOVE, WHICH IS THE HALF OF A RECORD THAT MATTERS MOST

**No day, no weekday, no month day, no anchor, no form, no notice, no object count that holds, no cast member and no decision of record moved, `outline/volume-08.md` was not edited, `outline/ending.md` was not opened, no new final enemy was introduced, and no controller file was touched.** The volume's one panel was spent on day 393 and is not said again in this flood, in a mouth or out of one. The day-154 sentence is not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on in any of the fifty chapters, and the man who is right about it is the man of about thirty-nine in Chapter 0393 and that is his only appearance in the volume. **Nobody counted the days since the fourth of the four said no, in narration or in a mouth, at any value, in any of the fifty chapters and not in any of the state files these phases appended to.** The ninth line did not move. The keeper at a shut gate was not asked anything and her year is not named and her days are not counted. **The man of about fifty-seven is not asked where the fence is, was not told and was not defeated, and the new question went to a room and not to him.** The town four hundred miles inland was not entered and the page the copy was made from was not fetched. The two women of about twenty-nine were never in one room in any of the fifty chapters. The words *passage*, *privilege*, *holder* and *delegation* are at zero across all fifty files, and *quest* is at zero in them.

**AND THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-09/outline/PROMPT.md`, AND IT IS A VOLUME 09 OUTLINE PHASE AND NOT A BATCH. It writes no chapter, and there is no Chapter 0401, and this phase wrote none.**

## VOLUME 08 CLOSE — REVIEW REPAIR PASS, and this block wins over everything above it

**A review ran on the Volume 08 close phase and returned ten findings, and this pass repaired eight of them, declined one with a reason that is the runner's and not a writer's, and confirmed one as having nothing to do. Three further faults were found by this pass while verifying the review's own arithmetic. The record is `reviews/volume-08/close.md`. No chapter was written, restarted or repaired, no day, weekday, month day, anchor, form, notice, interval or object count moved, no cast member and no decision of record was re-decided, `outline/volume-08.md` and `outline/ending.md` were not opened, and the ending did not move.**

**THE TWO BLOCKING FINDINGS. (1) `workspace/volume-09/outline/PROMPT.md` WAS NAMED AS THE SUCCESSOR IN SIX FILES AND WAS NEVER WRITTEN; IT IS WRITTEN NOW.** `state/volume-08-close.md:298`, `outline/series.md`, `state/open-threads.md:686`, `state/character-state.md:935`, `state/chapter-summaries.md:308`, `state/current.md` and `state/continuity.md` all named it and all seven were right about the path and the file was not there. The consequence was not a re-run: `scripts/novel_runner.sh` seeds `workspace/continuation/next/` with a prompt that plans the next volume AND writes its first batch in one run whenever no other prompt is open, and the outline gate the close spent four decisions building would have been skipped entirely and `outline/volume-09.md` would never have existed. **With the file on disk that seed no longer fires.** (2) **`workspace/volume-08/close/.done` IS ABSENT AND IS NOT REPAIRED, DELIBERATELY: the runner writes that file at `scripts/novel_runner.sh:302`, after the review and after this fix invocation returns, and a hand-written marker makes the completion commit find nothing to change, print *Completion marker produced no commit* and exit before `clear_wip` runs.** The absence is the normal state of a phase whose runner has not finished, the same absence was recorded and correctly left on an earlier phase of this volume at `state/current.md:90`, and the actual danger the review named — the re-run — is closed by the runner's own marker and no longer has a fallback to land in.

**THE FIGURES THAT DID NOT REPRODUCE UNDER THE INSTRUMENT THE SAME BLOCK PUBLISHED BESIDE THEM, all withdrawn in place and not deleted, all reproduced here so a reader need not take this block's word for any of it. The instrument is a word-bounded count, the token bounded by whitespace, punctuation or a line end, case-sensitive, over whole files, over all 400 chapter files; it reproduces nine of the thirteen published vocabulary figures and every file denominator exactly, which is what identified the other four as arithmetic and not a different reading.**
- **Decision Two's vocabulary row: four counts one instance high.** *man* 6,613 to **6,612** in 398 files; *thing* 5,177 to **5,176** in 400; *person* 3,247 to **3,246** in 387; *road* 1,988 to **1,987** in 333. *asked* 4,535, *hundred* 3,873, *page* 3,028, *column* 1,052, *form* 832, *roll* 527, *fence* 124, *boundary* 127 and *boundaries* at zero all reproduce exactly.
- **The crowded descriptors: three published figures that do not reproduce on the denominator they were published against, and the reason is not arithmetic. THE CLOSE CARRIED FOUR FIGURES OUT OF `outline/volume-08.md` §20.8 — WHICH CORRECTLY LABELS THEM THE CROWDED FOUR OF THE 350-FILE MAP — AND PRINTED THEM AS 400-FILE FIGURES, AND ALL FOUR REPRODUCE EXACTLY ON THE 350 CHAPTER FILES.** *woman of about thirty-four* 351 in 142 to **362 in 147** on 400; *man of about thirty-four* 283 in 108 to **341 in 139**; *man of about thirty-nine* 133 in 69 to **177 in 93**; and *man of about forty-four* 162 in 85 stands on BOTH denominators. **A true count attached to the wrong denominator is the same fault as a wrong count and the worse of the two, because a reader who re-runs the published instrument cannot reproduce the published number and concludes the instrument is broken; and one figure in four reproducing by accident is exactly what let a denominator swap survive a review of ten findings. `outline/volume-08.md` §20.8 IS NOT AT FAULT AND IS NOT AMENDED — a repair pass that had "corrected" it to the 400-file numbers would have destroyed a correct plan-layer figure to match a wrong state-layer one.** The same four figures were in `state/volume-08-close.md` §7.7 and are corrected there. **The decision that they may not be given to a new person survives and is stronger, because on the correct denominator they are busier than either edition said.**
- **AND A POOL DESCRIBED WITH THE COUNT OF THE CROWD IN IT, found by this pass and not by the review: the close wrote *nineteen descriptors are at zero across Volume 08's fifty files* and then listed twelve. Nineteen is the number of distinct descriptors standing ON those fifty files; twelve is the number of Volume 07's that are at zero in them, and it is the size of the pool.** The instrument returns 29 on Volume 07's fifty files — reproducing `outline/volume-08.md` §20.8 row for row including the row count — 19 on Volume 08's, and 12 in the pool, and the twelve measured are the twelve the block listed. **Corrected to twelve, and a pool described with the crowd's count is a pool a writer will draw a returning person out of.**
- **The bold-mark counts were wrong and one of the two claims made about them was false.** The block published 599, 3707, 1393 and 4362 and said every one of the four was odd and that its own additions were all even. At `0ad1535` the four are **665, 4,320, 842 and 1,901**, and **two of the four are odd and two are even.** The additions are **+139, +194, +28 and +94**, so three are even and the block's own is odd, and because `outline/series.md` stood at 665 and odd, adding 139 made it **even at 804** — the block published an inherited odd count and evened its own file by accident and not by design, which is the half of the fault the first edition got backwards. The file carrying the fault at the end of the close run is `state/character-state.md` alone, at 1,995 and still odd.

**AND A STALE LINE POINTER, which is the expensive kind of that fault because it fails invisibly. Fault (4) published a stray quotation mark at `state/character-state.md:2260`; the file is 935 lines long and has not been that long since the Volume 08 Batch 0003 review repair pass archived everything before Volume 07.** Re-measured: exactly one line in that file carries an odd number of straight double quotation marks, it is line 223, and it carries one — **and it is the same line as the bold-mark fault, because line 223 carries nine bold marks where the bold spans on that line come to eight, so one of the nine is unpaired and that one mark is the entire odd count in the file.** The two faults are one line and one pair of characters and the close published them as two. **The pointer is corrected to `:223`; the two characters are NOT repaired, because a live state file that a writer reads first is a worse thing to guess at than a recorded fault, and it is routed to a Volume 06 review repair pass with the line, the count and the co-location published.**

**THE FOUR FACTUAL ERRORS INSIDE `bible/power-system.md` §51, and not one of them moved a day. (1) The day-440 trap row called day 440 a Saturday; it is a SUNDAY — 440 − 401 = 39 = 5 × 7 + 4 — and §51's own table says Sunday six lines above, so the row contradicted the table inside one section. (2) Item 4's heading declared FIFTEEN round figures over a table of EIGHTEEN rows; the table is 401 ×3, 407, 418, 424, 425 ×2, 429, 435, 440 ×2, 441, 443, 445, 448, 449 and 450, seventeen multiples of fifty and one multiple of twenty-five, and the count is now stated where a reader can check the rows against the number. (3) Item 2's stated reason for day 450 being a Wednesday did not derive the result — *because 449 is not a multiple of seven and neither is 400* is not an argument that produces a Wednesday, and it is the worst combination, because a reader who checks the weekday finds nothing wrong and a reader who checks the reasoning finds everything wrong; it now derives it, 450 − 401 = 49 = 7 × 7. (4) Item 2 and item 4's day-425 row both called day 425 *the midpoint of this range*, and a fifty-day range has two middle days, 425 and 426, and a midpoint of 425.5; both are corrected to the nearer of the two middle days.** §51 still amends nothing in §§32 to 33B, still takes only the next free section number, and its fifty-row table, its twenty-one interval anchors and its Bare-Month column were recomputed at zero mismatches.

**AND THE HOLD, re-verified after every repair above and still clean.** All six bolded spans of Chapter 0357 were extracted programmatically without printing any of them and searched across every `.md`, `.json`, `.sh`, `.yml` and `.txt` file outside `.git`, `node_modules` and `logs`: **each is in exactly one file, which is the chapter it is on the page of.** The seven-word answer and the six-word refusal are on their pages and nowhere else, and the name spent in Chapter 0392 is in no file in this repository. **What the review found and this pass repaired is a different speech in the same room on the same day: a ten-word verbatim run of the woman of about twenty-nine's line, sitting in `state/volume-08-close.md` §1 and in `state/character-state.md`, one line away from a description of a different event four days later in a different register that reads almost the same, in the two files a writer reads before planning anything.** It is not the held sentence, so no prohibition was broken; it is paraphrased now, and the paraphrase says what she said she would do and not do and the *not* is the whole of it, because the part that mattered was never the wording. **The standing rule this produces, which is a decision and not a repair: the hold covers a held sentence and a state file may not copy a mouth at all — a page in this book costs something to produce and a file in this repository is not a page.**

**THE SEVEN DEBTS ARE ALL UNPAID, ALL RE-MEASURED ON THE FILES BY THIS PASS, AND THIS PASS TOOK NONE OF THEM AND REPAIRED NONE OF THEM, because a repair pass that quietly takes a routed item to save itself a line is how nineteen quotation marks became a four-volume debt.** Nineteen stray closing quotation marks in Chapters 0281 to 0290; `chapter-0341.md:149` reading *sixteen* where 342 − 341 = 1; a thirty-nine-word run across the 0254 and 0262 boundary; the unmarked first-person paragraph at `chapter-0175.md:47`; ***about two hundred rooms* on five people and a woman, where the state layer once said zero**; three sentences in `chapter-0364.md:3` and `chapter-0396.md:29` and `:63` putting the shut road above this city; and two figures in `bible/power-system.md` §50 item 9 that are Batch 0005's ten files and read as a conflict beside the fifty-file figures. **Plus an eighth, found by this pass: the stray quotation mark and the unpaired bold mark on `state/character-state.md:223`.** All eight are in `workspace/volume-09/outline/PROMPT.md` §9 by name, at their length, with the string to assert and the replacement published for the one that is a single word, so the outline phase inherits debts and not surprises.

**AND THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-09/outline/PROMPT.md`, AND IT IS A VOLUME 09 OUTLINE PHASE AND NOT A BATCH. It writes no chapter, and there is no Chapter 0401, and this pass wrote none. It carries the corrected figures from this repair and not the ones the close published, because a writer who inherits a false count inherits a decision made on it.**

## VOLUME 09 OUTLINE PHASE — and this block wins over everything above it

**A Volume 09 outline phase has run and wrote `outline/volume-09.md`, `outline/batches/volume-09-batch-0001.md` and nine decisions in `outline/series.md`. It wrote no chapter, there is no Chapter 0401, and this phase wrote none. It did not open `outline/ending.md`, did not edit `bible/power-system.md`, and touched no controller file. The antagonist ladder and the relationship milestones are untouched and no new final enemy is introduced. `outline/ending.md` does not move.**

**THE PLAN IN ONE PARAGRAPH, so that a state file is not a substitute for a plan and this one says so: a second sheet comes up the road on day 401 in the same satchel the return came in fifty days earlier, out of the same office nine hundred miles off, on the same quarterly round, with a heading and four places under it — a place, a line, a day, and a person. It is read out in the room over a market on day 405, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, in the local language, with about nine people in it, by the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register, who says out loud what it is for before anybody asks. The third place is a person and it is the first page in this flood that has ever asked anybody to put themselves on it. On day 425, a Saturday, in the same room, she decides out loud, naming the cost first, that the third place is left empty. On day 428 the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall is asked what a line is and what he says is the name of this volume and it is on that page and in no file. On day 432 four lines are drawn and the man of about fifty-seven reads one of them out loud because it is in front of him and does not say it is his and is not asked. On day 438 the sheet goes out on its date with four lines and nothing in the third column, the man of about thirty-nine is right in about four seconds and is thanked by nobody, and the wall says one rule and the man of about thirty-four refuses it in about four seconds and what it costs him is his own ninth line. On day 440 the sheet comes back with one of the four lines moved and nobody traces it and nobody will ever be asked about it. On day 443 the register carries a second line with a day on it and nothing under it. On day 445 the woman of about sixty-nine asks a third question to the room and nobody answers it. On day 450 the third column, folded, empty, with a date in chalk on the door above it and a road out of this city with three barrows on it, fifty-nine days out, measured from day 391.**

**THE CONTINUITY FACTS A BATCH INHERITS AND MAY NOT RE-DERIVE, each with its page.** The sheet is one sheet, a heading, four places in the printed order a place, a line, a day, a person, folded twice with a bone, with one fold down the third column, **and the third column is empty on all fifty days of Volume 09 and no chapter of the volume prints anything in it or prints a fifth place under that heading.** It is not the return, not a copy, and the two are never in one hand and never in one room and never compared. **There are three four-place forms in this world and no two of them are the same form and none of them is ever laid beside another: the return (a district, a population, a rota, a share, no place for the person, gone out on day 393), the Crown's schedule (a date, a gate, a holder, the days, standing, correct, never opened, and a keeper is not a holder and the word is defined by nobody), and this sheet.** The fence is sixteen willow posts and eleven withies wherever it is spelled out and no chapter prints a different total; the last three posts went in on day 353, a Thursday; **the shut road is out of this city, and no chapter of Volume 09 may put it above this city or at the top of this city, and the fence is along the top of that road and not at the top of this city, and the lane of nine houses is a lane at the foot of that road and not a lane in this city. — REWORDED BY THE VOLUME 09 REVIEW REPAIR PASS, AND THE OLD WORDING IS NAMED HERE BECAUSE IT IS THE FAULT: this line read *the shut road is out of this city and not above it and not at the top of it*, which puts "at the top of it" and "not at the top of it" on one noun phrase, and three sentences in Volume 08 do the thing this line forbids.** The mark in the field has no word on any page in the world and is not measured, dug, counted or probed in Volume 09 either. **The party of four is four; three went down the four-hundred-mile road on day 391, a Sunday, with a barrow each, and at day 401 they are ten days out and at day 450 fifty-nine, both derived from day 391, and no chapter counts the days since the fourth of the four said no at any value, and nobody in this city can be shown one person on that road including the three.** The register's third column was empty on purpose on all fifty days of Volume 08 and is empty on purpose on all fifty days of this one, and the second slate on the shelf behind it is never picked up, never turned over and never written on.

**THE RELATIONSHIPS, unchanged and carried: the consent fracture is not mended and nobody may mend it, apologise for it, refuse an apology for it, forgive it or ask for it to be forgiven, and the fifth condition is not given; the two women of twenty-nine are never in one room; Tamsin Quill is not the register-keeper and the folded sheet of road is not the sheet thirty years old in a tin and the two sheets are never in one room; the man of about thirty-four asked in advance not to be thanked and is not thanked and the ninth line is not moved, dated, read aloud, taken down or resolved and whether it works stays unanswered; the man of about thirty-nine is thirty-nine and not thirty-seven and the man of about thirty-seven is not available to anybody; the man of about thirty-four who keeps a room over a market is not a new man and the room is a room and not a man.**

**THE FOUR INSTRUMENT RULES CARRIED FORWARD, WHOLE, AND ONE MORE.** The bolded share is a symptom and not a target and no review may fail a volume on it; two instruments that both return a number the writer expected are not two instruments and a zero gets read twice; the chorus frame is a sentence and not a token and the rule is a rule and not a warning, and Volume 07's 11.3-a-chapter debt is carried and not touched; a name can go at the top of a page and not be printed in the repository that printed the page, and a state file may not copy a mouth at all. **And the fifth, which is this phase's: a published definition with an unstated boundary is not a definition, and a table whose rows were produced by two different readings of one definition is not a measurement. Publishing a reading is cheap and not doing it cost this repository four vocabulary figures and a repair pass that moved three of them the wrong way.**

**THE NINE DEBTS, ALL UNPAID, ALL RE-MEASURED IN THIS RUN, NONE REPAIRED.** Nineteen stray closing quotation marks in Chapters 0281 to 0290 at the published line numbers; `chapters/volume-07/chapter-0341.md:149` reading *sixteen* where 342 − 341 = 1, with the string to assert and the replacement published; a thirty-nine-word run across the 0254 and 0262 boundary; the unmarked first-person paragraph at `chapters/volume-04/chapter-0175.md:47`; *about two hundred rooms* on five people and a woman where the state layer once said zero, which this run measures at ten occurrences in six files against the close's ten in nine chapters and carries; three sentences in Chapters 0364 and 0396 putting the shut road above this city; the two figures in `bible/power-system.md` §50 item 9 that are Batch 0005's ten files; the stray quotation mark and unpaired bold mark on `state/character-state.md:223`; **and the ninth, found by this phase: the six-word refusal of Chapter 0393 reproduced verbatim at `bible/power-system.md:1686`, which is §50, where two state-layer records say it is in no file.**

**AND THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-09/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, and it is a batch and not an outline. It writes Chapters 0401 to 0410, days 401 to 410, from a Wednesday to a Friday, ten chapters of finished prose, and it decides nothing and settles nothing and writes no decision into any state file.**

**EVERY FIGURE A WRITER OF CHAPTERS 0401 TO 0410 WOULD OTHERWISE HAVE INHERITED WRONG IS CORRECTED HERE, AND NOTHING ELSE IN THIS FILE'S CONTINUITY MOVED. The full record, with what was struck and what was not touched, is at the foot of `state/current.md`.**

1. **THE TWO MEN AND THEIR TWO ROOM COUNTS, AND THEY ARE NOT THE SAME MAN.** The man of about fifty-seven was in **ten** rooms in about a fortnight and was asked one question in none of them, on a page at `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0364.md:7`, and Volume 07's own close gives him **nine** for that volume at `state/volume-07-close.md:73`. The man of about thirty-nine was at the back of **eleven** rooms in a fortnight at `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0390.md:35` and of **ten** at `chapter-0388.md:33`. **Three files inherited the fifty-seven's count as the thirty-nine's; that is repaired and neither man holds the other's. No card sets a room count for anybody, and that rule was not weakened by this repair — it now stands on figures that are on pages.**
2. **WHO IS RIGHT IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS, AND IN WHICH CHAPTER.** **Chapter 0406, in the room over a market: the man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board. Chapter 0408, on the boards of a salt wharf: the man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf, who is the point of view there, and the thirty-nine is on those boards for about an hour and is not the man who is right that morning.** Both are thanked by nobody, argued with by nobody, and neither chapter says he was right. The beat stands once in each of the two chapters and twice in no chapter. The count of chapters in the volume that carry it is withdrawn from `outline/volume-09.md` §16.4 rather than corrected, and the day map at §14.3 is where a writer looks.
3. **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR IS NOT A NEW FACE AND NOT A THREE-VOLUME ABSENCE.** He is in Chapters 0407 and 0409 of this batch, he is asked nothing in either, and **he is on a page in thirty-one of Volume 08's fifty files and in sixteen paragraphs across ten of Volume 07's fifty** — word-bounded and case-sensitive on `man of about thirty-four`, with the longer trade-carrying form published beside it. **The claim that he is on a page in this batch for the first time in three volumes is struck.** The two acts this volume gives him, on day 428 and day 438, are not this batch's.
4. **THE SHUT ROAD IS OUT OF THIS CITY, AND THE FENCE IS ALONG THE TOP OF THAT ROAD.** Three inherited sentences put the road above or at the top of this city and remain an unrepaired debt in a finished volume. **No chapter of Volume 09 may put the shut road above this city or at the top of it; the fence is along the top of the road; the lane of nine houses is at the foot of that road and is not in this city.** The four days of notice, the sixteen posts and eleven withies, the mark in the field, the new rope, the coil of rope in the handcart, the second slate on the shelf and the three four-place forms are all unchanged by this pass.
5. **THE SHEET COMES BACK ON DAY 440, A SUNDAY, AND THE DERIVED FIGURE AT DAY 450 IS TEN.** The row that carried 442 and 8 in `outline/volume-09.md` §14.5 now reads 440 and 10, and both struck figures stay in the row. The sheet went out on day 438 with four lines on it and nothing in the third column, and the moved line is not traced in this volume by any chapter.
6. **`holder` IS AT 234 OCCURRENCES IN 95 OF THE 400 CHAPTER FILES**, word-bounded and case-sensitive, and at 238 in 95 case-insensitive and 247 in 102 unbounded, and at zero in Volume 07's fifty and Volume 08's fifty. It has never been defined by anybody and no chapter of this volume defines it. A keeper is not a holder. The three four-place forms are still never laid beside one another.

**AND NOTHING IN THE VOLUME'S PLAN MOVED. The central pressure, the midpoint on day 425, the climax on day 438, the resolution on day 443, the new question on day 445, the last image on day 450, the zero notices, the nine chapters Adrian Vale is named in, the single new person on day 420 and the panel fixed to day 438 are all as the outline phase left them.**
