# VOLUME 09, BATCH 0001 — the continuity facts a later batch inherits, and this block wins over every block above it

**Ten chapters were written, days 401 to 410, one chapter to one day, no double day, the chapter number is the day number. Everything below is on a page in `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0401.md` through `chapter-0410.md` and in no other chapter and in no file but these four and the batch record, except where a page carries it and the page is named.**

1. **THE SHEET. One sheet, a heading, four places ruled under the heading, folded twice with a bone, with one crease pressed with a bone down the third column, and the third column empty on all ten days and on all fifty days of the volume.** **THE PRINTED ORDER OF THE FOUR IS A PLACE, A LINE, A PERSON, AND A DAY, AND THE THIRD OF THE FOUR IS A PERSON.** The plan's own enumeration lists them as *a place, a line, a day, and a person* while stating in nine places that the third is a person; the enumeration is a list of the four things the form has and not of their printed order, the printed order is as set out here, and the contradiction in the plan is published in `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0001.md` §3.11 and in `state/current.md`'s newest block and is not resolved in the plan. **It is not the return, not a copy, and the two are never in one hand, never in one room and never compared. No chapter of the volume prints a fifth place under that heading and no chapter prints anything in the third column.** It was put on a table on day 401 by the man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living, who did not open it; it was read out on day 405 by the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register, in the local language, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, in front of about nine people, in the order printed, stopping at each place long enough for the people at the back to hear it. It is not filled in, not sent, has not gone back anywhere and has not come back.

2. **THE FOURTH PLACE ON THE PAGE IS A DAY AND IT IS EMPTY TOO.** All four places were empty when the sheet came up the road and all four were empty when the reading finished, and the crease pressed down the third of them is the only thing on that page anybody in this city has ever touched. **A crease is not an entry. The crease was not undone in any of the ten chapters.**

3. **THE NOTICES OF THIS FLOOD ARE SEVEN AND THERE IS NO EIGHTH, AND THE TEN CHAPTERS WROTE NONE.** Every room in the ten is a room about nine people were already in, and the day on the door was still written in chalk. **The four days are the cooling, and the cooling is the time a room has in which to find out what it is being asked; that sentence is on a page once, in Chapter 0401, in plain narration and in no mouth, and the word *cooling* stands three times in the batch and all three are in that chapter.** The page that carries the fact is `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0389.md:13`. Two other chapters refer to the same absence in other words and use neither the sentence nor the word: Chapter 0405 says the sheet was read out on the morning it came up the road because there was nothing to be read out on any other morning, and Chapter 0409 says a room can get used to a thing it is not going to be told.

4. **THE SECOND SLATE IS NEVER PICKED UP, NEVER TURNED OVER AND NEVER WRITTEN ON, AND A CHAPTER THAT READS IT READS IT WHERE IT LIES.** It is named in five of the ten chapters and read in two of them, in Chapter 0402 and in Chapter 0405, and in both Adrian Vale is at the shelf in front of it and in neither does a hand go near it. It carries a date and nothing under the date. **The word that was on it once is not on any of the ten pages and the common noun is not used of anybody in any of the ten.**

5. **THE REGISTER'S THIRD COLUMN HAS BEEN EMPTY ON PURPOSE FOR FIFTY DAYS AND NO CHAPTER OF THE TEN PUTS ANYTHING IN IT, AND THE REGISTER-KEEPER IS THE ONE WHO HAS KEPT IT EMPTY AND SAID SO OUT LOUD IN THAT ROOM ONCE AND HAS NEVER BEEN ASKED ABOUT IT.** The register carries one line with a day in the first column and nothing under the day, and the second column is empty, and **no chapter of the ten entered a line in that book, and the line entered on day 443 is a later chapter's.** **THE FIGURE WAS PRINTED AS SIXTY DAYS IN THE FIRST EDITION OF THIS ITEM AND IT IS FIFTY, and fifty is what four of the ten chapters say on a page and what the Volume 09 outline phase's own block in `state/character-state.md` says, and a page outranks a state file and the state file was the thing that was wrong.**

6. **THE CHALK IS IN HIS OWN INSIDE BREAST POCKET AND HAS NOT BEEN TAKEN OUT SINCE THE FORTY-THIRD DAY OF THE SHORT MONTH, WHICH IS DAY TWO HUNDRED AND NINETY-EIGHT.** **The full form of that day is printed at full length in Chapter 0407, in the chapter lead and again in his mouth, and Chapter 0409 names the chalk with no date at all rather than shortening the date to a relative phrase, and there is no *the last week of that month* in any of the ten.** The chalk is named in eight of the ten chapters. He is not thanked in any of the three chapters he speaks in and he asked in advance, in a lane, not to be thanked.

7. **THE FENCE IS SIXTEEN WILLOW POSTS AND ELEVEN WITHIES AND NO CHAPTER OF THE TEN PRINTS A DIFFERENT TOTAL.** The count is printed once, in Chapter 0410, in this form: nine of the posts went into the ground on a Saturday, which is the three hundred and forty-eighth day of this flood; four more went in on the Monday after that, which is the three hundred and fiftieth day, and that made thirteen; the last three went in on the Thursday after that, which is the three hundred and fifty-third day. **A Bare Month ordinal was not used for any of those three days and was not available, since §51 item 1 puts the volume's range at the eighty-first day and after, and the day of this flood is the instrument that was used instead.** There has been no seventeenth post and nobody has said there is going to be one. **About four feet is a number a man of about sixty has in his mouth and is not a measurement; it is printed once in the batch, in Chapter 0410, and it is not in the object inventory. About four hundred paces between the fence and a mark in a field is printed once, in Chapter 0410.**

8. **THE SHUT ROAD IS OUT OF THIS CITY, THE FENCE IS ALONG THE TOP OF THAT ROAD, AND THE LANE OF NINE HOUSES IS AT THE FOOT OF THAT ROAD AND IS NOT IN THIS CITY.** Chapter 0410 says all three of those things in its own words at the end of the chapter. **No chapter of the ten puts the shut road above this city or at the top of it.** The mark in the field has no word on any page in the world and is not measured, dug, counted, probed or resolved in any of the ten, and no chapter goes and looks at it, and no chapter joins it to the chapel, to the boards under the chapel or to the two holes nine feet apart.

9. **THE THREE ON THE FOUR-HUNDRED-MILE ROAD. Three went down it with a barrow each and the figure printed for them in this batch is TEN DAYS, and it is printed once, in Chapter 0410, and day 391 is named in the same sentence.** **Nobody in this city can be shown one person on that road, including the three, and a party of four is four, and the fourth of the four is not on that road and is not going to be asked about anything by anybody, and no chapter of the ten counts the days since she said no at any value.** Chapter 0408 names the three barrows and prints no figure. The town four hundred miles inland is not entered, described from inside, or brought anybody back from, in any of the ten.

10. **THE PARTY OF FOUR, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR, THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN, THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE AND THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE — every decision `outline/volume-09.md` §16 carries for them held in all ten chapters and none of them moved.** **The two men of about thirty-eight are two people and were not merged: the man at the salt wharf is in Chapters 0403 and 0408 and the man who came up a lane with a barrow of boards on day 347 is in neither of them and is nowhere in this batch.** The man of about thirty-four is thirty-four and the man of about thirty-nine is thirty-nine and the man of about thirty-seven is at zero. **The figure the man of about thirty-eight sends is not the figure he has, and NEITHER IS PRINTED IN ANY OF THE TEN: Chapter 0408 states the difference in words and prints no number, and the object inventory's chapter list for that figure is departed from deliberately and the reason is in the batch record §4.** The man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living has carried seven things with somebody else's writing on them in this flood — five in a coat, a return in a satchel on the three hundred and fifty-first day of this flood, and this sheet — and has opened none of the seven, and the last of them is the last sheet of paper in this flood that has gone past him.

11. **THE FIGURES PRINTED IN THE TEN AND WHERE THEY ARE ANCHORED, so that a later batch does not have to re-derive them and does not have to guess which anchor each one had.** Day 351 named in the sentence that says the return came up the road, in Chapter 0401. Day 224 and day 225 named in Chapter 0407, where the store's board carries the figures of the two hundred and twenty-fourth day and has stood unwritten since the two hundred and twenty-fifth. Day 298 named in Chapters 0405, 0407 and 0409 in the full form of the Short Month day. Days 348, 350 and 353 named in Chapter 0410 against their weekdays. Day 391 named in Chapter 0410 in the sentence that prints the ten. Day 405 named as Sunday in Chapters 0403, 0405, 0406, 0407 and 0409 wherever the reading is referred to. **The eighteen round figures `bible/power-system.md` §51 item 4 names for days 401 to 450 are printed nowhere in the ten, including the three that fall on day 401, and a chapter that prints none of them is not required to mention an anchor it does not use.** The count of this city's silences is derived, on no page, in no mouth, three hundred at day 401, and printed in no chapter.

12. **THE CALENDAR DID NOT MOVE AND THIS BATCH DID NOT MOVE IT.** Day 401 is a Wednesday and the eighty-sixth day of the Bare Month and day 410 is a Friday and the ninety-fifth. **The run was rebuilt from day 1 being a Tuesday and from nothing else before any prose was written, and all ten were checked against the batch prompt's table, and the two agree at ten of ten; the Bare Month numbers are day less 315 in all ten and every one is inside the eighty-first-to-one-hundred-and-thirty-fifth range that §51 item 1 allows.** Days 411 to 420 recompute as Saturday, Sunday, Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, Friday, Saturday, Sunday, Monday, Bare Month 96 to 105, and agree with `outline/volume-09.md` §14.3 and §51 at zero mismatches.

13. **AND THE ONE WEEKDAY THIS BATCH READ AND DID NOT PRINT, so that the next batch does not think there was nothing to check: `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0389.md:3` calls the seventy-fourth day of the Bare Month a Friday, and that day is day 389, **and day 389 is a FRIDAY on the founding rule and not a Saturday, so the chapter is RIGHT and this debt is a false one and a later phase must not repair that page on the strength of it.** The founding run is day 1 a Tuesday, and (389 − 1) mod 7 = 388 mod 7 = 3, and index 3 on that run is Friday. A first edition of this entry said Saturday, and the same entry was carried forward twice, and a routed debt that is not a debt is the one instrument failure this repository cannot catch downstream: it sends a later phase into a finished volume to break a page that is already right.** It is a page in a finished volume and it was neither repaired nor restated. Chapter 0401 refers to that morning three times and names no weekday in any of the three.

**AND THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-09/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, A BATCH AND NOT AN OUTLINE, AND IT WRITES CHAPTERS 0411 TO 0420, DAYS 411 TO 420, FROM A SATURDAY TO A MONDAY.**

# VOLUME 09, BATCH 0001 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS — the continuity facts as they stand, and this block wins over every block above it

**Eleven faults were found in Batch 0001 and corrected, nine in its record and two on its pages, and the full list with the old figure beside every new one is at `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0001.md` §11 and the account of it is at `state/current.md`'s newest block. Three of the eleven change what a later batch inherits and the three are set out here. Nothing else in the block above moved, and no debt was taken and none was rerouted.**

1. **THE DAY THE RETURN CAME UP THE ROAD IS NAMED CORRECTLY ON THE PAGE NOW, and it is the only continuity fact this pass altered.** Chapter 0401 printed *the fifty-first day of this flood* where it means the three hundred and fifty-first, in the sentence that says the return came in a satchel and in the sentence that counts the seven things the carrier has carried. **Both now read *the three hundred and fifty-first day of this flood*, so `bible/power-system.md` §51 item 4 is satisfied on a page and §52 item 1's claim that the chapter names day 351 in that sentence is true, and item 10 of the block above is true because of it.** The carrier's seven things are unchanged: five in a coat, that return, and this sheet, and none of the seven opened, and the last of them the last sheet of paper in this flood that has gone past him.
2. **THE REGISTER'S THIRD COLUMN HAS BEEN EMPTY ON PURPOSE FOR FIFTY DAYS, not sixty.** The block above said sixty; four of the ten chapters say fifty on a page and the Volume 09 outline phase said fifty in `state/character-state.md`. **Fifty is the figure, it is corrected in place above, and the state file was the thing that was wrong.** The rest of that item stands: the register carries one line with a day in the first column and nothing under the day, the second column is empty, no chapter of the ten entered a line in that book, and the line entered on day 443 is a later chapter's.
3. **THE THREE FIGURES A LATER BATCH WAS GOING TO INHERIT FROM THIS ONE'S MEASUREMENTS ARE CORRECTED, and the measurements are not continuity but a batch that hands a next batch a wrong number hands it a wrong number.** The sentences of forty words or more in the ten are **179** on the definition published beside the figure and 166 on the reading that joins a sentence across a blank line, and neither is duplicated inside the batch or across the four hundred. The longest of them is **151 words in Chapter 0410, in its lead**. The longest word-run inside the batch is **42**, between Chapters 0403 and 0408, and the longest against the four hundred pre-existing files is **47**, between Chapter 0405 and `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0355.md`, and that one is the room-coda lead and not a lift.

**AND WHAT IS UNCHANGED AND STILL BINDS: the printed order of the four places is a place, a line, a person and a day and the third is a person; the sheet is one sheet and is not the return and the two are never in one hand; the second slate is never picked up, turned over or written on and is read where it lies in Chapters 0402 and 0405; there is no eighth notice and these ten wrote none and the four days are the cooling and that sentence is on a page once, in Chapter 0401, in plain narration and in no mouth, and the word *cooling* stands three times in the batch and all three are in that chapter; the fence is sixteen willow posts and eleven withies and no chapter prints a different total; the shut road is out of this city and the fence is along the top of that road and the lane of nine houses is at the foot of it and is not in this city; the man of about fifty-seven is in two of the ten and is asked nothing in either and does not touch the rope; Adrian Vale is in Chapters 0402 and 0405 and in no chapter of the ten in any other form; and all nine inherited debts stand where they stood.**

**AND THE NEXT PHASE IS STILL `workspace/volume-09/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, A BATCH AND NOT AN OUTLINE, AND IT WRITES CHAPTERS 0411 TO 0420, DAYS 411 TO 420, FROM A SATURDAY TO A MONDAY.**

# VOLUME 09, BATCH 0002 — the continuity facts as they stand after days 411 to 420, and this block wins over every block above it

**Ten chapters, days 411 to 420, and every figure in this block is either on a page in `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0411.md` through `chapter-0420.md` or is published in `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0002.md` with the instrument that produced it. Nothing here is derived on no page and in no mouth. The definitions and the counts are at the batch record and are not repeated at a smaller length.**

1. **THE PRINTED ORDER OF THE FOUR PLACES IS A PLACE, A LINE, A PERSON, AND A DAY, AND THE THIRD IS A PERSON, AND THIS BATCH INHERITS THE RESOLUTION AND NOT THE CONTRADICTION.** `outline/volume-09.md` enumerates them as *a place, a line, a day, and a person* and states in nine places that the third is a person. The printed order on the page is place, line, person, day, and Chapters 0402 and 0405 put it that way, and **no chapter of this batch enumerates the four in any other order, prints a fifth place under that heading, or prints anything in the third column on any of its ten days.** The plan is not amended, because a batch may not amend an outline, and the contradiction stands in the plan.

2. **THE SHEET CAME UP THE ROAD ON DAY 401 AND NOTHING HAS BEEN DONE TO IT IN THESE TEN DAYS.** It has a heading and four places ruled under the heading, it was folded twice with a bone, **and Chapter 0413 puts on a page that one of the two creases is the one pressed down the third of the four places and the other is along the top, and that a sheet folded that way was put together by somebody nine hundred miles off so that the third of the four places fell on a fold.** A crease is not an entry. **No hand in any of these ten chapters opened the sheet, folded it, wrote on it or turned it over, and the third of the four places is empty on all ten of these days and the other three are empty too.**

3. **THE NOTICES OF THIS FLOOD ARE SEVEN AND THERE IS NO EIGHTH, AND THESE TEN CHAPTERS WROTE NONE.** Every room in the ten is a room about nine people were already in, and the day on the door was still written in chalk on all ten of them. **What is lost with the notice is not named again in these ten, and the word *cooling* stands at zero across the ten files; the sentence it belongs to is on a page once in this volume, in Chapter 0401, in plain narration and in no mouth.** The page that carries the fact is `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0389.md:13`. Two of these ten chapters refer to the same absence in other words and use neither the sentence nor the word: Chapter 0419's *nobody went for anybody who had the language* and Chapter 0420's *nobody in that room followed her*.

4. **THE SECOND SLATE IS NOT NAMED IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS, IS NEVER PICKED UP, NEVER TURNED OVER AND NEVER WRITTEN ON, AND IS NOT THERE.** It carries a date and nothing under the date, as it has for the whole of this volume. **The one occurrence of the word *slate* in the batch is a reference in Chapter 0411 to the slate the woman of about twenty-nine wrote a day on in Chapter 0356, in a different room in a finished volume, and it is not this slate.** The word that was on the second slate once is not on any of these ten pages and the common noun is not used of anybody in any of them.

5. **THE REGISTER'S THIRD COLUMN HAS BEEN EMPTY ON PURPOSE FOR FIFTY DAYS AND NO CHAPTER OF THESE TEN PUTS ANYTHING IN IT, AND THE REGISTER-KEEPER IS THE ONE WHO HAS KEPT IT EMPTY AND HAS NEVER BEEN ASKED ABOUT IT.** The register carries one line with a day in the first column and nothing under the day, the second column is empty, and **Chapter 0414 is the chapter in which a man reads that book where it lies and finds the margin worn pale in a curve about the width of a thumb beside the third column and not beside the other two, and says one true thing about the paper and stops one step short of it, and nobody in that room asked him for the rest and nobody thanked him.** **No chapter of these ten entered a line in that book, and the line entered on day 443 is a later chapter's.**

6. **THE CHALK IS IN HIS OWN INSIDE BREAST POCKET AND HAS NOT BEEN TAKEN OUT SINCE THE FORTY-THIRD DAY OF THE SHORT MONTH, WHICH IS DAY TWO HUNDRED AND NINETY-EIGHT.** **The full form of that day is printed at full length in Chapters 0413, 0416, 0417 and 0418, in a mouth in three of them and in narration in one, and no chapter shortens it to a relative phrase, and there is no *the last week of that month* in any of the ten.** He is present in seven of the ten and speaks in five, **he is thanked in none of the five and argued with in none of the five**, nobody in these ten asks him what a line is, and nothing in these ten warms up the day he is asked it.

7. **THE FENCE IS SIXTEEN WILLOW POSTS AND ELEVEN WITHIES AND NO CHAPTER OF THESE TEN PRINTS A DIFFERENT TOTAL.** The count is printed **twice** in this batch, in Chapter 0416 and in Chapter 0418, in the same form both times, and it is the only count of the fence printed in the volume so far after Chapter 0410's. **The shut road is out of this city, the fence is along the top of that road, and the lane of nine houses is at the foot of that road and is not in this city**, and the two chapters that name the fence say so in their own words. **The mark in the field has no word on any page in the world and is not named, measured, dug, counted, probed or resolved in any of these ten, and the word *field* stands at zero across the ten files.** About four feet is a number in a man's mouth and is not a measurement and is not printed in any of these ten, and so is about four hundred paces.

8. **THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN IS IN FOUR OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS AND IS ASKED NOTHING IN ANY OF THEM.** Chapters 0411, 0416, 0418 and 0420. **Nobody asks him where the fence is, nobody tells him, he does not move the fence, and he does not touch the rope; the rope is over the back of his chair in three of the four and has still never been used.** In Chapter 0418 he says out loud, unasked, that there is a first thing he could say to anybody who asked him and that he is not going to say it, and **that is a statement that he is not going to say it and is not a hint at what it is, and no chapter of these ten says what the first thing is and no chapter asks him.** He is right about nothing in these ten and is not defeated in any of them.

9. **THE THREE ON THE FOUR-HUNDRED-MILE ROAD ARE NAMED IN CHAPTERS 0412 AND 0418 AND NEITHER CHAPTER PRINTS AN ELAPSED COUNT FOR THEM.** Day 391 is named in the sentence that says they went, in both chapters, and 391 is a Sunday on the founding rule and both chapters say so. **The figure that comes out at exactly twenty on day 411 and exactly twenty-nine on day 420 is printed nowhere in these ten, and the better chapter prints neither.** Nobody in this city can be shown one person on that road, a party of four is four, the fourth of the four is not on that road, and **no chapter of these ten counts the days since she said no at any value, in narration or in a mouth, and a search for *days since*, *weeks since* and *months since* across the ten returns zero.** Chapter 0412 puts the man of about thirty-eight on a wharf with a space at the top of his boards about the length of a barrow, and one of the three barrows on that road is the one that used to stand there; the town four hundred miles inland is not entered, described from inside, or brought anybody back from, in any of the ten.

10. **THE BARROWS ARE THREE, AND THIS BATCH NAMES A FOURTH KIND AND DOES NOT JOIN IT TO ANY OF THEM.** Three barrows went down the four-hundred-mile road, one each, in Chapter 0412. **Chapter 0417 has a barrow standing at the end of a stall in this city with nothing in it and there is nothing in it and there is not going to be anything in it, and it is not one of the three and the marker cannot tell them apart and the collision is published.** The handcart and its coil of rope are not named in any of these ten chapters.

11. **ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED AND FORTY IS ON A PAGE ONCE IN THIS BATCH AND THE OTHER FIGURE IS ON NONE.** Chapter 0414 prints it, in narration attributing it to the man who has put a number into a column four times a year since the winter before the water, on eleven miles of flats. **The figure he has is not printed in any of these ten chapters and the difference between the two figures is not stated in any of them, and no chapter of these ten prints the two as two numbers.** Nobody in these ten asks him what a column is for and no chapter answers it.

12. **THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE IN A VERY GOOD COAT IS IN CHAPTERS 0415 AND 0419 AND HAS NO LOCAL LANGUAGE AND NOBODY HAS ARRANGED FOR IT.** He said it out loud in that room once and said it once on day 415 because nobody had said it for him, **and nobody in either chapter arranged for the language, nobody offered to find somebody who had it, and the language is not mended in this volume.** In Chapter 0419 it is said out loud at the back of a room, in front of him, that nobody in that room has read one word of the page his office sent, and **nobody says it to him and nobody does anything about it and he is not exposed, not converted and not put down.** He did not describe the room in either chapter and has not been in a room in this city in which anything got decided and is not going to be. His document is inside the front of his coat in both and its contents are given to nobody.

13. **AND THE THING THAT IS NEW IN THIS BATCH AND IS A PAGE.** On day 420 a woman of about fifty-four was asked, in a room over a market, in daylight, in the local language, with a date on the door, where her line is, and she answered it correctly. **Her line is a line that is at the second board of her own step and is not the same line twice in the same week, and nobody in that room asked her a second question, nobody thanked her, and the register-keeper said out loud in her own mouth that there is nowhere in four hundred miles to put an answer somebody gave out loud in a room.** **Her second handle is a length of blue thread tied round her own wrist, which is how she has always known a length by, and it is on no page before Chapter 0420 and it is the same in every chapter she is in after it, and she is in no chapter of this volume after that one.** `woman of about fifty-four` stands at four occurrences in Chapter 0420 and at zero in the other nine files of this batch.

14. **THE FOUR PLACES AND THE THING THAT IS NOT IN ANY OF THEM.** The four lines are not drawn in these ten days and are drawn on day 432 by four people, and one of the four reads his out loud because it is in front of him and does not say it is his. The sheet has not gone out of this city. **No chapter of these ten traces a move, investigates one, asks who made one, reports one to a room, guesses at one, or says in any mouth that anybody moved anything, and nothing has moved because nothing has been drawn.**

15. **THE CALENDAR DID NOT MOVE AND THIS BATCH DID NOT MOVE IT.** Day 411 is a Saturday and the ninety-sixth day of the Bare Month and day 420 is a Monday and the hundred and fifth. **The run was rebuilt from day 1 being a Tuesday and from nothing else before any prose was written, and all ten were checked against the prompt's table and the two agree at ten of ten; the Bare Month numbers are day less 315 in all ten and every one is inside the eighty-first-to-one-hundred-and-thirty-fifth range that §51 item 1 allows.** Twenty-four weekday tokens stand across the ten files and every one was read in the sentence it stands in against the day that sentence names, and every one is right. **Days 421 to 430 recompute as Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, Friday, Saturday, Sunday, Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday and Thursday, Bare Month 106 to 115, and agree with `outline/volume-09.md` §14.3 and §51 at zero mismatches.**

16. **AND THE ONE WEEKDAY IN A FINISHED VOLUME THIS BATCH READ AND DID NOT PRINT, so that the next batch does not think there was nothing to check: `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0389.md:3` calls the seventy-fourth day of the Bare Month a Friday, and that day is day 389, **and day 389 is a FRIDAY on the founding rule and not a Saturday, so the chapter is RIGHT and this debt is a false one and a later phase must not repair that page on the strength of it.** The founding run is day 1 a Tuesday, and (389 − 1) mod 7 = 388 mod 7 = 3, and index 3 on that run is Friday. A first edition of this entry said Saturday, and the same entry was carried forward twice, and a routed debt that is not a debt is the one instrument failure this repository cannot catch downstream: it sends a later phase into a finished volume to break a page that is already right.** It is a page in a finished volume and it was neither repaired nor restated, and this batch printed no weekday for that morning at all.

**AND THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-09/batch-0003/PROMPT.md`, A BATCH AND NOT AN OUTLINE, AND IT WRITES CHAPTERS 0421 TO 0430, DAYS 421 TO 430, FROM A TUESDAY TO A THURSDAY.**

# VOLUME 09, BATCH 0002 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS — the continuity facts as they now stand, and this block wins over every block above it

**A review of this batch repaired four things on its pages and corrected three faults in its own record, and every figure this block gives is re-measured on `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0411.md` through `chapter-0420.md` as they now stand, with the instrument published at `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0002.md` §3 and the whole list of what moved at its §13. **Nothing here is derived on no page and in no mouth, and the definitions and the counts are at the batch record and are not repeated here at a smaller length.**

1. **NOTHING THAT BINDS CHANGED, and the list of it is the list in the block above, item for item.** The printed order of the four places is a place, a line, a person and a day and the third is a person, and the contradiction in the plan is not resolved in the plan. The sheet came up the road on day 401 and has not been opened, folded, written on or turned over in any of the ten, and the third of its four places is empty on all ten days. There is no eighth notice and these ten wrote none, and *cooling* is at zero across the ten and the sentence it belongs to is on a page once in this volume, in Chapter 0401, in plain narration and in no mouth. The second slate is not named in any of the ten. The register's third column has been empty on purpose for fifty days and no chapter of the ten entered a line in that book. The chalk is in his own inside breast pocket and has not been out since the forty-third day of the Short Month, which is day two hundred and ninety-eight, printed at full length in Chapters 0413, 0416, 0417 and 0418. The fence is sixteen willow posts and eleven withies and the count is printed twice, in Chapters 0416 and 0418, in the same form both times. The man of about fifty-seven is in four of the ten and is asked nothing in any of them and does not touch the rope, and the rope is still over the back of his chair. The shut road is out of this city. Adrian Vale is in Chapter 0414 and in no other of the ten. The woman of about fifty-four is on a page once, in Chapter 0420, and is in no chapter of this volume after it.

2. **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE WHO TRADES ON THE OPEN HAND BOARD IS IN CHAPTER 0412, AND THE BLOCK ABOVE THIS ONE SAYS HE IS NOT IN THIS BATCH, AND THAT SENTENCE IS WRONG.** He is in Chapter 0412 three times and in no other of the ten, and **the error was in the state layer and not on a page: he came down onto the boards at about the eighth hour with his own board under his arm, stood at the bottom of them for the whole of the morning, and put one length off the board he had come down with, and the man of about thirty-eight cut it without being asked how long and it came off about a forearm long, and he went up off the wharf with two boards under his arm instead of one.** `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0002.md` §7 published that he is not in any of these ten chapters, is corrected in place, and the same error is corrected in `state/character-state.md`'s own newest block. **A next batch that inherited the wrong sentence would have written a man out of a chapter he is in, and a cast file that loses a man is a cast file that cannot be checked against a page.** He was right in Chapter 0406 and nobody thanked him there and nobody thanks him anywhere.

3. **THE SECOND THING THAT CHANGED AND THAT A LATER BATCH INHERITS: the man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf has now given away two things in one volume, and the two are not joined on any page.** The barrow went down the four-hundred-mile road on day 391, a Sunday, in about a quarter of an hour, and he said so out loud on day 412 in Chapter 0412 and said he did not say why and is not going to. **The length of board came off a board at the bottom end of the same wharf on the same day and went up the same wharf with the man who brought it, and no chapter says the two are the same kind of thing and no chapter says the two are the same decision twice.** It is a length of wood and it is not a page and it is on nobody's list. The strip of clear board lying at the bottom of the same stack is a different piece of wood, did not move, and is not joined to it. **He is thanked in none of the three chapters he is in — 0412, 0414 and, in this volume, nowhere else — and he is not asked anything in any of them.**

# VOLUME 09, BATCH 0003 — days 421 to 430, and this block wins over every block above it

**Ten chapters, ten days, no double day, the chapter number is the day number, and the run was rebuilt from day 1 being a Tuesday and from nothing else before any prose was written.** 421 Tuesday, 422 Wednesday, 423 Thursday, 424 Friday, 425 Saturday, 426 Sunday, 427 Monday, 428 Tuesday, 429 Wednesday, 430 Thursday. The Bare Month days are 106 to 115, the Nth being day less 315, and the hundred and sixth to the hundred and fifteenth are the only Bare-Month days any of the ten prints. **Ten of ten agree with the prompt's table and the prompt said in advance that if the two disagreed the arithmetic was right, and the arithmetic was checked first.**

**WHAT IS ON THE TABLE AND WHAT IS IN IT, after these ten days.** One sheet, a heading and four places ruled under the heading, printed in the order place, line, person, day, with one crease pressed with a bone down the third of them. **The third of the four places was decided empty on day 425, out loud, in a room over a market, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, by the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register, with the cost named out loud before the decision and no vote and nobody asking for one.** Nothing was written on it on any of the ten days and no hand filled any of the four places, and the crease is still the only thing on that paper anybody in this city has ever touched, and the four places were all empty when the page came up the road and all four were empty when Chapter 0430 ended. **In Chapter 0422 the sheet was carried out onto the step at the back in the woman's own two hands, still folded the way it came, for about an hour, and put back on the same place on the table in the afternoon, and a woman in the market asked what the third of the four places was for and was told, out loud, that it is a person and that nobody knew what it was for.**

**THE REGISTER, THE SLATES AND WHAT WAS ENTERED.** One register with three columns, the third empty on purpose and still empty on purpose after these ten days. One second slate on the shelf behind the table, named in two of the ten chapters, carrying a date and nothing under it in both, not picked up in either, not turned over in either, not written on in either. **No line was entered in that book in any of the ten days. The two lines this volume enters in that book are a later chapter's and both stand as the plan has them: one entered on day 391 with a day on it and nothing under the day, and one to be entered on day 443 with a day on it and nothing under the day, the day being 438.** The register-keeper's third column and the sheet's third place are two different things and no chapter of these ten says they are the same thing and no chapter of these ten joins them.

**WHAT WAS DRAWN AND WHAT WAS NOT.** **Nothing was drawn on any of the ten days.** The four lines are drawn on day 432 by four people, and one of the four reads his out loud because it is in front of him and does not say it is his, and nobody asks him, and no chapter of this batch drew one and no chapter of this batch warmed that day up.

**THE OBJECT THAT CAME ONTO A PAGE IN THIS BATCH AND ONTO NO PAGE BEFORE IT.** One piece of bark off a stall frame at the market end of a market at the bottom of this city, carried up nine steps in a man's hand on the morning of Chapter 0422 and put on a table in a room and left there. It is wood and it is not a page and it is not a form and it is on nobody's list, and the man who carried it up had come to say where he had been standing. **It is published in the batch record §4 and it is the only object this batch invented.**

**THE FIGURE THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT CARRIES, AND THE FIGURES THIS BATCH DID NOT PRINT.** He is in Chapter 0427 and in no other of the ten, and he put his tally-board on a table face up for the first time in this flood, and he said out loud that the figure he sends is not the one he has, and **he printed neither figure, and the difference between them is stated with no number attached to either. The figure is on a page in Chapter 0414 and the other one is on no page and is printed nowhere in this volume.** The eighteen round figures `bible/power-system.md` §51 item 4 names for days 401 to 450 stand at zero across these ten files, and the one that falls inside this window, on day 429, is the wind having been at that wall exactly two hundred days measured from day 229, and it is not printed and no chapter of these ten mentions that wind or that wall. The two elapsed counts for the three on the four-hundred-mile road are printed nowhere: Chapter 0429 names the three hundred and ninety-first day in the sentence that says they went and prints no count of how far they have got. **The count of this city's silences is derived and printed in no chapter, and the corrected figures are 320 at day 421, 319 at day 420 and 329 at day 430, and the block above this one publishes 320 at day 411 and 329 at day 420, which are each ten high; the correction is published in the batch record §3 item 9 and in this block and nothing was repaired.**

**THE FENCE, THE SHUT ROAD AND THE MARK, in the one chapter of the ten that goes near any of them.** A shut road out of this city has a fence along the top of that road. Sixteen willow posts and eleven withies bound along it with the same cord, and one withie came off in the wet on day 423 and was put back on with a new length of cord off a coil that has been used. **The count is printed once in this batch and it is the count that holds, and no chapter of these ten prints a seventeenth post or a twelfth withy.** The shut road is out of this city, the light comes up out of the low ground and stops at that fence and does not come over it, and the lane at the foot of that road is not in this city. **The mark in a field is not named, not measured, not dug, not counted, not probed and not resolved in any of the ten, no chapter goes and looks at it, and the word *field* stands at zero across the ten files.** The man of about fifty-seven was on the low side of that fence and was not asked anything, was not told anything, did not move it, and did not touch the length of new rope, **and the length of new rope is in a room in this city in every chapter of the ten that is in a room and is not named in the one chapter that is on the road, and Chapter 0423 names the coil in the handcart and the length of new rope in adjacent sentences and joins them in nothing.**

**THE THIRD PARTY ON THE ROAD, AND WHAT WAS AND WAS NOT DONE WITH IT.** Three people went down the road that goes out of this city on the three hundred and ninety-first day of this flood, one each with a barrow. They are on that road on every one of the fifty days of this volume. **No chapter of these ten goes to them, asks about them, catches up with them or brings anybody news of them, and the town four hundred miles inland is not entered, described from inside, or named as a thing anybody going down that road is going to see.** A party of four is four and the fourth is not on that road and **the days since she said no are not counted at any value in any of the ten and a search for *days since*, *weeks since* and *months since* returns zero.**

**WHAT WAS ASKED AND BY WHOM, across the ten days, because this volume's whole question is who is asked and the answer is a short list.** The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register asked a woman of about fifty-four where her line is on day 420, in the batch before this one, and is thanked by nobody. **On day 424 she asked the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall what he sells and what he does with the rest of his day, and both questions were hers and neither was about the page. On day 428 she asked him what a line is, in the local language, in daylight, with a date in the door, in front of about nine people, once, and he said five words and nobody thanked him and nobody improved on them.** The man of about fifty-seven was in five of the ten and **was asked nothing in any of them and nobody in any of them looked at him while the reason for that was being said out loud.** The man of about thirty-nine was asked one fair practical question on day 425 at the point where a room ran out of forms and said nothing at all and was not asked it again. **The man of about fifty-two, who said one of the four lines is going to be hers, is in none of these ten and was not asked about anything. The woman of about fifty-four is in none of these ten and will never be asked about anything, and her line is the one that comes back moved on day 440 and no chapter of this volume will ever say so.**

**WHAT THE NEXT BATCH INHERITS AND WHAT IT MUST NOT UNDO.** The third place is decided empty and the third column is empty and nothing was written in either. The four lines are not drawn. The sheet goes out on day 438 with four lines on it and nothing in the third place and comes back on day 440 with one of them moved, and no chapter traces the move. **What the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall said on day 428 is on that page and in no file in this repository and may not be printed in a state file, a batch record, a bible section, an outline, a prompt or a chapter, and the next prompt must not carry it and this paragraph does not.** The volume's one rule is fixed to day 438 and is a later batch's page and these ten chapters neither wrote it nor anticipated it and did not echo it.

**THE PANEL, ONCE, ON DAY 438, AND NOWHERE ELSE.** A page in a room in daylight with a date in chalk on its door carries this volume's one panel, set as its own short block with its own line breaks and no emphasis marks, and it is a rule and not an answer, and it is refused out loud in about four seconds by the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall, and the cost of the refusal is his own line on a wall nine miles up that road, and nobody improved on what he said and nobody thanked him. **Its text is a plan's copy of a rule and not the page, and it is not printed in this block, is not printed in the batch record, is not printed in `bible/power-system.md`, and is not printed in the next prompt, and the word *claim* is inside it and is the only occurrence of that word in fifty chapters of this volume. **THE REGISTER, AND WHAT IS NOW IN IT, AND WHAT IS NOT.** The register on its shelf behind the table in the room over a market has three columns. **The second line is in it. A day is in the first column, nothing is under the day, and nothing is in the third column. The day on that line is the day the sheet went out of this city, which is the hundred and twenty-third day of the Bare Month and a Friday, and the day the line was entered is the hundred and twenty-eighth day of the Bare Month and a Wednesday, and both days are named in one sentence in one mouth in Chapter 0443, and the five days between them are named in that same sentence counted back from the morning of the entering and not forward.** It is the third time anything has gone into that book since the water came and the second of the three with nothing under the day. **Nobody may put a name under either day, no chapter of these ten filled the third column, and the only things entered in that register in this volume are the line of day 391 and the line of day 443.** The second slate is on the shelf behind the table with a day on it and nothing under the date, **it is named in seven of these ten chapters and in all seven it was not picked up and was not turned over and was not written on and no hand in that room went near the shelf.** The four days of notice is not available in this volume and there is no eighth, and **the marker for a notice is at zero across these ten files on both case readings, and no room in any of them was summoned, and Chapter 0450 opens by saying in its own words that nobody in that room was sent for and nobody was waiting to be sent for.** The cost that is lost with the notice is on a page once in this volume, in Chapter 0401, in plain narration and in no mouth, **the word *cooling* stands at zero across these ten files and this is the fifth batch of the volume to keep it there.**

**THE PANEL, ONCE, ON DAY 438, AND NOWHERE ELSE, INCLUDING IN THESE TEN.** **This batch wrote no panel, and the wall said nothing on all ten of these days, and the count of this city's silences rises by one for each of them, which is why day 438 and day 437 carry the same figure and why no figure for this stretch is printed in any chapter of it.**

**THE QUESTION, ASKED ONCE, ON DAY 445, AND NOT ANSWED.** A woman of about sixty-nine came up nine steps with a tin in both hands and asked, to the room and not to anybody, what a line is for on a page when the only thing anybody will use it for is to move it. **Nobody in that room answered it, about four of the nine had an answer and about four said nothing, no chapter of the ten puts an answer in anybody's mouth, and the two questions before it on day 346 and day 396 are on pages and are not answered in these ten either, and no chapter of these ten says that the three rhyme or improves the rhyme or says that the rhyme is the point.** A later volume may ask it again; a later volume may not answer it.

**THE VOLUME'S LAST IMAGE IS AN IMAGE.** On day 450, a Wednesday, the hundred and thirty-fifth day of the Bare Month, the room over a market with a date in chalk on the door above the table, about nine people, a sheet with four places under a heading of which the third is empty, and a road out of this city with three barrows on it. **No figure is printed for the three, no count of how far they are is printed, the hundred and thirty-fifth day of that month is written as a day like any other and nothing is said about it, and the Bare Month does not end inside this volume and no chapter of these ten ends it, converts an ordinal into a span, or prints a word about what the month does at its end.**

**WHAT A LATER PHASE INHERITS AND WHAT IT MUST NOT UNDO.** The third of the four places is decided empty and the third column of the register is empty and nothing was written in either. The four marks are drawn and one of them came back moved and nothing in this volume traces the move. The rule has been said once and refused once and is not going to be said again in this flood, in a mouth or out of one. **What the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall said on day 428 is on that page and in no file in this repository and may not be printed in a state file, a batch record, a bible section, an outline, a prompt or a chapter, and this block does not print it; the same holds for the seven words spoken in Chapter 0357, the six words spoken in Chapter 0393, the six words at `bible/power-system.md:1686`, and the volume's one rule, whose page is Chapter 0438.**

**AND THE THING NO FIGURE IN THIS BLOCK CAN CERTIFY.** A reader who finishes Chapter 0450 knows that a page went out of a city with four marks on it and no person on any of them, that one of the four came back in a place it had not gone out in, that ten days went by afterwards without a sentence about it, that a register took the day down and nothing under it, and that a road out of that city has three barrows on it that nobody in that city can be shown one person on. **There is not going to be an answer to any of it in this volume, and the state layer is not going to give one, and no prompt written by this batch gives one, because this batch wrote no next phase.**


## Volume 09, Batch 0005 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS — and this block wins over every block above it

Both wrong figures the block above names are still wrong and the corrected ones are still the corrected ones. THIS BLOCK WINS OVER EVERY BLOCK ABOVE IT IN THIS FILE.**

**2. THE COUNTS THAT HOLD, ASSERTED AGAINST THE STRINGS ON THE REPAIRED FILES, and every one of them reproduces.** The fence is **sixteen willow posts and eleven withies bound with the same cord**, once, in `chapter-0442.md`. The cart-track is **about nine yards** and no chapter prints a different length. The register is **one book with three columns, the third empty on purpose**; **one second slate** on the shelf behind it, with a date on it and nothing under the date, named in seven of the ten and turned over, picked up or written on in none of them. **The one line entered in that register in this volume is the second one, entered on day 443 with the hundred and twenty-third day of the Bare Month, a Friday, in the first column, five days counted back from that Wednesday and not counted forward, and nothing under it and no name in the third.** The sheet is **one sheet, a heading and four places, one fold down the third, the third empty on all fifty days**, and the third is still empty in every one of the ten. **Sixteen posts and eleven withies did not change; three barrows are on a road out of this city and nobody in this city can be shown one person on it; four hundred miles is a distance and not a place anybody went to.**

**3. THE FIGURES THAT MUST NOT BE PRINTED ARE AT ZERO ON THE REPAIRED FILES, all fourteen read word-bounded.** The count of this city's silences, the elapsed counts for the three on the road, the flats figure of about four hundred and forty, *days since*, *weeks since*, *months since*, *about four seconds*, *cooling*, *holder*, *quest*, *boundary*, *claim*, *passage*, *privilege* and *blue thread*. **The count of this city's silences across this batch's ten days is 339 to 348 on the corrected chain whose pair at days 411 and 420 is 310 and 319, and it is still printed in no chapter and in no mouth, and `bible/power-system.md` §51 item 9's own figure of three hundred and forty-nine at day 450 is still wrong by one against its own stated rule and was still not amended, because this pass may not amend §51.**

**4. THE PROHIBITIONS OF §16, RE-ASSERTED BECAUSE A REPAIR PASS IS THE ONE THING THAT CAN BREAK THEM BY ACCIDENT.** The fourth of the four said no is not said again, argued with, apologised for, softened, explained or counted, and a search for *days since*, *weeks since* and *months since* across the ten returns zero. **The questions of day 346, day 396 and day 445 are not answered and are not improved on, and no chapter of the ten says that the three rhyme and no chapter says the rhyme is the point.** The three four-place forms are never joined and no chapter of the ten names the contents of any form but this sheet's. The man of about fifty-seven is not asked where the fence is, is not told, does not defeat anybody, and the length of new rope stays over the back of his chair unused in all five chapters he is in. The mark in the field is not named, not measured, not dug, not counted and not resolved, and **the one of the four marks that came back out of place is not traced, not guessed at, not reported to a room and not said to have moved by anybody: every instance of *move* across the ten is the light on the flags, the piece of chalk, the chair, or the line in the woman's own question, and every instance of *mark* is inside *market*.** The ninth line is not named and is not the same thing as the four marks. The three on the road are not followed and no one is brought back from that town. **The Bare Month does not end, is not converted into a span, is not said to be nearly over and is not said to be a month of anything.**

WHAT THE REPAIR WAS NOT ALLOWED TO DO, and did not.** **No name was given to anybody.** The descriptors are the characters' names in this manuscript and Volume 09's §7.2 and §20.8 exist because a descriptor may not be used for a new person and a returning person may keep the one he has; inventing a name in a prose pass would have merged the cast in a way no instrument in this repository can see. **No System panel was added, and there is still exactly one in the volume and it is on day 438.** **No sentence was added that reports a fact, a day, a count or an event that was not already on the page.** **Adrian Vale is in two of the ten and in no other, and in both he is in a room with a page and in both he is furniture, and the repair did not improve him, because `outline/volume-09.md` §4 withdraws the licence to make him useful in a room that is waiting for a cart and the card may not put him anywhere else.** In 0447 he still says one sentence and it is still the same five words, and the woman at the far end still says them back to him as a question and he still does not answer it.

# VOLUME 09 — CLOSE — and this block is at the foot of this file and it wins over every block above it

**A close ran on Volume 09. Volume 09 is fifty chapters on fifty days, days 401 to 450, one chapter to one day, and it is finished. The close's record is `state/volume-09-close.md` and its four decisions are in `outline/series.md` under *DECISIONS OF RECORD, VOLUME 10* and its calendar is `bible/power-system.md` §57. No day moved, no weekday moved, no Bare-Month day moved, no cast member moved, no decision of record was withdrawn, no thread was discharged, re-opened or re-dated, and `outline/ending.md` was opened once and nothing in it moved. THE PLANNED PLOT DID NOT MOVE.**

## 1. THE CALENDAR, REBUILT FROM DAY 1 AND FROM NOTHING ELSE, AND CHECKED AGAINST THREE TABLES

**Day 1 is a Tuesday. The fifty rows for days 401 to 450 were recomputed from that and from nothing else and then checked row by row against `bible/power-system.md` §51's own fifty-row table on chapter-equals-day, on the weekday and on the Bare-Month ordinal being day less 315: fifty rows, fifty agreements, ZERO MISMATCHES. The same run reproduces `outline/volume-09.md` §14.3's map at fifty of fifty, and it was validated before it was used by reproducing §33A item 4's and §33B's tables. Neither table was consulted until after the run was built.** **Day 401 is a Wednesday and the eighty-sixth day of the Bare Month, because day 400 is a Tuesday. Day 450 is a Wednesday and the hundred and thirty-fifth, because 450 − 401 = 49 = 7 × 7. Both ends are Wednesdays and no chapter may derive a middle day from either of them.** Day 425 is a Saturday and the hundred and tenth and is the nearer of the two middle days of a fifty-day range and may not be called the midpoint. Day 438 is a Friday and the hundred and twenty-third. Day 440 is a Sunday and the hundred and twenty-fifth. Day 443 is a Wednesday and the hundred and twenty-eighth. Day 445 is a Friday and the hundred and thirtieth. **The hundred and thirty-fifth is the last day of that month any chapter of this volume printed, no chapter printed a day after it, and no chapter of the fifty attached a meaning to the hundredth, the hundred and tenth, the hundred and twenty-fifth or the hundred and thirty-fifth, and this block attaches none.**

**AND THE BARE MONTH, WHICH STILL HAS NO STATED LENGTH AND DID NOT END IN THIS VOLUME AND THIS BLOCK DOES NOT GIVE IT ONE.** It was registered with no length stated by a notary of fifty who named it on a page in Chapter 0316 and said why, and the reason is hers. **No chapter of the fifty printed a day after the hundred and thirty-fifth, converted a count into a span, said the month was nearly over, said what it is a month of, or said what it does at its end. The words *length*, *long* and *short* applied to the month are at zero across the fifty. NO CHAPTER OF VOLUME 10 MAY USE AN ORDINAL FOR A MONTH, and `bible/power-system.md` §57 gives the Bare Month no length, no end and no seventh form, and says in a sentence that the end of it is a thing a person does in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, having said the cost out loud first and been refused.**

## 2. WHAT THE FIFTY CHAPTERS PUT ON A PAGE, gathered and checked against the files, and it is the shortest list in this repository

1. **A second sheet with a heading and four places under it — a place, a line, a day and a person — came up the four-hundred-mile road in a satchel with a flap on day 401, a Wednesday, and was read out in the local language in a room, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, in front of about nine people, on day 405, a Sunday.** It is the third four-place form in this flood and the first with a place for the person in it. The return is a district, a population, a rota and a share. The Crown's schedule is a date, a gate, a holder and the days and stands and is correct and is not opened. **The three are never joined, never conflated and never compared out loud in any of the fifty, and the order of the four places on each is the order they are printed in.**
2. **On day 425, a Saturday, a woman decided in that room to leave the third of the four places empty, and said out loud in about nine words that a page with a person on it is a page that can be used to find them and a page with a line on it and nobody on it is a page that can be moved by whoever comes next, and said that both of those are true and that she is going to leave it empty anyway.** There is no vote. Nobody filled a column. Nobody produced a document. Nobody named a place. She is thanked by nobody and told she is right by nobody and told she is wrong by nobody. **Adrian Vale is not in that chapter.**
3. **Four people drew four lines, on day 432, a Saturday, and the man of about fifty-seven read one of them out loud because it was in front of him and did not say it was his, and nobody asked him.** The third place is empty and stays empty.
4. **The sheet went out of this city on day 438, a Friday, with four lines on it and nothing in the third of its four places, and came back on day 440, a Sunday, with one of the four in a place it had not gone out in.** The wall said one thing that day and it is this volume's one panel and it is in its own block and it gives a rule and not an answer, and the wall said nothing on the other forty-nine days and there is exactly one `>` block in the fifty files. **The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall refused it out loud in about four seconds, and the cost of the refusal is his own ninth line nine miles up a road, and nobody improved on what he said and nobody thanked him and he asked in advance not to be.**
5. **The register took a second line on day 443, a Wednesday, with a day in the first column and nothing under it, and the day on the line is day 438, the morning the sheet went out, and both days are named in one sentence in one mouth, counted back and not counted forward.** She said out loud what such a book is for before the pen went near the page, and said that nothing goes in the second column and no name in the third. **It is the third time anything has gone into that register since the water came and the second with nothing under the day, and the third column stayed empty on purpose on all fifty days and nobody may put a name under either day.**
6. **A question was asked to a room on day 445, a Friday, by the woman of about sixty-nine, and it was not answered.** See §6.
7. **A man who trades on a board was wrong about four stalls in a market on day 444, a Thursday, was told so from the step by a woman who has had a stall there since before the water, did not argue and did not concede, and said out loud that he would rather have been wrong about that than right about a page.** One of the four boards is his and he said not one word about it and nobody asked him. **Nobody in that room agreed with him out loud and nobody disagreed with him out loud.**
8. **Nobody in this city knows when the cart comes round again, and a woman who keeps a public register walked a market on two feet asking four people who sell things and has stopped.** Day 441, a Monday. The next round comes on its own day and not on anybody's. **The four days of notice are spent and there is no instrument left in this flood for asking anybody anything in advance of anything, and that is why the man of about fifty-seven is not asked, and it is a consequence and not a decision anybody took about him.**
9. **A length of new rope fell off the back of a chair onto the floorboards on day 449, a Tuesday, because a woman moved the chair about a foot to let somebody past her, and the man it belongs to picked it up and put it back, and the room went on talking about something else for about as long as it takes to count nine, and nobody said one word about it and nobody has ever thanked him for anything.** It has never once been used. The coil of rope in the handcart on the shut road is a different rope and has been used, and the chapter that names both of them joins them in nothing.
10. **The last image is a road out of this city with three barrows on it, going away under a fence along the top of it and out past the last named house, and there is nobody in this city who can be shown one person on that road.** Chapter 0450, day 450, a Wednesday, and the count of days out is printed in none of the fifty.

## 3. THE OBJECT INVENTORY, CLOSED, WITH THE CHAPTERS THAT NAME EACH SET

**Every count in the second column of `outline/volume-09.md` §20.6 holds, and a review repair pass on Batch 0005 corrected six entries in its third column against the files and no count in its second column moved, and this close re-ran the second column on the files as they stand and every count in it holds.** **One sheet with four places and the third empty on all fifty days, folded twice with a bone, one fold down the third, named in forty-six of the fifty files. Seven notices and no eighth, all of them in Volumes 07 and 08, and a chapter may name the seven and may not write one. A fence of sixteen willow posts and eleven withies bound with the same cord, the last three posts in the ground since the thirty-eighth day of that month, a Thursday, named in six of the fifty with a day in the same sentence. One register with three columns and the third empty on purpose and two lines entered in it in this volume, and one second slate on the shelf behind it with a date on it and nothing under it, named in seventeen of the fifty and picked up, turned over and written on in none of them. A party of four and three who went and one who said no, and three barrows on a road. Three four-place forms, never joined. One piece of chalk in an inside breast pocket, not taken out since day 298. A date in chalk on the door in every one of the fifty chapters. One length of new rope, never used. About four hundred and forty, of which one figure is on no page and is printed nowhere in this volume. Four hundred miles, a measure of a distance and not a place anybody went to.** **A set of objects counted in two chapters is counted by no check in this repository and this volume's last image is an object.**

The two men of about thirty-eight are two people. The man of about thirty-seven in `chapters/volume-06/chapter-0293.md` is a real person on a page and is not the board-trader. The hold is on THIS PHASE'S OWN FILES AND NOT ON THE REPOSITORY: no state file, no close record, no handoff and no next-phase prompt this phase writes prints any of them, and no sentence in any of them certifies that any of them is in no file, because the ninth inherited debt is a record that asserted exactly that and was wrong, and because three of the four places that asserted it were asserting it about a string that is standing in a bible section and a plan file where anybody may read it.**

## 6. THE NEW QUESTION, AND IT IS NOT ANSWERED

**On day 445, a Friday, the hundred and thirtieth day of the Bare Month, in the room over a market, in daylight, with the day in chalk on the door, the woman of about sixty-nine asked, to the room and not to anybody: *what is a line for, on a page, when the only thing anybody will use it for is to move it.* Nobody answered it. About four of the nine people in that room had an answer and about four of them said nothing, and nobody put either group in front of the other one, and nobody thanked her, nobody told her it was a good question, and nobody in that room said one word about it again for the rest of that morning.** A page with a place in it for a person was lying on the table the whole time and nobody in that room used any of it to answer the question. **The two questions before it, of day 346 and of day 396, are not answered either, and the three rhyme on purpose — a person, then a thing, then a line, asked three times by one woman in one building about a hundred days apart, each harder, each got less — and the rhyme is not improved on and no chapter says that the three rhyme. A later volume may ask it again, in a mouth, in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door. A later volume may not answer it, and a batch may not answer it, and nothing in this volume answers it and nothing in this record does.**

**AND THE ONE THING IT MUST NOT TRACE BY ACCIDENT: the one of the four marks that came back out of place.** No chapter of Volume 09 traced it, investigated it, guessed at it or reported it to a room, and ten chapters after it came back not one of them said one word about it. **A chapter of Volume 10 may not trace it either unless tracing it is a decision to trace it, and that decision is an outline's to take and not a close's and not a batch's.**

## VOLUME 10 — OUTLINE, and this block wins over every block above it

**This is a plan's continuity layer and not a record of pages. No chapter of Volume 10 exists. Everything below is proposed and is subject to a chapter saying it in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door. The plan is `outline/volume-10.md`; the ten cards for the first batch are `outline/batches/volume-10-batch-0001.md` and that file exists; the decisions of record are the block at the foot of `outline/series.md`.**

### 1. The calendar, the form, and the two things a chapter may not do with a date

**Days 451 to 500, one chapter to one day, no double day, no chapter writing another chapter's day. The form is *the Nth day of the Bare Month*, the Nth being day less 315.** Day 451 is the hundred and thirty-sixth and a Thursday; day 500 is the hundred and eighty-fifth and a Thursday; the midpoint is day 475, a Sunday, the hundred and sixtieth, and it is the nearer of the two middle days and neither middle day may be called the midpoint. **`bible/power-system.md` §57 is the calendar and it is unamendable by any batch and by the Volume 10 outline phase, and the Volume 10 outline phase amended nothing in §51 or §57 and moved no day in either. The day map was rebuilt from day 1 being a Tuesday and from nothing else and validated against three earlier tables at zero mismatches on all one hundred and fifty rows, and then checked against §57's own fifty rows at zero mismatches on all fifty.**

**No chapter of Volume 10 may write a day after the hundred and eighty-fifth of the Bare Month, may not say the month is nearly over, may not say what it is a month of, may not convert the hundred and eighty-fifth into a span, and may not say what the month does at its end, how many days it has, or whether it is long or short. No chapter may use an ordinal for a month** — not *the second month*, not the third, not the fourth, not the fifth, not the sixth, never *of this month*, *of the month*, *of next month*, *of this new month*. **The Bare Month has no stated length, it does not end inside Volume 10, and no phase in this volume decides how long it is. The words *length*, *long* and *short* applied to the month stay at zero.**

### 2. The figures that fall inside Volume 10, derived, and the anchor for each

**Every value here is derived and is on no page and in no mouth, and a chapter that prints one names the day it is measured from in the same sentence.** The three who went down the four-hundred-mile road on day 391 are **sixty days out at day 451 and one hundred and nine at day 500**, and the anchor is day 391. The store's board has stood unwritten since day 225, which is **two hundred and twenty-six days at day 451 and two hundred and seventy-five at day 500, and the second of those is false by the end of this volume because this volume writes on that board on day 490, and a chapter on the last page that prints it prints the correction in the same sentence or prints nothing.** The sheet that went out on day 438 came back on day 440 and the moved mark on it is not traced. **The count of this city's silences is 348 at day 450, and Volume 10's figure at day 500 is 398, and both are for this state layer and no chapter prints either.**

### 3. The objects Volume 10 will have on a page, with the counts that hold

**The object inventory is published at `outline/volume-10.md` §20.6 before any batch counts any of it, and its third column is a plan and not a record and a batch that departs from it publishes the departure in its own record and repairs nothing.** The counts that bind: **one store's board, on the outside wall at one end of that market, unwritten since day 225, carrying the figures of day 224, and carrying this city's own figure in chalk from day 490 with the day 224 figures NOT rubbed out on any of the fifty days — and it is not blank, it is stale, and a chapter may not print it as blank.** One satchel with a flap. One sheet with a heading and **one** place under the heading, the one being a figure, read out on day 455 and never returned. One register with three columns, the third empty on purpose on all fifty days, two lines entered before this volume each with a day and nothing under the day, and **one line entered in this volume on day 493 with a day in it and a figure under the day, and the day on that line is day 490.** One second slate, never picked up, never turned over, never written on. One trough, one bucket, one rag, and **a wall above the board that is not washed on any of the fifty days and that no chapter washes.** Two kinds of chalk that are not the same chalk: a piece in an inside breast pocket, not taken out since day 298, and a box of chalk on the step. One length of new rope, never used, which falls off a chair on day 456 and is put back. Sixteen willow posts and eleven withies. Three barrows on a road nobody in this city can be shown one person on. About four hundred and forty, and the other figure the man of about thirty-eight has is on no page.

### 4. The four documents, and the rule that binds them

**Four documents, three of them with four places and one of them with one.** The return: a district, a population, a rota and a share, gone out on day 393, and it has no place for the person. The Crown's schedule: a date, a gate, a holder and the days, standing, correct, and never opened, filled in or withdrawn. The sheet that went out on day 438: a place, a line, a day and a person. **The one-place sheet: a figure, and the name of the thing the figure is about is not on the page.** **The four are never joined, never conflated and never compared out loud. The order of the four places on the first three is the order they are printed in and a chapter that reorders them has got a different document. A keeper is not a holder and the word is defined by nobody. The one-place sheet is not a shorter version of any of the other three.**

### 5. The cast, and the one decision about it that is not an estimate

**Volume 10 adds no new person and takes no descriptor for anybody.** The published pool of descriptors a new person may be drawn from **does not exist**: measured over all 450 chapter files, every one of the thirty-seven survivors of the close's subtraction is standing on a page in a finished volume, thirty-seven of thirty-seven, and the clean pool is zero. **A descriptor at zero in two consecutive volumes of this manuscript is not unused, it is between uses.** The rule that replaces it: **three occurrences or fewer across all 450 files, every one a passing mention and not a scene, and zero in each of the last three volumes' fifty.** The crowded four are not available to anybody. The man of about thirty-seven is not available to anybody. **The man of about thirty-four and the woman of about thirty-four and the man of about thirty-nine and the woman of about thirty-four are returning people and may not be given a second descriptor.**

**AND THE ONE PERSON THIS VOLUME PUTS ON A PAGE PROPERLY IS A PERSON WHO IS ALREADY ON ONE: the woman who walked a market on two feet asking four people who sell things, who has stopped, who has no age and no descriptor on any page of this manuscript and is given none here, and who is put on a page by being asked, in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, on day 453.** She asks the volume's new question on day 498 and nobody answers it. **No chapter asks her why she stopped, and no chapter gives her a number, and a card that does either has invented a person.**

**AND THE STANDING RULE THAT A DESCRIPTOR IS NOT A DECISION, restated here because this volume found the case that made it necessary: a descriptor map counts occurrences and does not decide which occurrence is the same person. The last volume published a woman of about fifty-four as its one new person, and she is standing on a page in `chapters/volume-06/chapter-0294.md` at lines 15, 25 and 33, with a sheet on a sill and two sentences in her own mouth. Two people, one descriptor, and nothing on any page has ever joined them and nothing on any page ever will. That is a Volume 09 review repair pass and not an outline's, and the rule stands and the case is now on the record.**

### 6. Where Adrian Vale is, and what a chapter may not do to him

**Fifteen chapters of fifty: 0453, 0454, 0458, 0461, 0464, 0466, 0471, 0476, 0479, 0482, 0485, 0488, 0491, 0494 and 0496, and no chapter in any other form in the other thirty-five.** **He is in no chapter in which he is reading a page.** The volume's three chapters in which a page is read out, a decision is taken or a figure goes on a board in front of about nine people are 0455, 0475 and 0490 and he is in none of them. **A card may not put him in a chapter unless the card names the thing his hands are on.** In at least four of the fifteen nobody asks him anything and he is not reading anything. **He is Stage 2 on all fifty days, performs no working, opens no threshold, the Narrow Mark is not opened, the other world is not named once, nobody offers him a workway and he asks for none, the words *passage* and *privilege* stay at zero, and he is aged nowhere.**

### 7. The notices, and the one thing nobody may write

**Zero notices in Volume 10 and there is no eighth in this flood, and the page that says so is `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0389.md:13`.** Every room in this volume is a room about nine people were already in, and the four days of cooling are gone, and the midpoint is decided faster than it should be for the third volume running, and **that consequence is the reason the man of about fifty-seven is not asked, and it is a consequence and not a decision anybody took about him and no state file may record it as a decision.**

**THE ONE RULE OF THE VOLUME IS ON DAY 490 AND IS FIXED SO THAT THE COUNT OF SILENCES IS COMPUTABLE, and the wall says nothing on the other forty-nine days.** The panel's text is at `outline/volume-10.md` §17.8 and on the page of Chapter 0490 when it is written, and **it is a rule and not an answer and it gives no permission, opens nothing, resolves nothing and is not to be said again in this flood in a mouth or out of one.**

**AND THE THINGS THAT CARRY FORWARD UNCHANGED AND AT THEIR LENGTH, none of which this block restates at less than its length and none of which is closed by this block: the six held things at the head of the close record, the three questions of day 346, 396 and 445 and the fourth of day 498, the moved mark and the hand that moved it, the woman of about fifty-four, the fourth of the four who said no and the prohibition on counting the days since, the day-154 sentence and the unpublished count of the chapters carrying it, the ninth line and whether it works, the fence and about four feet and whether it stands between the right two things, the three on the road and the town four hundred miles inland and the page a hundred and forty years old, the word on the second slate, the two women of twenty-nine, the two men of about thirty-eight, the two rolls of the same gates, the Crown's schedule, the mark in the field, the boards under the chapel, the box, the island, the girl of seventeen, the keeper of the Redroot gate, the Regent, the consent fracture and the fifth condition, and the eleven debts at `state/volume-09-close.md` §7.7.**

**AND THE THING THIS BLOCK ADDS TO THE CONTINUITY LAYER, which is a finding about instruments and not about pages and which binds every batch: every figure a batch publishes carries the reading that produced it, and every figure a batch publishes about a vocabulary, a descriptor, a phrase or a habit is published over the whole manuscript and not over a window, and every published zero is run a second time on a different reading. *Sledge* is in a list of fifty-six published zeros and it is at seven occurrences in two files, and that is the one that got through, and the reason it got through is that it is in a state layer and a check of numerals cannot see a state layer.**

**AND THE ONE THING NO FILE THIS PHASE WROTE MAY PRINT: the name of Volume 09 and the words the man of about thirty-four says in Chapter 0428; the volume's one rule said on day 438; the answer spoken in Chapter 0357; the refusal spoken in Chapter 0393, whose six words also stand at `bible/power-system.md:1684` and at `outline/volume-08.md:208`; and the day-154 sentence. NAMED WHERE EACH OF THEM IS, PRINTED NONE OF THEM, NO ABSENCE CERTIFIED. The hold is on this phase's own files and not on where a string stands.**
