**This block is at the foot and wins over every block above it. It repairs three things that were internally contradictory and it decides nothing.**

1. **ADRIAN VALE'S PLACEMENT IN 0453 AND 0454, WHICH WAS THE ONE FINDING THAT COULD HAVE COST A CHAPTER.** The plan said he was not in 0453 in one sentence and said he was in it in five places including the day map's own column. **He is in both 0453 and 0454, and he is in 0453 on a barrow of stall boards and in 0454 on the satchel going round the room, and he does not speak in either and is spoken to by nobody in either, and the woman who walked a market is in 0454 too.** A writer obeying the card file's cast lists would previously have left him out of both, and would then have contradicted the prompt on the same desk. **This does not move him into any other chapter: the fifteen are still 0453, 0454, 0458, 0461, 0464, 0466, 0471, 0476, 0479, 0482, 0485, 0488, 0491, 0494 and 0496, and he is aged nowhere, and he reads no page in any of the three, and he performs no working.** The line he and the man of about thirty-four share is unchanged and still says nothing.
2. **THE BOARD'S SPAN OF SILENCE, WHICH HAD TWO ANCHORS AND WAS BEING USED AT THE WRONG ONE.** The store's board has stood unwritten since the two hundred and twenty-fifth day of this flood, and the man of about twenty-seven has washed it every morning since the seventh week of this flood. **Those are two different spans a hundred and ninety-eight days apart at day 451 and they are both true, and the plan had begun to use a third, which was two hundred and seventy-five, and that is only correct on day 500.** Every general-prose use is now the seventh-week anchor, which needs no subtraction and cannot be wrong on any day of the volume. **Day 451 is two hundred and twenty-six days from day 225 if a chapter wants the figure and names day 225 in the same sentence; two hundred and seventy-five remains exactly one thing in this phase, the day-500 trap at §14.6, and it is a multiple of twenty-five and not of fifty and it is false by the end of the volume because the volume writes on that board on day 490.**
3. **THE SATCHEL'S ARRIVAL, WHICH WAS NAMED ON THE WRONG DAY.** It comes up the road on day 452, is put on a table unopened, goes round a room in three hands on day 454 and comes back to the table in the fourth, and goes round a market in four hands on day 460. **It is not on the day-451 page and a chapter that has it there is writing another chapter's day.**

**AND WHAT DID NOT MOVE, re-run on the files as they stand after the repair: the day run, the counts, the object inventory, the cast placements and the prohibitions all re-assert, and the day map is at zero mismatches on all fifty rows and the Adrian column and the fifteen are the same fifteen chapters.**

**THE ONE-PLACE SHEET. One sheet, a heading, and one place ruled under the heading, and the one place is for a figure.** Read out on day 455 in the room over a market by the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register, who said out loud what it is for before anybody asked. It is named in Chapters 0461, 0463, 0467 and 0470 of this batch. **It has never been returned, never been withdrawn, and is not going back up the road; in Chapter 0470 it goes off the table in one pair of hands at the eleventh hour and down to that market and is not opened on the way. No chapter of these ten prints a second place under that heading and nothing under it but the figure, and the name of the thing the figure is about is on no page of these ten.** The figure one thousand three hundred and eight, read out in Chapter 0455, is at **zero across the ten files of this batch**, deliberately: a chapter that reprinted it beside a number of marks on a slate would have put two figures side by side on a page, and the volume's whole finding is that nobody can do that.

**THE STORE'S BOARD. One board**, on the outside wall at one end of that market, **unwritten since the two hundred and twenty-fifth day of this flood, carrying the figures of the two hundred and twenty-fourth day of this flood, and carrying nothing else on any of the ten days of this batch.** It is named in Chapters 0462, 0464 and 0470 and **not in Chapter 0461**, where the wharf boards are a set of planks and the store's board is not on the page. It is washed every morning by the man of about twenty-seven and **the wall above it that he does not wash stands over the top of it on all ten days and no chapter of these ten washed it.** **No figure went on that board in these ten days and the volume's figure goes on it on day 490.**

**THE REGISTER AND THE SLATES. One register** with three columns, **the third column empty on purpose on all ten days and the register not opened on any of them**; **one second slate** with a date on it and nothing under the date, **not picked up, not turned over and not written on, and no hand but the register-keeper's near that shelf on any of the ten days.** The second slate is named in Chapters 0462, 0463, 0466, 0467 and 0468. Two lines were in that book before this volume and are still there, each with a day in it and nothing under the day, and neither was read out. **The register's third line is entered on day 493 and not before, and nothing in these ten chapters is a line entered in it.**

**THE NOTICES. Seven, all in earlier volumes, and there is no eighth, and the page that says so is `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0389.md:13`.** The word is at **two case-insensitively and one case-sensitively across the ten files of this batch**, both in Chapter 0467, and both are the man of about thirty-one naming the five he carried in a coat once. **No chapter of these ten writes one, and the sentence about there being no eighth was said in Chapter 0457 and is not restated here.**

**THE THREE FOUR-PLACE FORMS. The return, the Crown's schedule and the sheet that went out on day 438.** **None of the three is named in any of the ten chapters of this batch, and none is laid beside the one-place sheet, and the word *holder* is at zero.**

### 3. The figures that are now on a page, and the one that is not and never will be

**ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED AND FORTY is the figure a man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf sends up a road four times a year, and it is on a page in Chapters 0457, 0461 and 0466 of this manuscript and in no mouth in this batch as a count of anybody.** In Chapter 0466 a count made on foot across eleven miles of flats comes to about four hundred and forty marks on a slate, and **the chapter does not say whether that is the same figure, and says in a plain sentence that nobody in this city has said out loud whether they are the same and nobody is going to be in a position to say it this side of a long while.** **THE FIGURE THAT MAN HAS IS ON NO PAGE AND IS PRINTED NOWHERE IN THIS VOLUME AND APPEARS IN NO MOUTH, and this batch printed it zero times.**

**THE COUNT OF THIS CITY'S OWN FIGURE is not on a page in these ten chapters and no chapter of them prints it.** The register's second column is empty on all ten days and holds a figure for the first time in this flood on day 493, and that figure is this city's own and not the office's.

**AND THE COUNT OF THIS CITY'S SILENCES, derived, on no page, in no mouth, and printed in no chapter, is unchanged by these ten days at 348 at day 450 and is not 358 at day 460 and is not 368 at day 470.** The wall said nothing on all ten days of this batch and the addition rule is one for every day the wall says nothing, so the day-470 figure is **368** and no writer may compute it any other way. **Volume 10's one panel is on day 490 and takes that day out, and the figure at day 500 remains 398 on the rule published at `outline/volume-10.md` §14.5 and `bible/power-system.md` §57 item 9. No chapter of these ten prints any of these four numbers and this record computes them for the state layer and not for a page.**

### 4. What is new on a page and is not in any file a plan can hold

**THE WORD.** In Chapter 0463 the woman of about fifty-two says, in her own mouth, in the room over a market, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, in front of about nine people, that the office's figure is a count of places and this city's figure is a count of people, and that the whole of the difference between them is a unit, and that a unit is a decision somebody has made about what a thing is. **The word stands at two across the ten files of this batch and both are in that mouth, which is the only use the volume's plan permits: in a mouth naming the difference, never in narration, and never in the name the volume speaks on day 486.** The rest of the volume's twenty-word zero column is at **zero** across the ten, and *tracing* and its family are at zero, and *sledge* is at zero on both readings.

**THE COUNT, AND WHAT IT IS A COUNT OF, AND WHOSE SLATE IT WAS MADE ON.** In Chapter 0466 a man puts one stroke on a slate for every place there is anybody at, across eleven miles of flats, in weather, from the sixth hour to the fourth, and comes back with about four hundred and forty on it. **THE SLATE IS NOT HIS. It is the register-keeper's own working slate, taken off the bench in the room over a market in the sixth hour without asking and put back on that same bench at the fourth hour with the marks on it and the cold still on his hands, and the chapter says so at `chapter-0466.md:3` and again at `chapter-0466.md:31`; the first edition of Batch 0002's record called the slate his own, that was wrong, and it is corrected here and in the batch record. It is NOT the second slate on the shelf behind that bench, and the chapter keeps the two apart in one sentence, and the second slate is not picked up, not turned over and not written on and no hand but the register-keeper's goes near that shelf on day 466. A later batch that wants a slate for a second count takes this one off the same bench and it is the same bench, and a batch that conflates the two has broken prohibition 2 by accident.** **He says out loud, twice, to the flats, that there are places with nobody in them and places with three people in them and he has counted the same thing in both and they are the same mark. The chapter does not say he was right, and nobody asks him anything, and nobody thanks him, and the slate went on a bench in the room over a market and there is no form in four hundred miles on which anybody could write down that they had failed to.** It is the first of three mornings and the other two are not named.

**THE QUESTION AND THE NO-ANSWER.** In Chapter 0470 a man asks a room out loud to put a figure on the store's board in chalk in the open. The register-keeper answers a different question correctly in one sentence: **that there is no instrument in four hundred miles for asking a room to be asked anything in advance of anything.** Nobody answers his question, the man of about thirty-nine picks his own board up off the leg of the table and carries it out of that room himself, and the room empties with a question on a table and nothing at all on a wall. **Nobody answered it and nobody said out loud that nobody had, and the question is not answered in any later chapter of this batch and is not previewed.**

**AND FOUR SMALL THINGS THAT ARE NOW ON A PAGE AND ARE NOBODY'S BUSINESS.** Sixteen willow posts and eleven withies bound with the same cord, and about four feet that nobody measured, in the mouth of the man of about sixty who cut them and is not asked why. A hurdle mended on the near gate and a bar he put in nine years ago that is still the bar in that gate, and four hundred miles walked four times in nine years by a man nobody asked and who is not going after anybody. A stool carried up the stair at the back of the room over a market because there are more people in it than there are benches. And a man with a satchel who has carried four things up and down that road in three volumes and has opened none of them, and the difference he minds, which is that a thing in a flap on a table is a thing four people know about and a thing in a flap on a man's back is a thing nobody knows about.

### 5. Where Adrian Vale is, and what these ten days did to him

**He is named in THREE of the ten chapters — 0461, 0464 and 0466 — and in no chapter in any form in the other seven, and the three are the three `outline/volume-10.md` §14.3's Adrian column has him in, and the batch added no sixteenth and moved no chapter.** **He is in no chapter in which he reads a page.** His hands are on the frame of a barrow in 0461, on the board off its nails and the bucket and the water and the trough in 0464, and on **the register-keeper's own slate, off her bench, and a hazel stick off a gate, and eleven miles of wet ground** in 0466, **and the thing his hands are on is named in the card in all three, and in 0466 the thing is borrowed and returned and the reason he has it is his own and the slate is not.** **0461 and 0466 are two of the four chapters in the whole volume in which nobody asks him anything, nobody declines to ask him anything, nobody is waiting for him to be useful and he is not reading anything**, and in both the chapter lets him work something out and does not say he was right. In 0464 he is asked one ordinary question, which is where the trough stands, and he answers it in one sentence and nobody answers him and nobody thanks him. He performs no working, no threshold is opened, the other world is not named once, nobody offers him a workway and he asks for none, and he is aged nowhere in any of the ten. **The words *passage* and *privilege* are at ZERO across all ten files on both readings.**

### 6. The prohibitions, re-asserted after ten days

**All fifteen of the plan's prohibitions held, and the full table with the instrument beside each figure is §6 of the batch record.** **Zero elapsed counts of days of any kind stand anywhere in the ten files.** The moved mark is not traced and the day 440 is not on a page of these ten. The word on the second slate is not printed. The fourth of the four and the days since she said no are not on a page of these ten. The three questions are not answered and the fourth is not asked and is not previewed. The day-154 sentence is not said. The man of about fifty-seven is not asked where the fence is, is not told, is not defeated, and his rope is not used, not cut, not taken up and does not fall. No two of the four documents are joined or compared out loud. The town four hundred miles inland is not entered and the three on the road are named nowhere. **The woman who walked a market on two feet is at zero in these ten chapters and is given no number, and the reason is published in the batch record and is an instrument and not a decision about her.** No new descriptor was taken. There is no eighth notice. No room count is printed and *dozen* is at zero. The two women of twenty-nine are never in one room and Tamsin Quill is not in these ten chapters at all, so the consent fracture is neither mended nor visible and the fifth condition is not given. The two men of about thirty-eight are two people and only one of them is in these ten. **Nobody gets stronger.**

**AND THE THINGS THAT CARRY FORWARD UNCHANGED AND AT THEIR LENGTH, none of which this block restates at less than its length and none of which is closed by this block: the six held things at the head of the close record, the three questions and the fourth, the moved mark and the hand that moved it, the woman of about fifty-four and the unpaid attribution, the fourth of the four and the prohibition on counting the days since, the day-154 sentence and the unpublished count of the chapters carrying it, the ninth line and whether it works, the fence and about four feet and whether it stands between the right two things, the three on the road and the town four hundred miles inland and the page a hundred and forty years old, the word on the second slate, the two women of twenty-nine, the two men of about thirty-eight, the two rolls of the same gates, the Crown's schedule, the mark in the field, the boards under the chapel, the box, the island, the girl of seventeen, the keeper of the Redroot gate, the Regent, the consent fracture and the fifth condition, and the eleven debts at `state/volume-09-close.md` §7.7.**

**AND THE ONE THING NO FILE THIS BATCH WROTE MAY PRINT: the name of Volume 09 and the words the man of about thirty-four says in Chapter 0428; the volume's one rule, said on day 438; the answer spoken in Chapter 0357; the refusal spoken in Chapter 0393, whose six words also stand at `bible/power-system.md:1684` and at `outline/volume-08.md:208`; and the day-154 sentence. NAMED WHERE EACH OF THEM IS, PRINTED NONE OF THEM, NO ABSENCE CERTIFIED. THE HOLD IS ON THIS BATCH'S OWN FILES AND NOT ON WHERE A STRING STANDS: a plan is not a page and a permanent witness section is not a page either.**

## VOLUME 10, BATCH 0002 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS, and this block is at the foot of this file and it wins over every block above it

**A reviewer returned six blocking and three moderate findings on Chapters 0461 to 0470. Four were fixed on the page, one in six files, two were carried to the plan, and one is published with the reason it was not repaired. The full record is §12 of `state/batch-summaries/volume-10-batch-0002.md`. NO PLOT CHANGED, NO DAY MOVED, NOTHING RESOLVED, NO DEBT TAKEN.**

### 1. THE THREE FACTS A LATER WRITER MUST CARRY THAT THIS PASS ESTABLISHED OR CORRECTED

**THE SLATE ON THE FLATS IS THE REGISTER-KEEPER'S, NOT ADRIAN'S. CORRECTED IN THIS FILE ABOVE AND THE CORRECTION IS THE ONE THAT BINDS.** `chapter-0466.md:3` and `chapter-0466.md:31` both say the slate was on a bench in the room over a market and went back onto that same bench at the fourth hour. **It is taken in the sixth hour without asking and returned before the room fills, it is a third object and NOT the second slate on the shelf behind that bench, and no hand but the register-keeper's went near that shelf on day 466.** A batch that wants a slate for a second count takes this one off the same bench; a batch that conflates it with the second slate has broken prohibition 2 by accident. **The reason the flag under the far end of the trough in `chapter-0464.md` is past tense is now *had been out of true* and no generalising present is claimed for it anywhere.**

**AND THE SECOND SENTENCE, WHICH WAS A LEAK AND IS GONE: `chapter-0462.md` no longer says *for two volumes of a book*.** A chapter in this manuscript does not name the thing that contains it, in a sentence, in the middle, and the sentence that forbade a chapter opening on a ledger did not reach a leak in the middle of one. The rule is published in the batch record at §12.1 item 2.

### 2. WHAT DID NOT MOVE, and this is the half that matters

**THE OBJECT INVENTORY IS UNCHANGED AND EVERY COUNT IN THE SECOND COLUMN OF `outline/volume-10.md` §20.6 STILL HOLDS ON ALL TEN FILES.** One store's board, unwritten, carrying the figures of the two hundred and twenty-fourth day and nothing else. One one-place sheet. One register, unopened, third column empty. One second slate, never picked up, never turned over, never written on. One trough, one bucket, one rag, and a wall above the board that is not washed and is not washed by this pass. Seven notices and no eighth. Sixteen posts and eleven withies. A party of four, three of whom went. Three barrows. Four forms, never joined. Two kinds of chalk. A date in chalk on the door. One length of new rope, still on the back of his chair. About four hundred and forty, and the other figure still on no page. Four hundred miles, a distance and not a place. **THE THIRD COLUMN OF §20.6 IS UNTOUCHED, INCLUDING ITS SIX DEPARTURES IN AND THREE OUT, WHICH ARE A PLAN AND A RECORD RESPECTIVELY AND WHICH NOBODY REPAIRS.**

**THE CAST PLACEMENTS ARE UNCHANGED: Adrian Vale in 0461, 0464 and 0466 and in no other of the ten, and in no chapter in which he reads a page. The seven incidental figures across the ten still carry no descriptor and none was attached to anybody. The woman who walked a market is still at zero in these ten chapters. The two men of about thirty-eight are still two people.**

**THE PROHIBITIONS ALL STILL HOLD AND NONE WAS WEAKENED: the word on the second slate is at zero, no figure of days since the fourth of the four said no stands anywhere, no elapsed count of days of any kind stands anywhere, the day 440 and its sheets are named nowhere, the three questions are unanswered and the fourth is unasked, the man of about fifty-seven is not asked and not told and his rope is not used and does not fall, the town four hundred miles inland is not entered, the page a hundred and forty years old is not read, the three on the road are not named, no room count is printed for anybody and *dozen* is at zero, no month ordinal is used, no panel is written, the wall said nothing on all ten days, and the day 440 mark is not traced by anybody in any mouth.**

### 3. THE TWO THINGS THE PLAN NOW OWES THE NEXT THIRTY CHAPTERS, carried from `outline/volume-10.md`

**§17.19: "nobody asks him anything" is the pressure and not the reward.** In each of the fifteen chapters he is in he must be the cause of at least one thing that happens to somebody else, and that consequence may not be a decision, a figure, a document, a notice or the panel. **§17.18: every batch of ten carries at least one thing that is different at the end of it from the start, and no more than two of a batch's ten chapters may close on a movement that states nothing changed.** Neither rule touches a day, a figure, an object or a fact already on a page, and both bind Chapter 0471 onward and not Chapter 0470 backward.

### 4. THE ONE DEBT THIS PASS ADDED AND DID NOT PAY, and it is the same debt the review named

**A card file for Volume 10's Batch 0002, 0003, 0004 and 0005 does not exist as an outline-layer file, and `outline/batches/volume-10-batch-0002.md` exists only because a batch wrote it, which a batch is not permitted to do.** The collision was published three times before this pass and routed now, and the routing decision is `outline/volume-10.md` §21, and **the debt is owed by the next outline phase and by no writer and by no repair pass.** Carried, unpaid, and named here so that a later phase does not discover it as a surprise and take it as permission.
